# Changelog

The full change log, newest first, moved verbatim from the README's "Recent changes" section (only relative link paths were adjusted for this folder); the [README](../README.md#recent-changes) keeps one-line summaries of the newest entries.

- Print-dialog diagnostic flake and sanitizer P3 (SM6; branch `sm6/flake-and-diag` off `855a618`; scripts and tests only). CI run 40656514501 failed `installed-print-dialog-workflow.test.ts` "selects one visible dialog-class HWND from the hosted 11-owned 5-candidate topology" on the childless save-dialog receipt check, and passed on rerun. Root cause: `Wait-NewProcessNativeWindowSurface` polls until its deadline; when a poll iteration straddles the deadline, the deadline `catch` rebuilt the receipt from that interrupted iteration (no top-level records, stage `not-started`) and overwrote the last completed receipt, so the diagnostic lost `native-enumeration-complete`. It depends on where the deadline lands, so it shows up on a loaded runner. Fix: new `Get-NativeWindowReceiptRank`; the deadline catch only replaces the receipt when the new one is at least as informative (late-root `native-enumeration-started` is still kept). New deterministic regression in the same test: the provider blocks the second top-level enumeration past the deadline; it fails on the old code and passes now. Also the `WEBDRIVER_ERROR_DIAG` sanitizer: first rule is `file://[^'"<>|]*` (two-slash `file://server/share/x`), and a backslash UNC path may follow a word character (`foo\\srv\share`); `//` keeps its word-character guard and `:` guard, so http URLs and `a//b` are unchanged. Tests add both cases plus http negative controls. Print-dialog file 5/5 runs of 26/26, webdriver-error-diagnostics 5/5, the eight other files that load these scripts 127/127, with `--pool=threads --maxWorkers=1 --no-file-parallelism`.

- Copy-permission follow-ups (SM3; branch `sm3/permission-followups` on `268a994`; review P3s: SM7 two, code three; `service.rs`, `pdf_engine.rs` doc comment, `xref_resolve.rs` (test module `pub(crate)`), `xref_resolve_tests.rs`, `pdf_helper/tests.rs`; no dependency, version or workflow change). (1) `checked_text_replacement` checked only that PDFium has no security handler, while its reply carries the run's text; it now also runs `text_extraction_permission` (lopdf AND PDFium). (2) `CreateTextHighlight` read PDFium glyph geometry ungated; it is now gated as well: the start and end come from a selection that the gated `page_text_geometry` already refuses, so the gate costs nothing legitimate, and glyph boxes stored in a saved file reveal text layout. (3) Owner password: on a copy-denied file with user password "user", opening with "owner" is allowed by both readers and with "user" denied by both. With an empty user password lopdf has already decrypted and dropped `/Encrypt`, so `authenticate_owner_password` fails and lopdf keeps denying while PDFium allows; the gate denies (fails closed). The app never prompts for a password on such a file (PDFium opens it without one), so users cannot reach this. (4) The helper equivalence test now loads generated copy-denied and copy-allowed encrypted files and asserts the in-process bit (false, true) before comparing it with the helper's. (5) `extraction_bits_allow` doc comment: bits numbered from 1, bit 5 = 0x10, bit 10 = 0x200 (accessibility, what pdfium-render reads). New reverse-disagreement fixture `commented_encrypt_trailer`: the cross-reference stream's `/Encrypt` key renamed (same length) and the encrypted trailer kept only in a comment after a `startxref` past the end. PDFium sees no handler and allows; lopdf's rebuild takes the comment trailer, decrypts and denies; the gate and `checked_text_replacement` deny. Tests in `text_extraction_needs_lopdf_and_pdfium_to_agree`; filters `pdf_helper` 33 pass, `comments` 14 pass, `text_edit` 1 pass. Full `--bins`: 483 pass, 1 fail (`ocr_process::nonzero_exit_is_reported_ahead_of_a_writer_error_when_input_is_never_read`, timing under full-suite load; `ocr_process` filter alone 19 pass; file untouched), 35 ignored.

- In-app update tests: explicit 60 s per-test timeout (SM0; test only; branch `sm0/inapp-test-timeout` off `dc63d31`). CI run 40655137857 failed `does not redefine shared helper functions and fails fast when the firewall cannot enforce the cutoff` at 5.5 s against vitest's 5 s default: CI runs vitest without `--testTimeout`, and this file's tests spawn pwsh. The describe block now sets `{ timeout: 60_000 }`, matching the per-test timeouts other pwsh-heavy test files use. 21/21 pass with the CI flags.

- Text-extraction gate consults PDFium (SM3; branch `sm3/pdfium-permission` off `dc63d31`; SM7 P3 from the review of `c1e5fed`, pre-existing; `pdf_engine.rs`, `service.rs`, `pdf_helper/{mod,remote,serve,protocol,tests}.rs`, `xref_resolve_tests.rs`; no dependency, version or workflow change). The gate for text, selection, copy, current-page OCR and searchable OCR trusted only lopdf's view (`editor.rs` `source_text_extraction_permission`). lopdf can resolve a different trailer than PDFium (a `startxref` past the end makes lopdf rebuild and take the newest `trailer` with a usable `/Root`), so PDFium could decrypt an empty-user-password file whose `/P` denies copying while lopdf saw it unencrypted and allowed it. Now `service::text_extraction_permission` requires both: the session's lopdf result and PDFium's `FPDF_GetDocPermissions` bit 5 (0x10, the bit lopdf's `COPYABLE` reads; bit 5 is copy/extract in every standard handler revision, so `FPDF_GetSecurityHandlerRevision` does not change the rule; unprotected and owner-unlocked files report every bit). The bit is read once at load, no second open: `InProcessEngine` keeps a second binding to the same library (pdfium-render exposes only bit 10 for revision 3+, and refuses a binding after `Pdfium::new`), and the helper returns it in the load reply (`Loaded.text_extraction_allowed`, helper protocol version 2; a reload that changes it marks the document suspect, as `unprotected` does). Normal files are unchanged (unprotected: all bits). Test `text_extraction_needs_lopdf_and_pdfium_to_agree` (child process, generated fixtures): unprotected allows; RC4-128 empty-password files allowing or denying copy are allowed or denied by both readers; the trailer-disagreement file denying copy is allowed by lopdf (no encryption seen, rebuilt table) but denied by PDFium and therefore by the gate; the same file allowing copy stays allowed. Helper equivalence now compares the bit. Not covered: lopdf's owner-password check does not lift its restriction on an empty-user-password file (pre-existing; PDFium does), unchanged. `--bins`: 483 pass, 1 fail (`ocr_process::stdout_and_stderr_caps_are_exact_and_fast_exit_cannot_bypass_them`, timing under full-suite load; passes alone; file untouched), 35 ignored.

- Large-document OCR save in parts: review fixes (SM4; branch `sm4/large-doc-ocr-split` on `f27797b`; code review CHANGES NEEDED, SM7 PASS with P3s; `large_ocr_parts.rs`, `large_ocr_commands.rs`, `large_ocr_job.rs` tests, `main.rs` (`cancel_large_ocr` takes `keepSavedParts`), `src/bridge.ts`, `src/LargeOcrPanel.tsx`, `.test.tsx`; no dependency, version or workflow change). P2: Discard or Cancel of a job some of whose parts are already saved is refused by the backend unless the call confirms it (`keepSavedParts`); the panel asks first, naming the saved files, and the saved parts are never deleted. A paused job (and one from an earlier session) lists the parts already saved with their page ranges (`published_parts`), and Save elsewhere is hidden then (the backend already refused it). P3: crash recovery between moving a part and recording it also covers a cross-volume copy that stopped before removing its source: when both files have the recorded size and identical SHA-256 the move is counted and the source removed; a same-size file with other contents is never taken for ours. Part folders are checked with `symlink_metadata` before use (resume, roll-over, publish, crash recovery, publish-temp cleanup): a link or junction pauses the job and is never followed. Before starting a new part the free space on the app-data volume must be at least twice the part cap, else the job pauses with a message naming the free and needed MiB. `bridge.ts` spacing. Tests: crash window between move and save (moved, copied with source left, someone else's same-size file), free space below the margin pauses before a new part and resume finishes, a junction as the open part folder pauses with nothing written through it, discard refusal names the saved part, two panel tests (saved parts listed and confirmed discard; discard with nothing saved). OCR filter (`ocr`, `searchable`): 84 pass, 8 ignored; panel vitest 12 pass.

- Large-document OCR: save in parts over 1 GiB (SM4; branch `sm4/large-doc-ocr-split` off `bead614`; new `src-tauri/src/large_ocr_parts.rs`; `large_ocr_job.rs`, `large_ocr_commands.rs`, `searchable_pdf_stream.rs`; small hunks in `main.rs` (module, one command `save_large_ocr_in_parts`) and `src/bridge.ts`; `src/LargeOcrPanel.tsx`, `.module.css`, `.test.tsx`; `docs/features.md`; no dependency, version, installer or workflow change). Joshua's ruling "output over 1 GiB → warn and offer to split". Writer: the size cap is injectable (`set_size_cap`, `JobSettings.size_cap`; the app passes 1 GiB), and while pages are appended the writer keeps room for the font, page tree and xref (`tail_upper_bound`), so finalising never passes the cap; `finalize_pages(n)` finalises the first n pages of a job planned for more, keeping object numbers and listing only existing objects in two xref subsections (bytes unchanged when n is the full count). Parts: part 1 is the job folder's own partial copy, part n is `part-n/` with its own manifest, partial copy and journal; when a page does not fit, the open part is checkpointed, `parts.json` records its page count durably (temp file, fsync, rename), and the next part starts with that page, so boundaries depend only on the pages and an interrupted job gives the same parts. Nothing is published until every page is done; then the names `<name> (part i of N).pdf` next to the chosen destination are checked to be free, every unpublished part is finalised and checked from disk, and the parts are moved in order with the no-clobber publish (the commit barrier before the first move). `parts.json` records how many are published and the size of the one being moved, so a crash between a move and the save is recognised on resume; a part name taken during publishing pauses with the earlier parts kept and the rest resumable. `parts.json` is read as untrusted input (bounded, format, page counts). Save elsewhere is refused once a part is saved; a job in parts does not require the chosen name itself to be free. Panel: the projected-size warning while running offers **Save in parts** (the job then continues into the next part at the cap instead of pausing); the paused-at-cap state offers **Save in parts** and **Discard progress**; a saved set lists each part with its page range and an **Open part n** button. Tests (debug build, fake source and engine): a 60-page job with a 25,000-byte cap and one failed render gives five parts, contiguous page ranges, each under the cap and reopening in lopdf with the right page count, PDFium extracts `Page<n>` for the source page on the first and last page of each part, and the report gives source page numbers; without Save in parts the cap pauses at the page where part 1 ends and Save in parts then gives parts byte-identical to a clean run; a pause mid-part-2 plus a torn part-2 journal resumes to byte-identical parts; a part name taken before publishing publishes nothing, one taken between the check and the move keeps part 1 and resume finishes the set, with the other file untouched; a refused commit leaves every part partial again, and a damaged part 2 fails the check with no part published; a small-cap writer stops with room to finalise; invalid `parts.json` records are refused; relocation and resume rules for jobs in parts. OCR test filter (`ocr`, `searchable`): 81 pass, 8 ignored; panel vitest 10 pass. Not verified: a real copy over 1 GiB, installed builds, real scans.

- Open dialog title (SM3; branch `sm3/open-dialog-title` off `bead614`; `src-tauri/src/main.rs`, `scripts/verify-native-annotate-dialogs.ps1`, `scripts/verify-native-page-dialogs.ps1` and their vitest files; no version change). Every save and image-picker route in `main.rs` sets a dialog title, but `open_document` did not, so Windows showed its default "Open" (the 2026-10-14 native verifier runs asserted exactly that). It now sets "Open a PDF", in the same sentence style as "Choose one PNG or JPEG image" and "Restore unsaved PDF edits". Both native dialog verifiers now expect the new title, and the vitest checks tie each verifier's expectation to the `set_title` call in `open_document`. The recovery picker already had its own title and is unchanged.

- Refused-file copy permission fix (SM3; branch `sm3/xref-objstm-gap` on `c1e5fed`; `xref_resolve.rs`, `editor.rs` visibility only, one test). Code review of `c1e5fed` (P1): `refused_source_permission` allowed extraction whenever the unreconciled trailer had no `/Encrypt`, but lopdf 0.45 removes `/Encrypt` from the trailer after decrypting with the empty user password and keeps `encryption_state` instead, so search, selection and copy bypassed a `/P` without the copy bit on such a file. It now calls the same `source_text_extraction_permission` every open uses (`encryption_state` first). Test: an empty-password encrypted file refused by a later update that frees a used object stays refused for extraction when `/P` lacks the copy bit and is allowed when it has it.

- Cross-reference resolution review fixes (SM3; branch `sm3/xref-objstm-gap` on `28a65aa`; `xref_resolve.rs` and its tests, small hunks in `editor.rs`, `page_labels.rs`, `pdf_load.rs`; no version change). Code review and SM7 review of `28a65aa`. (P1) A refused file became a plain parse failure, so the text-extraction permission failed and search, selection/copy and OCR stopped too. `EditSession` now recognises the refusal and takes the permission from lopdf's unreconciled load when the newest trailer has no `/Encrypt` (an encrypted refused file still reports the permission as unverifiable, with the reason). Comments, forms, page labels, page edits and saved copies show the refusal message itself; page labels used to say only "could not be read". (P2) Cross-reference streams are charged on encoded `Length` before they are copied and on decoded output, against one 32 MiB total; repeated `/XRefStm` pointers are parsed once; sections are keyed on the corrected offset, so nearby pointers cannot re-parse one section; trailer and stream dictionaries parse in a 64 KiB window. Whether a file has object streams now comes from its raw bytes (`/ObjStm` or `/XRefStm`), so a walk failure in a file where lopdf loaded none of them is refused instead of kept. Offset-0 and direct-entry disagreements now refuse only when a reachable object is involved, and are otherwise dropped like the rest. (P3) An entry whose offset holds a different object number (`N G obj` read within 64 bytes) is refused when either number is reachable. A table lopdf rebuilt by scanning is refused when the raw bytes show object streams and kept otherwise (every reader's scan finds the same direct objects). Tests: 16 (new: a file shaped like the refused real one, which lopdf loads with no containers, refused yet still searchable, with comments and page labels giving the reason; offset holding another number; rebuilt table; 1,000 sections pointing at one `/XRefStm`; an over-budget stream refused before decoding; unterminated trailer string; nearby `/Prev`). Real work PDFs re-measured read-only (4,606 files): 10 changed (506 unreachable freed objects removed), 1 refused, reason "a used member lopdf did not take from its named container", and no other refusal from the new checks.

- Cross-reference resolution reconciled onto every lopdf load (SM3; branch `sm3/xref-objstm-gap` off `bead614`; new `src-tauri/src/xref_resolve.rs` and `xref_resolve_tests.rs`, one call in `pdf_load::load`, module registered in `main.rs`; no version change). Closes the SM3 LOW gap "an object stream can supply an object whose xref entry is free, missing or offset 0". lopdf 0.45 drops `f` rows (a number a newer revision freed is read from the older revision), reads `/XRefStm` only from the newest trailer and only with `/Prev` (never an older section's, never a same-section hybrid without `/Prev`), and adds every object-stream member that has no compressed entry, from whichever container holds it (with rayon, which of two copies wins is not fixed). `reconcile` re-walks the chain itself (tables, cross-reference streams, hybrid `/XRefStm` per section, newest first; bounded: 1,024 sections, 4,000,000 rows, 32 MiB of decoded cross-reference streams in total, dictionaries nested 32 deep, `/Prev` cycles stop) and compares it with lopdf's load. Measured on the bundled PDFium 151.0.7881 with generated files: PDFium reads a `/XRefStm` only when that section's trailer also has `/Prev`, keeps an object a newer revision freed, and treats an object-stream member with a free or missing entry as null. So: a member with a free or missing entry is removed (PDFium and the standard agree); an object the readers disagree about that nothing reachable uses is removed (a saved copy prunes it anyway); and the load is refused ("PDF readers resolve this file's cross-reference data differently ... Viewing is unaffected.") when the readers disagree about a reachable object, an object stream fills an offset-0 entry, a direct entry lopdf did not read from the same place, or the chain cannot be walked in a file with object streams. On the open path a refusal is a parse failure: viewing works and lopdf-backed features say why they are unavailable (search, selection and OCR were also lost, fixed in the review entry above). The resulting `reference_table` is the standard one, so the forms completeness check uses it. Tests: 11 in-memory (generated hybrid, incremental and malformed files; a PDFium agreement test in a child process asserts every kept load matches PDFium and every refusal is a real disagreement; bounds; repo fixtures unchanged). Read-only measurement on real work PDFs (4,606 files, 4,602 loaded by lopdf, 471 with object streams, 2 with `/XRefStm`): 10 changed (506 unreachable objects with a free entry removed, none reachable), 1 refused, reason "a used member lopdf did not take from its named container": a Word file with a later update whose object streams are listed only in the older section's `/XRefStm`, which lopdf never reads.
- WebDriver error diagnostic sanitizer hardened (SM6; `scripts/installed-app-launch.ps1`, `scripts/webdriver-error-diagnostics.test.ts`; branch `sm6/diag-sanitizer` off `b3a19be`; no version, installer or workflow change). Review of `faf122d..71dcf12` found `ConvertTo-SanitizedWebDriverError` missed `C:/x/...` paths and left the tail of a path containing a space. Drive paths (either slash style), `//server/share` and `\servershare` UNC paths and `file:///` URIs are now redacted through to the next quote, angle bracket or pipe (or the end of the line), so a space inside a path cannot leave a tail; look-behinds keep `http://host/x`, `a/b` and `3:4` untouched. Tests add six path cases, four clean-text negative controls, and a planted-offender control proving the backslash-quote scan flags a bad string and ignores a clean one.

- Large-document OCR review round 2 (SM4; branch `sm4/large-doc-ocr-1`). The pause test was timing the checkpoint's disk flushes as well as the stop (3.01-3.43 s against 3.0 s); the job now records, in test builds only, when its stop released the workers, and the test asserts that moment is between 2.0 and 2.4 s after the pause (the 2 s stop wait plus scheduling), independent of disk speed; 10 of 10 solo runs pass. Startup cleanup now uses `inspect_manifest`: a job folder older than a day is removed only if its description is missing or invalid; one that cannot be read right now (locked, scanned) or carries a newer format number is kept. Save elsewhere (`prepare_relocation`, factored out of `relocate`) removes a temporary copy left next to the OLD destination before switching, uses the open document only if its identity matches the job, and otherwise (all pages done) uses a stand-in with no pages, so a resume that must redo a page pauses instead of rendering another document. When the OCR slot is still held by a page render that a pause left running, Resume says so instead of "OCR is already running". `ocr_process.rs` test helper: each fake engine case starts from an empty folder, because a leftover folder from an earlier run whose process had the same id made `input_and_asset_integrity_fail_before_process_creation` fail once in the OCR filter. Tests: sweep (missing, corrupt, newer, locked, young, foreign folders) and relocation (missing source, other document, app-data destination, old temp copy removed, stand-in source when all pages are done). OCR filter: 71 pass, 8 ignored, four runs.

- Large-document OCR review fixes (SM4; branch `sm4/large-doc-ocr-1`, now on top of `sm4/docs-031`; code review CHANGES and SM7 P3s). `searchable_pdf_stream.rs`: resume accepts a part file whose header was already switched to `%PDF` (crash between finalize and publish), truncates it and restores the partial header, so no work is lost; the final check bounds `/Width` and `/Height` to 1..=16,384 and 1 or 8 bits, uses checked arithmetic and returns a check failure instead of panicking on a tampered file; a failed rollback poisons the writer; the durability comment now says journal records are buffered for up to 1 s; a cross-volume publish records its temporary copy in `publish-temp`, and a leftover `.tmp*` directly in the destination folder is removed on the next start, discard or cancel; `set_destination` rewrites the manifest for Save elsewhere. `large_ocr_job.rs`: a worker counts itself out through a drop guard, and a panic in a page becomes a white placeholder reported as `internalError`; a stop waits at most 2 s for workers, so Pause and Cancel answer even when an in-process render cannot be interrupted (that render keeps running; its result is ignored). `large_ocr_commands.rs`: a panic in the job thread ends as a pause with a message instead of `running` forever; start, resume and Save elsewhere require an absolute `.pdf` destination in an existing folder outside app data that does not exist yet; Save elsewhere (`relocate_large_ocr`) points a paused job at a new destination and resumes it, without the source document when every page is done; job folders whose manifest no longer reads and that are older than 24 h are removed at startup (only 24-hex-character names); the saved-job status is read once and cached instead of on every poll. Panel: shows the destination on a paused job and offers Save elsewhere. `docs/features.md`: says a hung PDFium render stalls a job until the helper exists. OMP_THREAD_LIMIT not added: the pinned engine binary imports no OpenMP runtime (no `vcomp*.dll` reference), so it is single-threaded already. New tests: crash after finalize resumes byte-identical; impossible image size fails the check; stale publish temp removed only next to the destination; panicking page; Pause with an uninterruptible render; job-thread panic; destination rules; startup sweep; panel Save elsewhere.

- Large-document OCR step 3: app wiring and progress panel (SM4; branch `sm4/large-doc-ocr-1`; new `src-tauri/src/large_ocr_commands.rs`, `src/LargeOcrPanel.tsx`, `src/LargeOcrPanel.module.css`, `src/LargeOcrPanel.test.tsx`; small hunks in `service.rs` (two worker requests), `main.rs` (six commands, state, pause on window close), `ocr.rs`, `ocr_process.rs`, `ocr_commands.rs` (`configured_runner` crate-visible), `src/bridge.ts`, `src/App.tsx` (one menu item, panel mount); `docs/features.md`; no dependency, version, installer or workflow change). Available only when the build has the verified OCR engine (the same gate as the searchable copy). A document over 32 pages shows **Create searchable OCR copy (large document)…**; it asks for the destination, then runs in the background (`ocr-job` thread) with job folders under `<app data>\ocr-jobs`. `service.rs`: `OcrJobSource` checks the document (unprotected, export guard, text permission, 1-10,000 pages) and returns page sizes plus an identity = SHA-256 of the source bytes and of the page plan; `OcrJobRaster` renders one planned page like current-page OCR but at the highest DPI ≤ 150 that fits the 16 MiB input cap (`ocr::raster_dimensions_fitting`, reported as reduced resolution) and reports a page-only render failure separately from document errors, reusing the viewer's comment-rendered document cache. The job holds the OCR slot, so current-page OCR and the 32-page copy say OCR is busy meanwhile. Tesseract children for jobs run at below-normal priority (`recognize_p6_tsv_background`). Resume requires the open document to have the same identity. The panel polls status, shows page, time left, unread pages and the 1 GiB warning, confirms Cancel, offers Resume / Discard progress when paused and Open copy when saved; progress is announced every 10%. The 32-page path is unchanged. Tests: `LargeOcrPanel.test.tsx` 7; ignored real-engine test `real_engine_large_job_publishes_a_searchable_copy` run once with a local setup root: generated 120-page text PDF, 4 workers, 68.3 s (105.5 pages/min, debug app code), every page with text, sampled pages extract the expected lines. Not verified: installed builds, real scans, viewer smoothness during a job (renders share the viewer's PDF engine), very large outputs; saving in parts over 1 GiB is not built.

- Large-document OCR step 2: job engine (SM4; branch `sm4/large-doc-ocr-1`; new `src-tauri/src/large_ocr_job.rs`, module registered in `main.rs`; writer follow-ups in `searchable_pdf_stream.rs`: `reopen_for_resume` turns a finished-but-unpublished file back into a partial one, also after a failed final check, and `saved_pages` counts saved pages read-only; no app wiring, no user-visible change). `run_job` owns one job on the calling thread: N worker threads (1-4) claim pages in order but only within N + 1 pages of the next page to write, render (`PageSource` trait) and recognise (`PageRecognizer` trait, TSV) them and encode the image; the coordinator writes strictly in order. A page failure is retried once, then the page keeps its image without text (timed out, engine stopped, engine output refused) or becomes a white placeholder of the page's size (render failed) and is reported; five such pages in a row pause the job (breaker). Document-level errors (closed, changed) pause instead of degrading pages. `JobControl::pause` and `cancel` cancel the shared token, which kills running engine processes; pause keeps the checkpoint, cancel deletes the job folder. Progress at most 4 per second with a projected size after 10 pages (warning over 1 GiB) and time left after 5; the 1 GiB limit, a write error (disk full) or a publish failure (destination appeared) pause with a reason, and the work is kept. `SetThreadExecutionState` keeps the PC awake while the job thread runs; `automatic_workers` = logical cores / 4, clamped 1-4, lowered so the 256 MiB-per-engine budget stays under half the available memory. The traits are the seam for SM5's isolated render helper. Tests (12 + 1 ignored measurement, debug build, fake source and engine generated in code): 400-page job publishes with at most workers + 1 pages in memory and reduced-resolution pages listed; output identical with 1 and 4 workers; timeout, crash, refused output and render failure degrade exactly those pages, a first-attempt failure recovers on retry; five failures in a row pause at page 35 and resume completes; cancel mid-job (engine hung) leaves no job folder and no output; pause then resume, and a simulated crash with a torn journal, both give output byte-identical to a clean run; a document change pauses at the right page; a hung engine is stopped by pause in under a second; a full disk pauses and resume completes; an existing destination pauses, keeps the work, and publishes after it is removed. The ignored measurement (1,000 letter-size gray pages, 4 workers, debug build, run alongside other tests) recorded process private bytes of about 47 MB at page 100, 22 MB at the end and a 93 MB peak.

- Large-document OCR step 1: streaming searchable-PDF writer (SM4; branch `sm4/large-doc-ocr-1` off `2951e87`; new `src-tauri/src/searchable_pdf_stream.rs`, font/word helpers in `searchable_pdf.rs` made crate-visible, module registered in `main.rs`; nothing calls it from the app yet, so no user-visible change and the 32-page searchable copy is unchanged). Follows the SM4 large-document OCR design (§5, §7): pages are written one at a time to `output.part` (header `%PDW-PARTIAL-1`, never openable as a PDF) and only a ~150-byte record per page stays in memory; object ids are a pure function of the page index (1 catalog, 2 root, 3-8 shared invisible font, 3 objects per page, two-level page tree with fan-out 128), so the output is deterministic. Images are lossless in the smallest exact colour space (1-bit, 8-bit gray or RGB; Flate with the PNG Up predictor). Durability: every page is flushed to the OS; at most once a second the part file is flushed to disk and only then are the journal records (CRC by truncated SHA-256) appended and flushed, so a record never points past durable bytes. `resume` treats the folder as untrusted (bounded manifest and journal, sequential pages, monotonic offsets, character table rebuilt from records), truncates to the last complete page and continues. `finalize` writes the font, tree, catalog, classic xref and trailer, switches the header to `%PDF-1.7`, then re-reads every page from disk (xref framing, image decoded to the recorded pixel SHA-256, content stream decoded to the recorded word hash). `publish_noclobber` renames on the same volume or copies to a temporary file next to the destination, and never replaces an existing file; a commit callback runs just before the rename. Output cap 1 GiB, page cap 10,000. Tests (11, debug build, this machine): 300-page copy reopens in lopdf; two runs byte-identical; resume after torn part file and/or torn journal byte-identical to a clean run; tampered record and foreign or damaged folders refused; a changed pixel on disk fails the final check; a simulated disk-full write rolls back to the previous page; placeholder and reduced-resolution records; PDFium renders 1-bit, gray and colour pages pixel-exact at 150 DPI and extracts the words; publish refuses to overwrite and honours the commit callback.
- Test-only data folder override (SM5; branch `sm5/test-data-dirs` off `2951e87`; no version, installer or workflow change). Launching a debug build wrote recovery records to `%APPDATA%\local.pdfworkstation.desktop\recovery` and the WebView2 profile to `%LOCALAPPDATA%\local.pdfworkstation.desktop`, against the no-C: rule for local runs. New `src-tauri/src/data_dirs.rs`: in debug builds `SMACROBAT_TEST_DATA_DIR` set to an absolute folder (no `.` or `..` parts) moves app data to `<folder>\app-data` (recovery root, update-attempt note) and the WebView2 profile to `<folder>\webview`; the configured windows are then created in setup with that data directory, so Tauri never creates the profile folder. A relative or dotted value stops the app with exit code 2 instead of falling back to the profile. `main.rs` and `recovery_commands.rs` resolve app data through `data_dirs::app_data_dir`. In release builds the variable, its parser and the window override are compiled out (`cfg(debug_assertions)`): the release exe built from this branch contains no `SMACROBAT_TEST_DATA_DIR` string while the debug exe does, and release behaviour is the same `app_data_dir()` call as before. Tests: 4 new `data_dirs` unit tests (unset or empty keeps the defaults, absolute override, relative or dotted refused, release ignores it); the `recovery_commands` and `update_attempt` filters pass. Source-build check: the debug exe launched with the override for 12 s wrote `app-data\recovery` and a 213-entry `webview` profile under the folder, and the entry counts and write times of both profile folders on C: were unchanged; a relative value exited 2 with the message. Not changed: SM2's and SM3's UI Automation verifiers still look for records in the profile; they can now pass the variable.

- OCR docs review fixes (SM4; documentation only; branch `sm4/docs-031`). README: the SM6 "7-Zip and the standalone silent installers" line removed by the previous commit is restored with the file's final newline.

- OCR docs match the published 0.3.1 (SM4; documentation only; branch `sm4/docs-031` off `2951e87`). `docs/features.md` OCR section: both tools were labelled "source build; planned for the default installer from 0.3.1" and said published releases do not include them; they now say the published 0.3.1 installer includes them (searchable copy up to 32 pages), that the 0.3.1 draft and installed checks verified the five packaged OCR files and the engine signature, that no check on the published installer ran OCR recognition, that installed recognition was exercised only on packaged `welcome.pdf` page 1 by the signed opt-in 0.2.6 artifact, and that no installed build has created a searchable copy. `docs/ocr-feasibility.md`: Status, the runner-foundation note and Remaining work updated the same way; the accuracy line now says no real-scan result against hand-verified ground truth is recorded. README already matched. No code changed.

- OCR cwd test fixed for 8.3 temp paths (SM0; test only, `ocr_process.rs`; branch `sm0/ocr-cwd-shortpath` off `424beee`). CI run 40630914771 failed `child_working_directory_is_a_private_temp_directory_outside_the_engine_root`: the hosted runner reports the child cwd under the short `C:UsersRUNNER~1…Temp` while the test compared it with the canonical long `runneradmin` path. The test now canonicalizes the reported directory's parent (which still exists) and requires it to equal the canonical temp root exactly, which is stricter than the old prefix check. `cargo test --bins ocr` 40 pass locally; the short-path case itself was not reproducible locally (8.3 names are disabled on E:).

- OCR hardening follow-up (SM4; branch `sm4/sweep-once` off `06e50d6`; no app version, installer or workflow change). `ocr_process.rs`: the stale `pdf-workstation-ocr-<32 hex>` sweep now runs once per process (`std::sync::Once`) instead of at every OCR job. New test backdates matching directories 25 h with `File::set_modified` on a backup-semantics handle in a private temp root and asserts the old matching directory is deleted while a fresh one and an old non-matching name are kept.

- In-app update check: baseline 0.2.0 → published 0.3.1 (SM6; script, tests and docs; branch `sm6/small-2026-10-28` off `06e50d6`; no workflow, app or installer change; Open 38 and old Open 16). After the updater key swap the next release is signed with the new key, which published 0.2.0 does not trust, so `scripts/verify-in-app-update.ps1` must start from 0.3.1, the first build that embeds the new key. Pins: release 425998245, tag `v0.3.1`, installer asset 682909274 `PDF.Workstation_0.3.1_x64-setup.exe`, 11,210,976 bytes, SHA-256 `67C685264ABC094246E95C04629BFFF5D4854BD4FE5B84FEDE8760261A119038`, publisher Joshua Beel (all read back with `gh api` on 2026-10-27). A third authenticated API read (`releases/tags/v0.3.1`) binds the baseline release id, tag, stable state and the asset's id, name, size, digest and download URL (`Assert-ExactAsset` now takes the tag explicitly). 0.3.1 is publisher-signed, so the baseline installer must be Authenticode `Valid` with that publisher and a trusted timestamp, and the installed baseline application is checked as `Valid` too (three times: after install, after the blocked download, after declining). Its `tauri.conf.json` at `v0.3.1` polls `Joshua-Beel/PDF-Workstation/releases/latest/download/latest.json`, the verifier's existing manifest pin, so the check no longer exercises the `F-CK-Adobe` redirect; `docs/releases.md` now says run 40625100964 was the last proof of that path. UI: `src/Updates.tsx`, `src/updateCommands.ts` and `src-tauri/src/update_commands.rs` are unchanged between `v0.3.1` and `06e50d6`. The available-update check now uses the pinned baseline version and requires 0.3.1's save notice ("The app will close and reopen during installation. Save your work before continuing."). 0.3.1 asks before installing in a native `MessageBoxW` (`MB_YESNO | MB_ICONWARNING | MB_DEFBUTTON2 | MB_SETFOREGROUND`, title "Install update?", text telling the user to save first) owned by the main window from a worker thread. After the retry download the verifier waits for "Waiting for you to confirm installing version X…", finds exactly one visible `#32770` window of the baseline process, and requires title, exact text (CRLF normalised), owner in the same process and disabled (modal), only Yes (6) and No (7) buttons, both enabled, and No as the default (`DM_GETDEFID`). It answers No by posting `WM_COMMAND` (no focus or click needed), requires "Version X was not installed. Your installed version is unchanged. You can install it later.", no alert, the button enabled, the 0.3.1 install facts and both sentinels unchanged, the same baseline process running and no dialog left; then it clicks Install again, requires the same box and the waiting status, and answers Yes; the existing install, relaunch, recheck and repair steps follow. The retry's download evidence now also accepts the waiting status, which the app sets only after a completed download. Record schema 5: baseline release and asset ids, `signature = 'Valid'` and publisher; `updater.confirmation` (title, text receipt, default No, answered by window message, declined status); `nativeInstallConfirmationVerified`, `confirmationDeclineKeptBaseline`, `baselinePublisherSignatureVerified`. Local proof of the Win32 part: a pwsh child showed a real owned `MessageBoxW` with the same flags and text; the verifier's own functions read title, text, `&Yes`/`&No`, default 7, same-process disabled owner, rejected the text for another version, and answered No then Yes (the box returned 7 then 6). `scripts/signed-in-app-update-workflow.test.ts`: baseline pins and binding, the three API reads, the shipped `Updates.tsx` strings and `update_commands.rs` title, flags and confirmation format checked against the verifier, decline-before-accept order, mismatch naming for each confirmation fact, polling, two-dialog refusal and Yes-default refusal: 21/21. Not established until the next release runs it hosted.

- Workflows: Node 20 actions retired (SM6; workflows and tests; branch `sm6/small-2026-10-28` off `06e50d6`; not yet run hosted). GitHub Actions is deprecating the Node 20 action runtime. All 16 workflows used `actions/*` releases whose `action.yml` says `using: node20`: checkout 4.4.0 / 4.2.2, upload-artifact 4.6.2, download-artifact 4.3.0, setup-node 4.4.0, setup-dotnet 4.3.1, cache/restore and cache/save 4.3.0. Each now pins a `node24` release by full commit SHA, each SHA checked with `gh api repos/actions/<name>/git/ref/tags/<tag>` (all lightweight tags pointing at commits) and its `action.yml` read at that tag: checkout v7.0.1 `3d3c42e5…`, upload-artifact v7.0.2 `cf430e03…`, download-artifact v7.0.0 `37930b1c…`, setup-node v7.1.0 `949feb24…`, setup-dotnet v6.0.0 `a98b5685…`, cache v6.1.0 `55cc8345…` (restore and save). Checkout and download-artifact match the versions `ocr-installer-upgrade.yml` already used; download-artifact stays on 7.0.0 rather than 8 (8 makes digest mismatches fail by default; a separate decision). All six releases are older than two weeks. Release notes read for each major in between: no input this repo uses was removed; `persist-credentials: false` and every other `with:` value are unchanged; setup-node keeps the explicit `cache: npm`. One behaviour change: since download-artifact 5, a single artifact downloaded by `artifact-ids` without `merge-multiple` extracts straight into `path/`, not `path/<artifact name>/`. That affects the Step 1 proof download in `default-recovery-verification.yml`, `default-comments-forms-verification.yml` and `default-expanded-page-tools-verification.yml`. Their verifiers find the Step 1 receipt with a recursive one-file search, which accepts either layout, and the rehearsal-proof branch (`Read-DefaultArtifactRehearsal`) requires exactly one top-level file, which only the new flat layout gives. `dtolnay/rust-toolchain` is a composite action and is unchanged. The pin tests in 12 workflow test files now name the new SHAs, and `scripts/workflow-action-pins.test.ts` gains a per-workflow check that every `actions/*` reference is one of these reviewed Node 24 pins. 17 workflow test files: 146/146. Proof that the workflows run is still needed: the first hosted `ci.yml` run after merge.

- Release scripts: no assignment to the automatic `$matches` (SM6; scripts and test only; branch `sm6/small-2026-10-28` off `06e50d6`; no app, installer or workflow change). `scripts/prepare-default-draft-pdfium.ps1` `Assert-PdfiumReleaseMetadata` assigned the release-asset filter result to `$matches`, which shadows PowerShell's automatic `$Matches`; renamed `$assetMatches`. Extending the AST check found the same pattern twice in `scripts/verify-default-draft.ps1` (the draft asset lookup and the single-file lookup); renamed `$assetMatches` and `$fileMatches`. Reads of `$Matches` after `-match` are unchanged. `scripts/default-draft-verification-workflow.test.ts` gains an AST test that parses both scripts and fails on any assignment to an automatic or read-only variable (the same list as the recovery and installed-check tests); it failed on the unfixed verifier (`matches`) before the rename. The 11 vitest files that read either script: 86/86.

- Security: hostile-nesting test measured against the production stack (SM7; test and one dev-dependency; branch `sm7/scrub-depth`; no app behaviour change). Code review of `5d1397a`: `cargo test --bins editor` aborted with STATUS_STACK_OVERFLOW on 4 of 5 parallel runs. Cause: lopdf 0.45 parses indirect objects and object streams on rayon global-pool threads (default `rayon` feature, `reader.rs` `par_iter`), not on the caller, and in a debug build a 100-level object needs ~1.95 MiB, just under those threads' 2 MiB std default. The overflowing thread was unnamed; naming the test thread confirmed it was not the overflow. Production: the `pdf-worker` thread (`service.rs`, no `stack_size`) and the rayon pool both use the 2 MiB std default; no `RUST_MIN_STACK` is set. Measured by binary search over thread stack size, arrays and dictionaries, 100 levels: debug load, save-copy, the hostile 101/100,000-level files, a 100-level trailer and a 100-level content operand all need ~1.95 MiB (about 19.5 KiB per level); release needs ≤ 259 KiB (about 2.5 KiB per level), so production has about 7.9× margin; save-copy's own walkers on the calling thread needed under 67 KiB in debug. No fix needed. The test now runs on a private two-thread rayon pool (`rayon = "=1.12.0"` dev-dependency, already in `Cargo.lock` through lopdf) at a quarter of production (512 KiB) in release, which asserts a 4× margin, and at twice production (4 MiB) in debug; it also covers a 100-level trailer and content operand, parsed on the calling thread. Renamed to `hostile_nesting_depth_is_bounded_by_the_parser_and_save_copy_fits_the_production_stack`. `editor` filter, default parallel: debug 10/10, release 10/10 (23 tests each).

- Security: hostile nesting depth in save-copy (SM7; test only; branch `sm7/scrub-depth` off `a67cfe1`; no app behaviour change). Question from the review of SM2 `dcefeea`: `null_references::scrub` in `editor.rs` recurses once per level of array/dictionary nesting, so an unbounded depth would overflow the stack (an abort on Windows). Finding: bounded. lopdf 0.45.0's parser (`src/parser/mod.rs`) counts depth in `_direct_object` and fails with `TooLarge` past `reader::MAX_NESTING_DEPTH = 100`; every entry that yields an `Object` (indirect objects, stream and trailer dictionaries, xref streams, object streams via `direct_object`, content operands, inline images) passes that budget. `scrub` and the other recursive walkers do not follow references, so their depth is the parsed depth: `pdf_load::reachable`, `image_edit::count_references`, `text_edit::count_refs`, the `service.rs` reference collector, and `forms` `safe` (its own cap of 32); `combine` and `comments` walk iteratively; `page_labels` caps tree and reference depth at 16. New test `hostile_nesting_depth_is_bounded_by_the_parser_and_save_copy_survives_a_small_stack` generates PDFs (arrays and dictionaries) on a 512 KiB thread in a debug build: 100 levels load and save-copy with page 2 deleted, and the reference to it at the bottom is nulled; 101 levels are refused at load; 101 and 100,000 levels through export return without a crash. The 101 assertion fails if a lopdf upgrade drops the bound. `editor::` 23/23.

- Post-release P3s (SM6; review P3s on `sm6/post-release-031`; docs, one script line and tests; branch `sm6/post-release-p3s` off `bb96d4b`; no app, installer or workflow change). `docs/releases.md`: the 0.3.1 in-app update evidence and the signed OCR upgrade paragraph called the published 0.2.0 "unsigned". Its update package is signed (minisign key `85970C34AEABD775`); only the Windows publisher (Authenticode) signature is missing, and both places now say so. `verify-in-app-update.ps1`: the record leak scan also rejects a fine-grained personal access token shape (`github_pat_` plus 16 or more word characters). The token-shape test adds a `github_pat_` sample, which none of the earlier patterns caught. `signed-in-app-update-workflow` 20/20.

- Docs: 0.3.1 release evidence (SM6; docs only; branch `sm6/post-release-031`). New `docs/releases.md` § "0.3.1 (published)" under Verification: release 425998245, tag `v0.3.1` on `88bff90`, published 2026-10-27 21:36Z, with its three asset ids and sizes; the pre-tag rehearsal (signed OCR artifact run 40358532523, artifact 12671520323 from `96b0521`, and the eight installed checks that passed on it: default page tools 40548429058, comments/forms 40560976043, recovery 40366960608; signed expanded page tools 40523064917, OCR upgrade 40366971134, persistence 40366977658, print dialog 40366983518, reading tools 40366989008); release run 40580023911; Step 1 run 40586164144; Step 2 runs 40586452295, 40586456292, 40586460693; and in-app update run 40625100964. The in-app update record (artifact 12788927502, 5,962 bytes, schema 3) was downloaded and read for this entry. The section states what that run established and what it did not: the Explorer Digital Signatures UI, UAC and SmartScreen prompts, partial-download, app-kill and installer-stage interruption, installer transactionality, real user data, and other machines. It also notes the two schema-3 fields that overstate it, which schema 4 fixes. Run ids and states were read from the GitHub API; the rehearsal-to-check mapping is from the SM0 board. - In-app update record schema 4: honest metadata and setup-exit claims, JWT leak pattern (SM6; script, tests and docs; branch `sm6/post-release-031` off `17517e1`; no app, installer, workflow or dispatch-input change). Since the API-auth change, the two `api.github.com` release-metadata reads carry the job's read-only token, so `verification.publicUnauthenticatedReleaseMetadataVerified` (true in run 40625100964's schema-3 record) no longer described what ran. It is replaced by `releaseMetadataVerified = true` and `releaseMetadataAuthenticated = true`; `publicLatestManifestVerified = true` stays and gains `publicLatestManifestAuthenticated = false`, because `latest.json`, both installers and the `.sig` are still fetched without credentials, as 0.2.0 fetches them. Code-review P3: `updaterSetupExitVerified` was a constant `true`, even when `Wait-UpdaterSetupExit` saw no setup process on its first poll. The function now returns `processObserved` and `exitVerified`; the record writes `updaterSetupProcessObserved` and `updaterSetupExitVerified`, and the exit is claimed only when a setup process was seen and then gone. The behaviour is unchanged: the wait still fails if any setup process remains at the 60-second deadline, and a run that sees none still passes, recording both as false. The scope text names the authenticated metadata reads and the setup-exit condition. Schema 3 to 4. SM7 optional P3: the record leak scan also rejects a case-sensitive JWT shape (`eyJ` plus three dot-separated base64url segments of 8 or more characters), the shape GitHub's job token can now take. Tests: schema 4 and the new field pins; no constant-true setup claim; observed and unseen setup outcomes; the scan pattern, read from the script, rejects a JWT, a `ghp_` token and a host path and accepts a receipt-style record with hex hashes and an upper-cased JWT. The scan and the new scope text were also checked locally against run 40625100964's downloaded record (artifact 12788927502): no match. `signed-in-app-update-workflow` 20/20. Not run hosted.

- In-app update check: accept the current GitHub token format (SM0; script and test only; branch `sm0/inapp-token-format` off `bca01a0`). Run 40624309053 stopped at once with "token unavailable or malformed": the workflow token reached the step, but the check only allowed `[A-Za-z0-9_]{20,255}`, and GitHub tokens can now be longer and contain `.` and `-`. The check now allows `[A-Za-z0-9_.-]` up to 4,096 characters, anchored with `A`/`z`. The new test also showed the old `$` anchor let a token with a trailing newline through, because .NET `$` matches before a final newline; `z` closes that. Tests: a JWT-shaped token is accepted; empty, short, space, `!`, trailing-newline and 4,097-character values are rejected and cleared. In-app and launch workflow tests 43/43.

- In-app update check: driver start inside the try (SM6; review P3; script and test only). `$driverCapture.Start()` ran before `Start-WebDriverSession`'s `try`, so a failed start skipped its cleanup and left the capture undisposed. It now runs as the first statement inside the `try`. The `catch` saves the original error and runs the best-effort `Stop-WebDriverSession -AllowIncomplete` inside its own `try`, which disposes the capture if the stop itself fails (for example, on a process that never started). It then rethrows the saved error, so a cleanup error cannot replace it. Test: a missing tauri-driver path surfaces the process-start error, not a cleanup error, plus static checks on the order.

- In-app update check: minimal environment for 7-Zip and the standalone installers (SM6; review P3; script and test only). `Invoke-BoundedProcess` takes an optional `-Environment`: when given, it clears the child environment and sets only those variables. Both call sites, the 7-Zip extraction and `Invoke-SilentInstaller` (the baseline 0.2.0 install and the 0.3.1 repair install), now pass the shared `Get-MinimalWindowsProcessEnvironment`, the same ten-variable allowlist used to start tauri-driver. This is defence in depth: the API token is already removed before any child process starts. The updater's own installer launch, from inside 0.2.0, is not the verifier's to control. Test: a `cmd.exe` child run with `-Environment` cannot see a sentinel variable set in the parent, and a control run without it can (exit 3), which proves the check works; the static check covers both call sites.

- In-app update check: wait for updater setup to exit (SM6; review P3; script and test only). Nothing waited for the updater's installer process before the post-update WebDriver session and the repair stage, so a still-running setup could race them. tauri-plugin-updater 2.11.0 (the one 0.2.0 ships) writes the installer as `<app>-<version>-installer.exe` in a `<app>-<version>-updater-*` folder under the temp directory and starts it with `ShellExecuteW`. After the relaunch decision and before `$postSession`, `Wait-UpdaterSetupExit` polls `Win32_Process` every 250 ms for up to 60 s (`UpdaterSetupExitDeadlineSeconds`). It looks for any `.exe` started no earlier than 2 s before `$retryStarted` whose path lies under `$env:TEMP`, `GetTempPath()` or `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Temp`; all three are checked because the short and long forms of the temp path can differ. If any remain, it throws, giving the count and sanitized file names only. The record adds `verification.updaterSetupExitVerified = true`. Tests: exact detection (ignores an older temp process, the installed app and a sibling `Temp2` folder; finds the installer), polling until exit, and the remaining-process failure message with no path.

- In-app update check: check the WebDriver cleanup result (SM6; review P3; script and test only). `Stop-WebDriverSession` discarded the result of the shared `Stop-OwnedLaunchProcesses`. The new `Assert-WebDriverSessionStopped` requires the tauri-driver root outcome to be `already-exited`, `tree-kill-exited` or `fallback-exited`, and every captured category (application, tauri-driver, edge-driver, webview, ocr-engine, other) to be `absent` or `all-exited`. Otherwise it throws, naming the enum values only. It applies to the two main-flow stops (after the baseline session and after the post-update session). The failed-start path and the `finally` block pass `-AllowIncomplete` and run inside `try`, so cleanup there cannot hide the original error. Tests: the three accepted root outcomes; refusal of `deadline`, `kill-failed`, `not-invoked` and an `incomplete` category; the static split between the main-flow and error-path calls.

- Docs: in-app update schema reference (SM6; review P3; docs only). `docs/releases.md` said "schema v2 records those claims as false" for the hosted publisher-UI claims, but the in-app record is now schema v3. It now reads "its record (schema v2 onward; currently v3)". No code change.

- In-app update check: record both shell-window probes (SM6; review P3 from the `sm6/inapp-webdriver-align` review; script and test only). The second `GetShellWindow` probe, taken at the end of the relaunch wait, was used in the decision but not recorded. It is now held in `$shellWindowAtRelaunch` and written as `updater.shellWindowAtRelaunch` beside `shellWindowBeforeUpdate`. The decision is unchanged, and so is schema v3: the field is additive, within the same unpublished schema. The static test pins the variable, its use in `Resolve-AutomaticRelaunch` and the receipt field.

- In-app update check: authenticated GitHub API metadata reads (SM6; script, workflow, tests and docs; branch `sm6/inapp-api-auth` off `d58c38f`; no app, installer or dispatch-input change). Rerun 40622695094 failed about a second into the step: the first unauthenticated `api.github.com/repos/…/releases/tags/v0.3.1` read returned 403, almost certainly the 60-per-hour unauthenticated limit on a shared runner IP. The 0.2.0 updater never calls the API (it fetches `github.com/…/releases/latest/download/latest.json`), so authenticating the verifier's two metadata reads does not change what is proven. Workflow: the exercise step alone gets `IN_APP_UPDATE_API_TOKEN: ${{ github.token }}`; permissions stay `contents: read`. Script: before anything else, even dot-sourcing the shared helper, `Read-ApiTokenFromEnvironment` reads the variable, removes it from the process environment (`[NullString]::Value`; `$null` would leave an empty variable in PowerShell), checks it is gone, and requires a 20 to 255 character `[A-Za-z0-9_]` value. So 7-Zip, the installers, the drivers and the app never inherit it. `Invoke-GitHubReleaseApi` builds `https://api.github.com` plus an exact path matching `/repos/Joshua-Beel/PDF-Workstation/releases/(latest|tags/vX.Y.Z)` (no query, fragment or user info). It uses a handler with `AllowAutoRedirect = false` and refuses any handler that follows redirects, so the `Authorization: Bearer` header cannot ride a redirect. It accepts only 200 and refuses a 3xx without following it. It reads the body under the same 1 MiB bound. On 403 or 429 it prints only `GITHUB_API_DIAG status=… rateLimitRemaining=… rateLimitReset=…`, each value digits-only (otherwise `invalid` or `absent`). The token is cleared from the script variable right after the two reads. `Invoke-PublicDownload` now refuses `api.github.com` and still sends no credential; the baseline, installer, signature and `latest.json` downloads stay unauthenticated. The sanitized-record scan also rejects `IN_APP_UPDATE_API_TOKEN` and `gh[pousr]_…` patterns. Tests (a recording `HttpMessageHandler`, run from a file): the header is sent once, to `api.github.com`, as Bearer; a 302 is refused with exactly one request; five off-contract paths are refused before sending; the redirect-following handler is refused; the token is removed from the environment and a child `pwsh` cannot see it; malformed tokens are refused and still cleared; 403 and 429 report only status and header values, including a response that echoes the token in a header or body; the public download carries no header; and the token appears nowhere in stdout or stderr. Static checks: the token is read before the dot-source, the hosted-runner check, any download, extraction, install or driver start; the workflow carries exactly one token line, in the exercise step. `signed-in-app-update-workflow` 15/15 pass. Unverified until the in-app update check is rerun.

- Save-copy prune regression test (SM2; test only, `editor.rs`; branch `sm2/prune-kept-test` off `5326351`). Code-review P3 on `dcefeea`: an indirect destination array that targets a kept page, shared by two links, must survive the prune; the test asserts both links stay in `/Annots` with `/Dest` intact and the array still targets the kept page. `cargo test --bins editor` 22 pass. Also: README `## Recent changes` trimmed to the newest 15 lines (SM0); every removed line has its full entry here.

- In-app update check: honest automatic-relaunch outcome without an Explorer shell (SM6; script, tests and docs only; branch `sm6/inapp-webdriver-align`, separate commit on top of the WebDriver alignment; no app, installer, workflow or dispatch-input change). The 0.2.0 updater (tauri-plugin-updater 2.11.0) starts the downloaded installer with `/P /UPDATE /R`. The relaunch itself comes from the target installer's `.onInstSuccess`, which calls `nsis_tauri_utils::RunAsUser` (template of tauri-cli 2.11.4, used by both v0.2.0 and v0.3.1). That plugin DLL, extracted from the published 0.3.1 installer, imports `GetShellWindow`, `GetWindowThreadProcessId`, `OpenProcessToken`, `DuplicateTokenEx` and `CreateProcessWithTokenW`: from an elevated installer it launches the app with the Explorer shell window's token. GitHub-hosted runners never showed an Explorer window (controlled run 36950684317, and run 40588308128 at `wait-properties`), so an elevated hosted installer probably cannot relaunch. Not proven on a hosted run yet. Change: `Test-ShellWindowPresent` calls `user32!GetShellWindow` once before the first WebDriver session and again when the relaunch wait ends. `Resolve-AutomaticRelaunch` decides the outcome. An observed relaunch is recorded as verified. A missing relaunch still fails, with the same message as before, if either probe found a shell window. Only when both probes found none is it recorded as `automaticRelaunchObserved = false`, `automaticRelaunchVerified = false`, `automaticRelaunchNotObservedReason = 'github-hosted-no-explorer-shell'`. The relaunch wait is unchanged (up to the 180-second install deadline). The current-version recheck always ran in a fresh session the verifier launches through WebDriver; the record now says so with `updater.postUpdateLaunchedBy = 'verifier-webdriver'` and `updater.shellWindowBeforeUpdate`. Schema 2 to 3; the scope text says the relaunch is conditional and the recheck is verifier-launched; `docs/releases.md` updated to match. Tests: static order and record fields (no literal true relaunch claim); `Resolve-AutomaticRelaunch` verifies an observed relaunch, refuses to excuse a missing one when either probe saw a shell, and records the no-shell reason; the probe rejects a non-boolean provider, and the live probe returns a boolean. `signed-in-app-update-workflow` 13/13 pass. Unverified until the in-app update check is rerun.

- In-app update check: WebDriver lifecycle aligned with the hosted-proven verifiers (SM6; scripts and tests only; branch `sm6/inapp-webdriver-align` off `387402e`; no app, installer, workflow or dispatch-input change). Rerun 40619759531 failed 18 seconds into the step: the verifier's own `Invoke-LoopbackJson` capped every WebDriver request, including `POST /session`, at 10 seconds through `Invoke-RestMethod -TimeoutSec`, and creating a session (tauri-driver, msedgedriver, app and WebView2 start) takes longer than that on a cold hosted runner. Rather than raise one number, the verifier now dot-sources `installed-app-launch.ps1` and drives WebDriver exactly as the passing default expanded page-tools path (`Invoke-FreshInstalledImagePageToolsSession`) does. Before starting, it waits up to 300 seconds for the fixed ports to be free. It starts tauri-driver through `Start-BoundedDiscardProcess` with the minimal environment, on the shared ports 4444/4445 (was 4444/9515), under the shared 60-second total deadline (was 45). It polls `/status` every 200 ms, then waits for the live native EdgeDriver status to match the version in the receipt, which is now checked with `Assert-WebDriverReceipt`. It creates the session with `Invoke-BoundedLoopbackJson`: 30 seconds for `POST /session`, 10 seconds for every other command. Like the shared helpers, it does not retry session creation. It identifies the app as the one owned descendant of the driver at the exact path (`Get-OwnedLaunchProcesses`), and the baseline PID and start time must equal that owned process. On stop it deletes the session with `Invoke-SessionDeleteOutcome` and stops the driver and every owned descendant with `Stop-OwnedLaunchProcesses` under the shared 30-second cleanup bound; a failed start cleans up the same way. The verifier's own `Invoke-LoopbackJson` and `Invoke-WebDriverScript` are removed; the shared `Invoke-WebDriverScript` has the same signature. `Wait-WebDriverValue` now checks its deadline before each call, so a timeout names the stage. New `Assert-FirewallEnforcing` runs before the first session: it requires the `MpsSvc` service to be running and all three firewall profiles enabled, and otherwise fails at once, naming the service state and disabled profiles, instead of after 135 seconds in the blocked-download stage. Also renamed `$profile` (the automatic `$PROFILE`) to `$profilePath` inside the shared `Invoke-InstalledAppLaunchSmoke`, now that the AST guard covers `installed-app-launch.ps1`. Tests: shared-helper use and removed patterns (`Invoke-RestMethod`, `TimeoutSec`, port 9515); the verifier redefines no function from either shared helper; the firewall precheck accepts the enforcing case and fails closed on a stopped service, a disabled profile, or a missing profile. `signed-in-app-update-workflow`, `installed-app-launch-workflow`, `signed-ocr-upgrade-workflow` and `verifier-function-imports` 55/55 pass. Unverified until the in-app update check is rerun.

- In-app update check: no assignments to automatic variables, no stray bool output (SM6; scripts and tests only; branch `sm6/inapp-reserved-vars` off `a8bc72c`; no app, installer, workflow or dispatch-input change). Rerun 40618989631 passed the baseline facts and failed about 13 seconds later with "Cannot overwrite variable HOME because it is read-only or constant": `Open-UpdatesDialog` assigned `$home` (PowerShell's constant `$HOME`; variable names are case-insensitive). The `True` printed just before the error came from statement-level `WaitForExit(10000)` calls, whose bool result went to output: in `Stop-WebDriverSession`, run by the `finally` block as the error unwound, and also in `Stop-ExactProcesses` and the `Start-WebDriverSession` failure path. Fixes: `$home` renamed `$homeUi`; `$matches` (writable, but it shadows `-match` results) renamed `$found` in `Assert-ExactAsset` and `Get-AppProcesses`, and in the five functions of the dot-sourced `installed-publisher-ui.ps1` that used it; the three `WaitForExit(10000)` results are assigned to `$null`. PSScriptAnalyzer is not available offline, so the sweep used the PowerShell AST: assignments, `foreach` variables and parameters in both files, checked against the full automatic-variable list. After the fix none remain. New test in `signed-in-app-update-workflow.test.ts`: it runs the same AST scan over both files and fails on any assignment to an automatic variable or any statement-level `WaitForExit(...)` whose result is not used. It first proves the scanner catches seeded `$home`, `[object[]]$Matches`, `foreach ($input …)`, `param $PID` and one bare `WaitForExit`, and ignores `$null =` and `if (...)` uses. `signed-in-app-update-workflow` and `signed-ocr-upgrade-workflow` 21/21 pass (`--no-file-parallelism`). Unverified until the in-app update check is rerun.

- Save-copy prune follow-ups (SM2; `editor::remove_page_references` and one test in `editor.rs` only; branch `sm2/prune-p3s` off `a8bc72c`; no app version, IPC or UI change). Three code-review P3s on the removed-page reference prune: (a) a `/Dest`, or a GoTo `/D`, that points at an indirect array object targeting a removed page is now dropped like the inline case instead of becoming `[null ...]`; (b) a link annotation is dropped only when this prune removed its last `/Dest` or `/A`; a link that already had neither is kept untouched (simple and safe: nothing was wrong with it); (c) new test `page_reference_removal_resolves_indirect_destinations_keeps_bare_links_and_cleans_annots` covers indirect `/Dest`, indirect and referenced-action GoTo, the bare link, a kept link, and the nulls stripped from `/Annots`. `cargo test --bins editor` 22 pass; `--bins combine` 15 pass. Not run: full suite, installed app.

- OCR hardening (SM4; branch `sm4/inbox-2026-10-28` off `a8bc72c`; no app version, installer, workflow or script change). (1) `ocr_process.rs`: the Tesseract child's working directory is a fresh per-job directory `pdf-workstation-ocr-<128-bit random hex from the OS RNG (BCryptGenRandom, no new dependency)>` under the user temp folder (created with create_dir, retried with a new name up to 5 times on AlreadyExists, never deleting a directory it did not create; removed when the job ends, best effort; at OCR start, directories of exactly that name pattern, not reparse points, older than 24 h are swept) instead of the folder holding `tesseract.exe`, so a relative write cannot land in the install folder and survive uninstall; the fake test child now locates its files from its own exe path and records its cwd, and a new test asserts the cwd is under temp, not under the engine folder, and removed after the job. (2) Flaky OCR tests: the finish-barrier, suspended-cancel and unwind tests used a 2 s child timeout, and `wait_for_pid` / `assert_process_reaped` polled for a fixed 3 s / 10 s, all of which a loaded parallel `cargo test --bins` can exceed; they now use 120 s deadlines that only bound a hang. Also, the fake-child build directory was shared by every test process in a worktree, so two concurrent `cargo test` runs rewrote and copied `fake-child.exe` under each other; it is now per process (`fake-build-<pid>`). Root cause of the remaining Busy failures: service tests release the OCR slot from the PDF worker thread after replying, so the test lock could be released a moment before the slot; `test_lock()` now also waits (bounded) for the slot to be free and asserts it is. The existing 200 ms `timeout_and_unwind_drop_kill_and_reap_the_owned_child` test still proves `TimedOut`. (3) `src-tauri/ocr_build.rs`: the `PDF_WORKSTATION_OCR_LICENSE_*_BYTES/SHA256` `cargo:rustc-env` lines (read by nothing) are gone; `license_environment` became `require_exact_licenses`, which still requires exactly the three licence receipts, with its test updated.

- In-app update check: installed-facts expectations match the real installers (SM6; script and tests only; branch `sm6/inapp-baseline-facts` off `9f469f5`; no app, installer, workflow or dispatch-input change). Rerun 40596111704 passed the publisher step and failed six seconds later in `Assert-InstallFacts` on the 0.2.0 baseline. Cause: Tauri's NSIS template writes `InstallLocation` as `"$\"$INSTDIR$\""`, a value with literal quotes. The verifier passed it straight to `[IO.Path]::GetFullPath`, which treats a quoted string as relative and prefixes the current directory, so the comparison could never match. Static evidence: v0.2.0 and v0.3.1 both lock `@tauri-apps/cli-win32-x64-msvc` 2.11.4 and use the default NSIS template; that CLI's embedded template writes `DisplayName` `${PRODUCTNAME}` (`PDF Workstation`), `DisplayVersion` `${VERSION}`, and the quoted `InstallLocation`. The published installers, downloaded and extracted with 7-Zip outside any install (0.2.0: 6,418,229 bytes, SHA-256 `17788CB8…4D2E`; 0.3.1: 11,210,976 bytes, SHA-256 `67C68526…9038`), carry `pdf-workstation.exe` with FileVersion and ProductVersion exactly `0.2.0` / `0.3.1` (numeric `x.y.z.0`, which the string fields do not show). 0.2.0 is Authenticode `NotSigned`; 0.3.1 is `Valid`, publisher `Joshua Beel`, timestamped. The signed OCR upgrade verifier, which passed hosted on the same 0.2.0 installer, already strips these quotes. Fix: `Test-InstallLocation` requires exactly one leading and one trailing quote, no other quote, no surrounding whitespace, and a fully qualified inner path equal to the expected root (ordinal case-insensitive, as Windows paths are; trailing separator ignored). An unquoted value is now rejected, so the check is not loosened. `Assert-InstallFacts` now names every mismatched field: `displayName`, `appBytes` and `appSha256` by name only; version strings and signature status with their value only when it matches `^[A-Za-z0-9.+-]{1,32}$` (otherwise `<unprintable>`); `installLocation` with its form (`quoted`, `unquoted` or `other`). It never prints a path. The publisher check names `publisher` or `hasTimestamp`. The same fix covers the upgraded (0.3.1) and repaired checks. Tests: `signed-in-app-update-workflow.test.ts` covers the exact unsigned baseline, case-only and trailing-separator equality, rejection with named fields for unquoted, wrong-folder, doubled-quote, padded and relative locations, a wrong display name, `x.y.z.0` versions, an unprintable value (no leak), `HashMismatch`, two mismatches at once, and a missing timestamp; 9/9 pass. Unverified until the in-app update check is rerun.

- In-app update check: hosted noninteractive publisher mode (SM6; scripts, workflow and tests only; branch `sm6/inapp-hosted-mode` off `88bff90`; no app, installer, version source or dispatch-input change). Post-publish run 40588308128 (`in-app-update-verification.yml` on `88bff90`) stopped at `verify-in-app-update.ps1:477`: `Invoke-InstalledPublisherUiProof` failed at stage `wait-properties`, category `unexpected`. That is the known hosted limit: controlled run 36950684317 showed GitHub-hosted runners never expose the Explorer Properties window. The two Authenticode checks before it (installer and extracted application) had passed. Fix, the same pattern already used by `verify-signed-ocr-upgrade.ps1` and `reading-tools-verification.yml`: `verify-in-app-update.ps1` takes a mandatory `-PublisherUiMode` restricted to `HostedNonInteractive` and rechecks it right after `Assert-HostedRunner`, before any download. Both publisher proofs (target installer, installed application) now call `New-HostedPublisherUiNotAttemptedEvidence`, which binds the exact byte receipt and verifies the expected publisher and trusted timestamp twice, and records attempted/verified as false with reason `github-hosted-noninteractive-session` and no screenshots. That function moved verbatim from `verify-signed-ocr-upgrade.ps1` into the shared `installed-publisher-ui.ps1`, so both verifiers run one implementation. A new `Assert-HostedPublisherUiEvidence` checks the exact property set, kind, file name, bytes, SHA-256, publisher and the false claims before the record is written. Receipt schema 1 to 2: `installedTarget.publisherUi` gains `mode`/`attempted`/`verified`/`reason`/`screenshotsUsed`; `verification` gains `publisherUiMode = 'hosted-noninteractive'` and `publisherUiAttempted = $false`, and `installerShellPublisherUiVerified`/`installedApplicationShellPublisherUiVerified` are now false; the scope text says Explorer Digital Signatures UI is not attempted or verified. The workflow passes `-PublisherUiMode HostedNonInteractive`; its eight dispatch inputs are unchanged. Update flow, version, relaunch, sentinel, repair and signature checks are unchanged. Tests: `signed-in-app-update-workflow.test.ts` gains a static mode/receipt case and a pwsh case (exact receipt, two signature calls, rejects missing timestamp, wrong publisher, unsigned, wrong bytes, true shell-UI claims, other reason, wrong kind, extra property); `signed-ocr-upgrade-workflow.test.ts` loads the moved function from the helper and counts its checks per function. Targeted Vitest (`--no-file-parallelism`): those 2 files 20/20, and 8 related verifier files 86/86. Unverified until the in-app update check is rerun.

- Comments/forms verifier sweep (SM3; scripts and tests only; branch `sm3/comments-rehearsal`, on top of `1fed01c`; no app, installer, parity path, version source or workflow change). SM0 asked for a sweep of the whole verifier before review, because it has never reached its end. Each selector was checked against the source at `96b0521` (`src/` there is identical to master `71c3792`). Every step now goes through one helper, `Invoke-CommentsFormsPoll`, which polls until the session deadline, refuses any reply of an unexpected shape, and on timeout names only what is missing. **Would have failed on the first real run:** (1) The comments Save control matched button text "Save a copy", but that control is an icon button (`App.tsx:798`) whose only label is its aria-label, so the match found nothing. It now matches by aria-label. "Save filled copy" stays a text match (`FillFormsDialog.tsx:153`). (2) The comments reopen check clicked the "Comments" toggle (`App.tsx:820`) on every poll, so the panel opened and closed in turn. It now clicks only while the panel is absent. (3) The Consent checkbox and the Choice A radio were changed with the `checked` setter plus `change`/`input` events. React ignores those for checkboxes and radios because it listens for `click`, so the saved copy would have kept the source values. The verifier now clicks the box or radio, and only when its state differs. (4) The choice fixture compared and set `select.value` to the exports `north-001`/`print-001`. The dialog's option values are internal ids (`choice-{obj}-{gen}-{index}`, `forms.rs:617`), so the read never matched, and the set would have cleared both selections. The verifier now finds the exact `Destination, page 1` and `Delivery, page 1` selects and reads and sets them by the option label shown. The pins are now `South Hub|Email copy` to `North Hub|Print copy`, which are the labels for those same exports in the fixture's `/Opt`. **Races made polled:** form fields that were read before the dialog finished loading (a missing control would have thrown a TypeError); values that were set and then saved without confirming they took; editor text that was typed and saved in one script; Save controls disabled while busy. The comment layer is now taken from page 1's own container (`img` parent), so a page 2 layer that rendered first cannot receive the pointer. **Checked and unchanged:** `Add comment`/`Add area highlight` aria-labels (unique), `Comment text`/`Highlight description` textareas, `Save comment`/`Save area highlight`, `aside[aria-label="Comments"]` with the `Comment · Page 1` row text, `Fill existing fields`, `dialog[aria-labelledby="fill-forms-title"]`, the `Name`/`Consent, page 1`/`Preference: Choice A, page 1` labels, and support for comments on the Welcome PDF (`comments.rs` tests use it). Tests: `default-comments-forms-workflow.test.ts` gains a poll-helper case and a case that runs every generated WebDriver script against fake controls shaped like `96b0521`. `default-comments-forms-workflow`, `default-comments-forms-native-evidence` and `verifier-function-imports` pass 20/20; `default-rehearsal-workflow` passes. One `default-step2-oracles` case fails only because PDFium is not set up in this worktree (it copies `src-tauri/resources/pdfium/bin/pdfium.dll`), which is unrelated. Unverified until the rehearsal is rerun.

- Comments/forms rehearsal: sticky-comment controls polled instead of probed once (SM3; scripts and tests only; branch `sm3/comments-rehearsal` off `71c3792`; no app, installer, parity path, version source or workflow change; nothing checked was dropped). Rehearsal run 40548434586 (artifact 12671520323, source `96b0521`) passed the nested expanded page-tools stage and its uninstall, reinstalled, and then failed at `verify-default-comments-forms.ps1:61` with "Exact sticky-comment creation controls were unavailable." This was the first run to reach the comments session at all; every earlier comments/forms run stopped in the expanded stage. Cause, verifier timing: `Open-ReadingUserFile` returns when the native Open dialog closes and does not wait for a rendered page. In `Viewer.tsx` the `img[alt="Page 1"]` appears only after the asynchronous render returns a URL, and `CommentLayer` (`data-testid="comment-layer"`) mounts only after that image's `onLoad`. The single WebDriver call ran before either existed. Not an app regression: `git log 7d61748..96b0521` shows no change to `src/Viewer.tsx`, `src/App.tsx` or `src/Comment*`. Fix: new `Invoke-CommentsCreationControl` polls until the session deadline for all three conditions (page 1 image, comment layer, exactly one enabled button with the exact aria-label), clicks only when all hold, and on timeout throws naming what is missing (`page-image`, `comment-layer`, `enabled-button`); a reply of any other shape is refused. The same applies to "Add area highlight". The pointer placement that follows the click now repeats in a `Wait-WebDriverOracle` until the exact editor textarea appears, because the comment layer reads the creation mode from React state, which may not have re-rendered within the same script as the click. Tests: new case in `default-comments-forms-workflow.test.ts` (poll sequence, timeout message, five malformed replies refused); `default-comments-forms-workflow`, `default-comments-forms-native-evidence` and `verifier-function-imports` 19/19 pass (`--no-file-parallelism`). Unverified until the rehearsal is rerun on the same artifact.

- Uninstall leftover: root cause and fix (SM6; scripts and tests only; branch `sm6/pdfium-uninstall-lock` off `049efb5`; no app, installer, `tauri.conf.json` or workflow change; the cleanup check is not relaxed). Diagnostics run 40535339742 (master `049efb5`, artifact 12671520323) recorded 46 installed entries. After the silent uninstall exactly four remained, all `origin=installed`: `resources`, `resources/pdfium`, `resources/pdfium/bin` and `resources/pdfium/bin/pdfium.dll` (7,227,168 bytes).
