No process was running from the install folder, and the OCR folders were removed. The cause is (b), a lock, and the lock holder was the verifier itself. It is not (a): the default recovery check uses the same installer, uninstalls, and passes cleanup, so `pdfium.dll` is in the uninstaller's delete list. The four page-tool proof helpers (`ImagePagePdfiumRasterProof`, `OrganizerPdfiumProof`, `SignedPdfiumStructuralProof`, `SignedPdfiumPrintProof`) load the installed `pdfium.dll` into the verifier's own pwsh process with `LoadLibraryExW(…, 0x100|0x1000)` and never call `FreeLibrary`. The module stayed mapped for the rest of the run. Tauri's uninstall `Delete` failed on the file in use, so its non-recursive `RMDir` left `bin`, `pdfium` and `resources`, and therefore the install folder. The recovery check never loads PDFium in-process, which is why it passed. The leftover diagnostic missed it because it looked only for processes whose image is under the install folder; the verifier's own process is not. A local run with a real PDFium build reproduced the mechanism: two loads by full path share one handle with a reference count of 2, `Delete` fails while loaded, two `FreeLibrary` calls release it, and the file then deletes. Before running the uninstaller, `Invoke-OwnedCleanup` now calls `Clear-OwnProcessModules`. It finds every module the verifier process has loaded from under the install folder, releases each one with bounded `GetModuleHandleExW` (unchanged refcount) and `FreeLibrary` calls (256 at most), and logs `OWNED_CLEANUP_DIAG phase=released-own-modules modules=… references=… names=… remaining=…` with file names only. It refuses to uninstall if any module remains loaded. The leftover diagnostics also log `phase=leftover-own-modules`. The comments/forms verifier imports both helpers. No uninstaller hook is added: the uninstaller already deletes exactly what it installed, the app's own helper processes share the main executable's name and are covered by Tauri's running-app check, and a hook `Delete` would fail on a locked file just as the template's does. Joshua's ruling (uninstall removes only what it installed plus empty app-owned folders, never a recursive delete) holds without change. Tests load a real PDFium through the actual `ImagePagePdfiumRasterProof` constructor from a temporary install folder twice. They prove the file is locked, that the release frees both references, and that the file then deletes. They also check the diagnostic lines (file names only), the refusal when a module cannot be released, the release-before-uninstall order, that no uninstall runs after a failed release, and the load flags in all four proof helpers. Evidence (source, Windows): full Vitest in the hosted command, 111 files, 714 passed, 1 skipped.

- Uninstall leftover diagnostics: review follow-ups (SM6; scripts and tests only; branch `sm6/uninstall-leftover-diag`, on top of `b9abb83`; the cleanup check is unchanged). Code review P2 1: `Save-OwnedInstallSnapshot` now catches its own failures. The snapshot becomes `$null`, so leftover origins report `unknown`, and one `OWNED_CLEANUP_DIAG phase=snapshot-failed` line is written without the exception text. A diagnostics failure inside the install block can no longer fail the run; this covers both call sites in the expanded and comments/forms verifiers. Code review P2 2: `Get-OwnedInstallEntries` converts each entry separately, so an entry whose path cannot be resolved becomes `<unreadable>` (kind `unknown`, bytes -1) and the rest of the listing survives. SM7 P3 1: enumeration stops after 5,000 entries (validated 1 to 100,000) without walking the remainder, and logs `OWNED_CLEANUP_DIAG phase=enumeration-capped limit=N`. A new test covers a throwing snapshot (no exception text logged, origin `unknown`), an unreadable entry among readable ones, a 1,000-item source capped at 10 with at most 50 items produced, and a listing exactly at the limit. Evidence (source, Windows): targeted Vitest in the hosted command (step 2 oracles, expanded and comments/forms workflows, reachability), 4 files, 36 tests passed.

- Uninstall leftover diagnostics (SM6; scripts and tests only; branch `sm6/uninstall-leftover-diag` off `7087ab5`; diagnostics only, the cleanup check is not relaxed). Rehearsal page tools (40529127179) and comments/forms (40529132572) passed every feature stage, then logged `OWNED_CLEANUP_DIAG phase=uninstall-incomplete waitedMs=180250 remaining=install-folder`. The uninstall registry key was removed, but `%LOCALAPPDATA%\PDF Workstation` still existed after three minutes. Right after installation, before the app first runs, both default verifiers now record the relative names of everything in the install folder (`OWNED_CLEANUP_DIAG phase=installed-snapshot entries=N`). If the install folder outlives the uninstall wait, `Write-OwnedInstallLeftoverDiagnostics` logs a summary and then up to 30 entries. Each entry has its relative name only (at most 120 printable ASCII characters), `file` or `dir`, its size, and an origin: `installed` if the installer placed it, `runtime` if it appeared later. It also logs the names of any process whose image is under the install folder. A fresh install-and-uninstall run is not needed to tell installer leftovers from runtime content: the origin column answers that from the same run. A static review found nothing in the app that writes into its install folder. The app only reads `resource_dir()` (PDFium, the welcome sample, notices, the OCR engine root). Recovery data goes to `app_data_dir()/recovery`, atomic output temp files go next to the user's chosen output, test helpers use `%TEMP%`, and WebView2 data goes to the requested profile or the identifier folder. The OCR package reaches the installer as a Tauri `bundle.resources` map passed with `--config`, so the generated uninstaller does know those files. Whether that uninstaller also removes nested resource parent folders (`resources/ocr/<identity>/…`, `resources/pdfium/bin`) or the install folder recursively could not be confirmed here: the NSIS template is embedded in the Tauri CLI and is not in the local crate cache. The repository has no earlier record of an uninstall leaving a clean folder. The next hosted run will show which. Tests cover installed against runtime origins, relative names with no absolute paths, the 30-entry cap with a truncation count, sanitized process names, diagnostics running only when the install folder remains, and the snapshot hook after installation in both verifiers. Evidence (source, Windows): full Vitest in the hosted command, 111 files, 711 passed, 1 skipped.

- Pre-tag rehearsal fixes, round 8 (SM6; scripts and tests only; branch `sm6/rehearsal-fix-8` off `71dcf12`; no parity path, version source or workflow changed). The signed expanded check passed end to end on artifact 12671520323. The rehearsal page tools (40523055747) and comments/forms (40523060222) passed every feature stage and then failed final cleanup with "Owned installed state remains after cleanup." That message did not say which item remained. The signed expanded verifier never uninstalls; it leaves the disposable runner as it is. The default verifier's uninstall-and-assert-clean path had therefore never run hosted. The settings folder is not the cause: the verifier removes its own seeded `local.pdfworkstation.desktop` after the uninstall, and the silent Tauri uninstall is not expected to remove app data. The likely cause is NSIS behaviour. Without `_?=`, an NSIS `uninstall.exe` copies itself to `%TEMP%` and relaunches, so the process the verifier starts exits 0 almost at once. `Invoke-SilentUninstall` waited only for that stub, and the state check then ran while the real uninstall was still removing the install folder and its registry key. The NSIS template is embedded in the Tauri CLI, so this was not confirmed from source here; the next run's diagnostics will show it. A new `Wait-OwnedUninstallComplete` polls every 500 ms, up to 180 seconds after the stub exits, until both the uninstall registry key and the install folder are gone. It logs `OWNED_CLEANUP_DIAG phase=uninstall-complete waitedMs=…`, or on timeout `phase=uninstall-incomplete … remaining=…`, and fails with the remaining categories. If the uninstaller really leaves the install folder or key behind, that will now show as a named finding instead of a race. `Assert-OwnedCleanupState` now names what remains by category only: `uninstall-registry-key`, `install-folder` or `settings-folder`, never a path. The comments/forms verifier imports the two new helpers. Tests cover the wait (polls until clear, times out naming the category), the cleanup order (stub, polls, then settings removal) and the category messages. They also fix an existing test whose "residual install" rejection had passed only because a helper was not imported. Evidence (source, Windows): full Vitest in the hosted command, 111 files, 710 passed, 1 skipped.

- Pre-tag rehearsal fixes, round 7 (SM6; scripts and tests only; branch `sm6/rehearsal-fix-7` off `faf122d`; no parity path, version source or workflow changed). On artifact 12671520323, page tools (40516618370), comments/forms (40516622727) and signed expanded (40516626764) all failed at `installed-app-launch.ps1:375` with an opaque "(500) Internal Server Error" from `POST /session/{id}/execute/sync`. The app did not crash or hang; this was a verifier bug. No structural Save diagnostic had been logged, so the failure came before the first Combine save. The first new WebDriver script in the structural stage is the Combine dialog wait in `Wait-StructuralToolDialog`. It, and `Set-StructuralDialogInput` (Insert and Replace inputs), built their scripts as `-Script "...querySelector('dialog[aria-labelledby=\"'+id+'\"]')..."`. In PowerShell `\"` is not an escape. The `"` closed the argument, so the browser received JavaScript truncated at `querySelector('dialog[aria-labelledby=\`, a syntax error that WebDriver reports as HTTP 500 "javascript error"; rendering both functions locally reproduces the truncated text. Both scripts now match by attribute in JavaScript with no string-built selector: exactly one `dialog` whose `aria-labelledby` equals the id, and exactly one input inside an open dialog whose `aria-label` equals the label. The rendered scripts parse as valid JavaScript. A tokenizer scan of every `.ps1` found no other double-quoted string ending in a backslash before its closing quote, and a new test keeps it that way. `Invoke-BoundedLoopbackJson` no longer discards WebDriver error bodies. On any HTTP error response it reads the body within the existing byte and time bounds and writes `WEBDRIVER_ERROR_DIAG status=… error=… message=…`. It then throws "WebDriver returned HTTP <status> (<error>): <message>". `ConvertTo-SanitizedWebDriverError` keeps only the W3C error code (lowercase words, otherwise `unrecognized` or `unparsed`) and the first line of the message, with drive, UNC and file-URL paths replaced by `<path>`, user and runner names redacted, non-ASCII removed and 200 characters at most. The stack trace is never kept. Tests cover the sanitizer cases and a real loopback HTTP 500 served by a local Node server, which proves the logged line and thrown message carry the code and a path-free message. Evidence (source, Windows): full Vitest in the hosted command, 111 files, 709 passed, 1 skipped.

- Pre-tag rehearsal fixes, round 6 (SM6; scripts and tests only; branch `sm6/rehearsal-fix-6` off `758710a`; no parity path, version source or workflow changed). With the organizer and split passing on artifact 12671520323, all three page-tool checks (runs 40379633044, 40379637856, 40379643341) failed on entering the never-before-run structural stage: "Installed signed structural page-tools PDFium receipt has an unexpected or missing property." `Assert-StructuralFileReceipt` requires exactly `{bytes, sha256}`. Both expanded verifiers pass the signed PDFium base-resource record, which both receipt builders produce as `{target, bytes, sha256, unsignedBytes, unsignedSha256}` (default: `Assert-DefaultDraftReceipt` and `Assert-DefaultArtifactReceipt`; signed: `Assert-SignedArtifactReceipt`). The image and organizer helpers read only `bytes` and `sha256`, so they never noticed. No field was renamed: the record is richer than the structural helper assumed. A new `Assert-StructuralPdfiumReceipt` checks every field. It requires the exact five-property shape, `target` equal to `resources/pdfium/bin/pdfium.dll`, a positive `unsignedBytes`, an uppercase 64-hex `unsignedSha256`, and an installed path ending in `\resources\pdfium\bin\pdfium.dll`. It then checks the file's bytes and SHA-256 as before. The trusted-signature check stays next to it, and the unsigned values are still proven equivalent at extraction. A static walk of the rest of the structural stage found three more failures that would have come next, all fixed here. (a) and (b): the pinned tauri-driver and EdgeDriver records (`{version, cargoVersion, rustcVersion, bytes, sha256}` and `{version, bytes, sha256, signatureStatus, publisher, hasTimestamp}`) were also passed to the exact two-field check. `Assert-WebDriverReceipt` already validates their full shapes and pins, so the structural helper now checks each driver file against exactly its `bytes` and `sha256`. (c) Both expanded verifiers' final preserved-input loops passed the five-field PDFium record to `Assert-StructuralFileReceipt`; they now use `Assert-StructuralPdfiumReceipt`. The rest of the walk found no other mismatch: application, fixture, sentinel and welcome receipts are `{bytes, sha256}` by construction. The Combine, Insert and Replace selectors, dialog title ids, confirm labels, `Target PDF`/`Donor PDF` selects and numeric inputs match the current dialogs. The structural Save names stay pinned to `main.rs`, the two Open pickers are file pickers (1001/1148), the sentinel is `upgrade-sentinel.json`, and the comments/forms runtime receipts are `{bytes, sha256}` by construction. A new test checks the PDFium record against a real file (it accepts the builders' shape and refuses a bare pair, another target, zero or malformed unsigned values, a changed file, an extra property and a different path), and pins the driver projections and both verifiers' final checks. Evidence (source, Windows): full Vitest in the hosted command, 110 files, 705 passed, 1 skipped.

- Pre-tag rehearsal fixes, round 5 (SM6; scripts and tests only; branch `sm6/rehearsal-fix-5` off `7674af3`; no parity path, version source or workflow changed). On signed artifact 12671520323, image create and export passed for the first time, which exposed the organizer stage. Both failures were in the verifier, not the app; neither stage had run hosted before. (1) Default page tools and comments/forms (runs 40366950116, 40366955428) failed at the organizer's pre-launch check "Organizer profile roots were not fresh, exclusive, and sentinel-only". `verify-default-expanded-page-tools.ps1` wrote `page-tools-sentinel.json` into the settings root, while the organizer and structural helpers (and the signed flow) require exactly `upgrade-sentinel.json`. The default verifier now writes `upgrade-sentinel.json`; the file content is unchanged. (2) Signed expanded (run 40366965369) failed after the 180-second picker timeout with `newOpenCount 1, newFilenameCount 0`. Since 2026-10-16 (`e7fc7ee`, `844733a`), Split uses a real `IFileOpenDialog` folder picker (`FOS_PICKFOLDERS | FOS_FORCEFILESYSTEM | FOS_PATHMUSTEXIST`) and creates a new `Split PDFs` folder inside the chosen folder. A folder picker's "Folder:" box is `Edit` id 1152, a direct child of the dialog; a local rfd 0.15.4 probe confirmed this. The shared picker helper only knew the file-dialog boxes 1001 and 1148, so it found the Select Folder button but no box. The helper now also classifies `edit-1152` and maps it exactly when it revalidates the binding. The organizer split result must be `edit-1152`, while file-open results stay limited to 1001/1148 and `Wait-ReadingPostOpenState` still accepts only those. The split step submits the existing, empty split parent folder and expects `Split PDFs` with the unchanged `pages-0001-0002.pdf` … files inside it. The local probe confirmed that typing an existing folder path into box 1152 and pressing Select Folder returns that folder. Tests pin the sentinel name, the folder-picker classification and the split contract to `split.rs`. Evidence (source, Windows): full Vitest in the hosted command, 110 files, 704 passed, 1 skipped.

- Intermittent native-dialog test failures (SM6; tests only; branch `sm6/print-dialog-regression` off `96b0521`). `installed-print-dialog-workflow.test.ts` › "retains sanitized observed counts when an exact native match is ambiguous" failed in CI 40350977138 on `1c49e95` and in signed artifact build 40358532523 on `96b0521`. It passed in CI 40358529711 on the same `96b0521` and in CI on `8d5d76f`. The cause is the test's own deadline, not the round-3 change. It gave `Wait-ProcessUiElement` a 50 ms wall-clock deadline that starts when the argument is evaluated, and the counts appear only if the wait loop is entered at least once before that deadline. The time to reach the loop includes first-call compilation of the wait and diagnostic functions. Measured locally in fresh pwsh processes, it is 35 to 50 ms with both the old (`8d5d76f`) and new script: a 40 ms budget passed 3 of 5 runs (old) and 1 of 5 (new), and 50 ms passed 4 of 5 for each. With 8 of 16 cores busy, 50 ms passed 0 of 5 and 1000 ms passed 5 of 5. That test now warms the code path with an already-expired deadline and then uses 1000 ms. A scan of the other native-dialog verifier tests found the same shape in several places, each needing at least one observation before a short real-clock deadline: the four descendant-delta checks (50 to 1000 ms), the replaced-HWND mismatch and childless hosted Save receipts (250 to 2000 ms), and the persistent and reused quiescence probes in `installed-app-launch-workflow.test.ts` (20 to 500 ms). Tests that check lateness itself and expect the deadline to win keep their short budgets. No production script changed. Evidence (source, Windows): both affected files pass in the hosted command (`npm test -- --pool=threads --maxWorkers=1 --no-file-parallelism`), also with half the cores busy, and the full suite in that mode passes 110 files, 702 tests, 1 skipped.

- Page-image pixel sizes match the export dialog (SM2; from the 0.3.1 pre-tag rehearsal, SM6; source build; branch `sm2/export-dpi-rounding`). `page_image::dimensions` computed `scale = dpi / 72` first and then `ceil(points * scale)`; 150 / 72 is 2.0833333333333335 in f64, so Letter's 792 pt became 1650.0000000000002 and 1651 px (2550 × 3301 at 300 DPI), while the dialog (`ExportPageImageDialog.tsx`, `Math.ceil(points * dpi / 72)`) promised 1275 × 1650. New `page_image::pixels_at` computes it exactly as the dialog does: the f32 page size is read through its shortest decimal form (the number the dialog receives in JSON) and multiplied before dividing, which is exact for whole-point sizes. It is used for page-image export (PNG and JPEG) and for the 150 DPI raster-redaction plan, which shares `dimensions`. Redacted copies of Letter pages therefore change from 1275 × 1651 to 1275 × 1650 pixels per page; the page size in points is unchanged. Redaction security: the old size did not leave any strip uncovered or unrendered. PDFium renders the whole page into the bitmap at its fixed pixel size, the marks are mapped through that same bitmap size (floor for the top-left, ceil for the bottom-right), and the clean writer places the image over the full page box; new test `edge_marks_reach_the_last_row_and_column_whatever_the_pixel_size` (`src-tauri/tests/raster_redaction.rs`) checks that a mark on the page edge blackens the last row and column at both 1275 × 1651 and 1275 × 1650. Audit of other points-to-pixels conversions: the OCR and searchable-OCR raster (`ocr::raster_dimensions`, 150 DPI) already multiplied first, but from the widened f32 value, so after this fix it could differ by one pixel from a 150 DPI export of a page with a fractional size (the existing service test `ocr_raster_matches_current_print_pixels_and_preserves_worker_state`, on a cropped page, caught it); it now uses `pixels_at` too; print (`printing.rs`) multiplies before dividing; the viewer, thumbnails and helper renders take a pixel width from the frontend, not a DPI conversion; the searchable-OCR containment slack uses integer `div_ceil`; no other divide-first conversion was found in `src-tauri/src` or `src`. Tests: `letter_and_a4_match_the_export_dialog_at_every_dpi` (Letter, A4 and a fractional page at 72, 150 and 300 DPI, PNG and JPEG) and `dimensions_agree_with_the_dialog_formula_on_the_shared_table`, which reads `src/pageImageDimensions.fixture.json` (36 rows: Letter, A4, A3, A5, both orientations, whole, fractional and sub-point sizes at all three DPIs); `src/ExportPageImageDialog.test.tsx` checks the dialog's `pageImageDimensions` against the same table, so Rust and the dialog agree on every row. Evidence (source, Windows, E: build): `ExportPageImageDialog.test.tsx` 8 passed; `raster_redaction` 5, `redaction_adversarial` 38, `searchable_ocr` 18, `searchable_pdf` 10, `searchable_pdfium` 2 passed; parallel `cargo test --bins` 415 passed, 0 failed, 32 ignored (an earlier run also had one `ocr_process` timing test fail, which passed on rerun and alone). Not rerun in the installed app; the rehearsal repro (export page 1 of a Letter PDF at 150 DPI) remains to be repeated there.

- Pre-tag rehearsal fixes, round 3 (SM6; scripts and tests only; branch `sm6/rehearsal-fix-3` off `8d5d76f`; no parity path, version source or workflow changed). (1) Image create (runs 40333238684, 40333242231, 40333249766) logged `suggestion-seen`, `settled`, `typed-stable` and `pre-click name=typed`, yet the hosted dialog still saved the app's suggestion `image-source.pdf`. On Windows Server 2022 the Save dialog commits the name it was given with `SetFileName` and ignores a programmatic `WM_SETTEXT`, even though the box shows the new text. This could not be reproduced locally. On Windows 11 a throwaway rfd 0.15.4 program, driven by the repo's own helpers with the same timing, returned the typed name. Two transports replace it. **Accept the suggestion** (image create and export, Combine, Insert, Replace): the suggestion is deterministic from app source (`<image stem>.pdf`, `<pdf stem>-page-1.png`, `combined.pdf`, `inserted-pages.pdf`, `replaced-pages.pdf`; tests pin these to `main.rs` and `service.rs`). The verifier waits for it, settles, types nothing, reads the dialog's folder from its address breadcrumb, refuses to press Save when the folder is not the expected output folder (logged as `unavailable` when unreadable), re-checks the suggestion right before Save, and binds the expected output path to the suggestion. `Invoke-InstalledImagePageTools` requires the created PDF and exported PNG to be those names in the source image folder. Both expanded verifiers now pass `image-source.pdf`, `image-source-page-1.png`, `combined.pdf`, `inserted-pages.pdf` and `replaced-pages.pdf`. **Keyboard typing** (comments and forms, where the three form saves would all suggest `filled-form.pdf`): the new native `TypeSaveFileNameExact` selects the box, clears it with a backspace and sends each character as `WM_CHAR` through bounded `SendMessageTimeout` calls, refusing control characters. It re-reads the exact value under the same process and topology checks. The print-to-PDF flow, which has no app suggestion and already passes hosted, keeps `WM_SETTEXT`. Locally, accept mode saved `image-source.pdf` with `dialog-folder state=match` and keyboard mode saved `created.pdf`. (2) Recovery (run 40333246113) failed before the app started: the unauthenticated GitHub API request for the pinned PDFium release metadata failed in `prepare-default-draft-pdfium.ps1`. The metadata request and the archive download each run up to 3 times with 0, 5 and 15 second waits, and a partial archive is deleted before a retry. Release identity, asset id, size and digest checks, the exact byte count and the SHA-256 check still run on whatever succeeds. Errors now carry the HTTP status, and each failed attempt logs a `PDFIUM_FETCH_DIAG` line, so a rate limit (403) shows up as such. Evidence (source, Windows): full Vitest 110 files, 701 passed, 1 skipped.

- Pre-tag rehearsal fixes, round 2 (SM6; scripts and tests only; branch `sm6/rehearsal-fix-2` off `d765e64`; no parity path or version source changed). Round 2 on artifact 12545476382 confirmed both round-1 diagnoses and exposed two follow-ups. (1) Image create (runs 40330323271, 40330327063, 40330333934) logged `NATIVE_SAVE_DIAG t=20250 phase=suggestion-missing name=other:image-source`. The app's suggestion did arrive, but the File name box shows it without `.pdf` because Windows hides the extension the filter supplies, and the exact-match wait for `image-source.pdf` timed out. The wait now accepts the suggestion with or without its extension, case-insensitively, and exactly otherwise: `image-source.png`, `image-sources`, `image-source.pdf.pdf` and `image` are refused. The typed name is unchanged. Image export, structural page tools and comments/forms only wait for any non-empty suggestion, so they were not affected. (2) Recovery (run 40330330519) restored page 2, the dirty state and the notice, but the Phase B oracle never saw a rotated `Page 1` image (465×602). The cause was in Phase A. The organizer opens with the current page selected, and since round 1 that is page 2. Phase A clicked "Select page 1" and "Rotate selected pages clockwise" in one script. React 18 had not re-rendered between the two clicks, so the rotate handler used the stale selection and rotated page 2. Round 1 hid this because the current page was then page 1. Phase A now clicks "Select page 1" and waits until exactly that button is pressed before rotating. Its acknowledged-edit oracle also requires the page 1 thumbnail to be landscape and the page 2 thumbnail portrait. Phase B still checks the quarter turn on the `Page 1` image against Phase A's original size. The session budget (360 s) was not the limit: session B spent about 31 s launching and opening, then waited on an oracle that could not pass. Tests cover the hidden-extension and case variants, the near-miss refusals, the selection wait before rotating, the thumbnail checks and the Organizer facts they rely on. Evidence (source, Windows): full Vitest 110 files, 698 passed, 1 skipped.

- Pre-tag rehearsal fixes, round 1 (SM6; scripts and tests only; branch `sm6/rehearsal-fix-1` off `7b408e8`; no parity path or version source changed). The 0.3.1 rehearsal on artifact 12545476382 (run 40053645941) passed the four input-driven signed checks and failed recovery and image create. (1) Recovery (rehearsal run 40056084143): the SM5 cache fix works, and the last observed value shows a rotated 602×465 render. The oracle failed on `page:"1"` because of a verifier bug, not an app bug. Phase A set the page box to "2" and dispatched a synthetic `blur` event, but React 18 handles `onBlur` through `focusout`, so `go()` never ran. The page stayed at index 0, while the uncontrolled input (keyed by page, with `defaultValue`) kept showing the typed "2". The recovery checkpoint therefore recorded page 0, and after Keep the app correctly showed page 1. Phase A now clicks "Next page" (a React click) and waits until the box reads 2 and "Previous page" is enabled, a state-derived signal. The Phase A edit and Phase B Keep oracles also require "Previous page" to be enabled. (2) Image create (rehearsal runs 40056080788 and 40056082463, signed run 40056085769; it has failed the same way on every hosted run since 2026-10-01, so the round-4 premise that it once passed was wrong): the dialog saved `image-source.pdf`, the app's suggestion. The setter types into the right control: the only File name edit (`Edit` id 1001 under the `ComboBox`), which the readback matched. The app passes the dialog's result straight through (`main.rs` `create_pdf_from_image`). A local repro used rfd 0.15.4 with the app's exact pick-then-save sequence and the same `WM_SETTEXT` + `BM_CLICK` messages; it saved the typed name every time, including when typing at once. The same transport also passes hosted in the print dialog's Save flow, which has no app suggestion. The remaining explanation, not yet proven on the runner, is timing. The image Save dialog follows a just-closed Open picker, and the round-4 settle accepted two equal empty reads 250 ms apart, so the name could be typed before rfd's `SetFileName` landed. `Set-BoundNativeSaveFileNameExact` now takes `-AwaitSuggestion` and an optional exact `-SuggestedName`. It waits up to 20 seconds for the app's suggestion before typing, then settles, types and re-checks as before, and it fails closed if the suggestion never appears. `Invoke-BoundNativeSaveButtonExact` takes `-ExpectedFileName` and re-reads the box right before pressing Save. Image create passes the exact suggestion (`<source stem>.pdf`); image export, structural page tools and comments/forms pass `-AwaitSuggestion`. The print flow is unchanged. New `NATIVE_SAVE_DIAG` lines record timing and the name category (empty, suggested, typed, a bare other name, never a path), so the next hosted run will confirm or refute the race. Tests cover a late suggestion, any suggestion, a missing or wrong suggestion, a path-shaped suggestion, the pre-click check, the caller wiring and the recovery navigation. Evidence (source, Windows): full Vitest 110 files, 698 passed, 1 skipped.

- Input-driven signed checks, review fixes (SM6b; scripts, workflow and tests only; branch `sm6b/signed-checks-inputs`; review of `14c7e96..a0c1abf`: code SAFE TO PUSH, SM7 CLEAR). (1) P2: `Assert-SignedReadingSourceParity` in `verify-signed-reading-tools.ps1` returned `signedFeatureBlobsVerified` and `signedApplicationShellBlobVerified` as constant `$true` although the inspected-blob pins they described had been removed. Both fields are gone; `currentFeatureParityVerified` is now computed from a count of blobs that matched, `featureBlobCount` is that count, and `currentApplicationShellMatchesSigned` was already computed. A test asserts the record has exactly those three fields and that the function contains no constant `$true` claim. (2) `ocr-installer-upgrade.yml`: the dispatched checkout is now full history (`fetch-depth: 0`) and the clean-source step checks that `source_revision` exists and is an ancestor of the dispatched master, as the other four workflows do; the signed artifact metadata assert now runs before the second checkout and the PDFium preparation (WebDriver preparation and the clean recheck moved ahead of it so the token still appears only after them, which `installed-app-launch-workflow.test.ts` requires). The generic workflow test checks ancestry for all five, the full-history checkout, and the ordering. (3) `signed-check-inputs.test.ts` removes its temporary repository under `target/` in a `finally`. Verified locally: the nine affected test files, 106 tests. Not run: any of the five workflows hosted.

- Signed installed checks driven by dispatch inputs (SM6b; workflows, verifiers, tests and docs only; branch `sm6b/signed-checks-inputs`, based on SM6's `sm6/pretag-rehearsal` `bdb2260` merged with master `d18f45c`). The five checks (`expanded-page-tools`, `ocr-installer-upgrade`, `persistence`, `print-dialog`, `reading-tools`) were hard-pinned to one signed OCR artifact, so every new build needed a code change; the reading-tools drift test also failed on master once SM3 and SM5 changed `src/bridge.ts` and `service.rs`. Now each workflow takes twelve `workflow_dispatch` inputs (`target_version`, `source_revision`, `artifact_run_id`, `artifact_id`, `artifact_name`, `artifact_bytes`, `artifact_digest`, `installer_name`, `installer_bytes`, `installer_sha256`, `record_bytes`, `record_sha256`), exposes them to scripts only as job-level `env` (no `${{ }}` in any `run:` block; a test enforces it), validates them with the new `scripts/signed-check-inputs.ps1` (`Get-SignedCheckInputs`: plain MAJOR.MINOR.PATCH, lowercase 40-hex revision, `signed-ocr-installer-<revision>`, `sha256:` lowercase digest, installer name matching the version, uppercase hashes, positive decimal numbers) and with SM6's `assert-signed-artifact-metadata.ps1` against the GitHub API, and fails closed before downloading anything. Permissions stay `contents: read` and `actions: read`; no secrets. Verifiers: feature-source blobs and the app-shell blob are resolved at run time with `git rev-parse <source_revision>:<path>` (`Get-SignedSourceBlobs`) and compared with the dispatched checkout exactly as before (persistence and print-dialog already did this); the 16 expanded and 11 reading feature paths stay fixed in the scripts, their blob values are gone. The base-resource check in `Assert-SignedArtifactReceipt` compares the record's base targets with the expansion of the tagged `tauri.conf.json` (`Get-SignedBaseResourceTargets`, on a second checkout of `source_revision` with the pinned PDFium prepared by `prepare-default-draft-pdfium.ps1`), replacing the hard-coded 28; the check also requires the checkout HEAD to equal `source_revision` and the tagged `version` to equal `target_version`. Run-time leak regexes use the input ids. The published 0.2.0 baseline pins stay. `reading-tools-verification.yml` now also passes `-PublisherUiMode HostedNonInteractive`, which `verify-signed-ocr-upgrade.ps1` declares mandatory and the workflow had omitted. Tests: no test compares a hard-coded blob with the working tree; the parity functions take `-CurrentRevision` and the tests use fixed historic commits (`4a0f89c`, `67d2382`, and the parent of `bce0e0a` for persistence) as positive and negative controls; new `signed-check-inputs.test.ts` covers 17 wrong or mixed input cases, the base-resource derivation on a temporary git repository (including a wrong version and a different HEAD), and a generic contract for all five workflows (twelve inputs, ordering, no interpolation into scripts, permissions, no secrets, no old ids). Verified locally: affected files and the new test pass; full vitest 683 passed, 11 failed, 1 skipped, where the 11 failures are the environmental ones seen before (`pdfium-provenance`, `mpl-source-archives`, `ocr-installer`, `default-draft-verification-workflow`: no PDFium or OCR setup, no cargo fetch, Git Bash tar). Not run: any of the five workflows hosted; the new second-checkout and PDFium-preparation steps are unexercised on a runner.

- Pre-tag rehearsal for the default installed checks (SM6; scripts, workflows, tests and docs only; branch `sm6/pretag-rehearsal`; no parity path or version source changed). 0.3.0 was tagged before its installed checks could fail, and the recovery bug that surfaced in Step 2 burned the number. The three default verifiers now also accept `-ProofSource artifact`, so they can run on the signed artifact that `ocr-signed-artifact.yml` builds from the release commit before any tag exists. New `scripts/assert-signed-artifact-metadata.ps1` checks the pinned artifact and its run through the API: id, name `signed-ocr-installer-<commit>`, size, digest, not expired, and a successful `workflow_dispatch` of `ocr-signed-artifact.yml` on `master` at that commit. New `scripts/verify-default-artifact.ps1` takes the downloaded export (exactly the installer and `artifact-verification.json`, bound to commit, installer and publisher) and redoes the Step 1 extraction checks against the candidate source: six versions, a trusted installer signature, the embedded updater key check, 28 base resources, the exact extracted inventory, app identity and signature, the embedded candidate updater key, and the OCR package. It writes `default-artifact-verification.json`, mode `default-artifact-extraction`, with `draft=false`. New `.github/workflows/default-rehearsal-verification.yml` (manual, `master` only, `contents: read` and `actions: read`, no secrets, pinned actions, inputs passed only through `env`) requires the candidate to be an ancestor of `master`. It checks the metadata, downloads by run id and artifact id, prepares PDFium and WebDriver, runs the extraction, then runs the selected check (`expanded-page-tools`, `comments-forms` or `recovery`). It uploads only the sanitized `*-rehearsal.json` records. In artifact mode the verifiers skip only the draft-specific parts: the tag, release id, updater `.sig` and `latest.json`, Step 1 artifact metadata and the independent draft re-download. Installation, the installed inventory, source parity, page tools, comments and forms, recovery phases A to C, cleanup and the leak regexes run unchanged. They write separate `*-rehearsal.json` records whose scope names the rehearsal and that carry no `step1` or `draft` block. `Assert-ProofSourceParameters` fails closed when a run is missing inputs for its mode or mixes draft and artifact inputs. The draft path keeps all its checks and record bytes, and the three draft workflows still pass every draft input with the default `draft` mode. `scripts/verifier-reachability.ps1` now counts functions defined inside an imported helper (it reported the nested `Complete-Entry` as missing), and its test covers the rehearsal workflow. New `scripts/default-rehearsal-workflow.test.ts` covers the workflow shape and order, mode mixing in all three real verifiers, receipt binding and 28 mutations, export binding, the installer re-hash and API metadata mismatches. `docs/releases.md` step 4 documents the rehearsal.
- Version 0.3.1 prepared (SM6; branch `sm6/release-0.3.1`; rebased onto master `d18f45c`, which carries the SM5 recovery-cache fix, the SM6b signed-checks re-pin and the SM3 multi-select and editable-combo forms work; nothing tagged, built or signed). The maintainer ruled that the 0.3.0 draft is not published: `v0.3.0` was tagged on `7d61748` and its signed draft built on 2026-10-23, but the installed recovery check found that a page rendered behind the recovery offer stayed unrotated after "Keep recovered edits" (a render cache the keep path did not clear). Tags are never moved or reused, so 0.3.1 is the first public update after 0.2.0. All six version sources now read 0.3.1 (`npm install --package-lock-only --ignore-scripts`, `cargo update -p pdf-workstation --offline`; only those lines moved; `assert-release-version.ps1 -Version 0.3.1` passes); notices regenerated (368 packages, 0 review items; `inventory.json` input hashes and version only). `docs/release-notes.md` is one `## 0.3.1` section: the 0.3.0 content plus the new Fill forms controls (dropdowns, editable combo boxes with typed or listed values, list boxes with one or several selections), same source-build wording and required test phrases, 2,536 bytes, LF. `docs/releases.md`: the key rotation now names 0.3.1 throughout (what each version trusts, the order, the swap after publishing, the note for 0.2.0 installs that skip it), records that 0.3.0 was tagged and drafted but withdrawn so its number is used up, and the OCR section says the first published installer with OCR is 0.3.1; README and `docs/features.md` forward-looking OCR notes say 0.3.1. `scripts/updater-signing-key.json` is unchanged (signed by the old key `85970C34AEABD775`, the old key forbidden as the embedded key); `assert-updater-signing-key.ps1` on this source prints `EMBEDDED_KEY_ID 3E6DD54916A4E5E8`. Literal 0.3.0 values left in `update_commands.rs`, `update_attempt.rs`, `updateAttempt.test.ts`, `updateCommands.test.ts` and `signed-ocr-infrastructure.test.ts` are synthetic fixtures that never read the repository version. Evidence (source, Windows): full Vitest 108 files, 672 passed, 1 skipped before the rebase; after the rebase onto `d18f45c` 108 files, 674 passed, 1 skipped, 1 failed (the SM6b reading-tools negative control, which fails the same way on `d18f45c` itself because SM5 and SM3 moved the pinned `service.rs` and `bridge.ts` blobs).

- Save-dialog filename settling in the installed-app verifiers (SM6; scripts and tests only; branch `sm6/step2-verifier-fix-4`). Step 2 round 4 (runs 40008414416 and 40008418350) showed through the new diagnostics that the app converted the image and saved it as `image-source.pdf`, the name it suggests in the Save dialog, instead of the typed `created.pdf`, with no error shown. The shared `Set-BoundNativeSaveFileNameExact` (in `installed-print-dialog.ps1`, also used by the image, structural page-tool, comments-and-forms and print verifiers) set the filename edit with `WM_SETTEXT` and read it back once, immediately. The same code passed against 0.2.6, which points to a timing race: the dialog applies its own suggested name after the typed one. The setter now waits until the dialog's own filename has settled (two equal reads 250 ms apart, at most 3 seconds), types the filename, and requires it to read back unchanged twice more 250 ms apart before the Save button is pressed. If the dialog replaces it, it types it again, up to four times, and otherwise fails with "Native save filename did not stay set". A new bounded `ReadSaveFileNameBounded` native helper reads the edit under the same process and topology checks as the existing ones. Tests: the existing real-window native save fixture passes with the settling logic; a new provider-driven test covers a settled dialog (one write), a dialog that replaces the name once (two writes) and one that always replaces it (four writes, then refused). The recovery check in the same round failed because the restored page renders unrotated; that is reported separately as an application issue and is not changed here. Evidence (source, Windows): full Vitest 108 files, 672 passed, 1 skipped.
- Recovery render cache fix (SM5; `src-tauri/src/service.rs`, the native recovery gate and its pin; branch `sm5/recovery-cache-fix`). Found by the 0.3.0 installed recovery check and reproduced by SM6 in a source build: open a PDF with recovery on, rotate page 1, close, reopen (the recovery offer shows), render page 1 at 602 px (602 × 779), choose Keep recovered edits (the document now reports page 1 as 792 × 612), render page 1 at 602 px again: still 602 × 779, the unrotated bitmap. Without the render during the offer the result is 602 × 465. Cause: the viewer render cache was keyed by (document id, page, width) only. Every edit path clears the document's cache entries, but `KeepRecoveredEdits` replaces the session's content (revision 0 to the recovered revision) without clearing them. Fix, two independent layers: (1) Keep now clears the document's render cache and its cached annotated document after a successful restore, as the edit paths do; (2) the cache key now includes the session revision, so an entry made at one revision can never be served at another, whichever path changes the content. Revisions only move forward for a given document id (every edit, undo and redo bumps it; Keep moves 0 to the recovered revision, at least 1, and edits are refused while an offer is pending), so a revision names one exact content. Audit of the other paths that touch a session outside the normal edit commands: Open original and Discard only write a tombstone and leave the session unchanged; Restore recovery opens a new document id; checkpointing does not change content; a helper-engine restart reloads the same session and renders identical pixels, and the cache lives in the app's worker, not the engine, so the in-process engine and the debug-only helper share the same behaviour. The cached annotated document was already revision-checked. Page labels come from the source PDF and are not affected. New ignored native test `keep_recovered_edits_never_serves_a_render_cached_during_the_offer` repeats SM6's sequence exactly (with a checkpointed current page of index 1) and asserts Keep returns the recovered revision and current page 1 and that page 1 then renders 602 wide and landscape. It fails without the fix with SM6's exact 602 × 779, passes with either layer alone, and passes with the helper engine on (`SMACROBAT_PDF_ENGINE_HELPER=1`). It is added to `scripts/run-native-recovery-gates.ps1` and pinned in `release-workflow.test.ts`. SM6's second observation (the page box showed 1 after Keep while the offer's page was index 1): the backend returns the offered page (asserted in the new test) and the app sets page index 1, shown as 2, on Keep (`App.recovery.test.tsx`); nothing in the cache bug or the backend explains a 1, and the source build was not run with the app window, so it remains unverified until the next installed recovery run, which requires the page box to read 2.

- Step 2 installed-app verifier fixes, third round (SM6; scripts and tests only; branch `sm6/step2-verifier-fix-3`; no parity path or version source changed). The runs uploaded no artifacts on failure, so the analysis used the logs and the source. (1) Recovery (run 39979955448) reached "Recovery B rotated render" and timed out. Root cause, in the verifier: the check required the restored page image's pixel width to equal Phase A's pixel height and vice versa, which holds only at a fixed zoom. The app opens in its default fit-width mode, where the viewer scales every page to the widest page (`(window width - 144) / widest page width`); after page 1 is rotated it becomes the widest page, so its rendered width stays the same and only its height changes. The rotation is applied; the check could never pass in this layout. The check now requires the orientation to flip and the aspect ratio to match the quarter-turned original within 2 %, which holds at fit-width and at any fixed zoom, and refuses unrotated or wrongly shaped renders. Failed recovery checks now append the last value they observed (booleans, page number, image size), so a future failure shows what the app displayed. (2) Expanded page tools (run 39979945215) and comments and forms (run 39979950586) failed with "An image page-tools output did not become stable before its bounded deadline". From the timing it is the created PDF: the wait started about a minute after launch and ran its full 60 seconds. The native flow before it reported the source picker submitted and the Save dialog closed with the exact expected file name. The root cause is not determinable from the logs: nothing was captured about the app's state or the folder. In a source build, the app's image-to-PDF conversion (`image_pdf::prepare_and_write`) converted the verifier's exact generated PNG (64 × 96, with `sRGB`, `gAMA` and `pHYs` chunks) to a valid 898-byte PDF, so the conversion itself is not at fault; the app writes the file with a temporary file renamed into place in the same folder. A timeout now logs `IMAGE_PAGE_DIAG` lines (whether the expected file exists and its size, the names and sizes of the files in that folder, and the open dialogs' headings, alerts and status texts) before rethrowing the unchanged error, so the next run shows whether the app reported an error, wrote under another name such as the suggested `image-source.pdf`, or wrote nothing. Tests: new `default-step2-oracles.test.ts` (quarter-turn check at fit-width, fixed zoom and with rounding, refusals; last-observed reporting with caller variables passed through; diagnostics for a missing output); `default-recovery-workflow.test.ts` pins the new check. Evidence (source, Windows): full Vitest 108 files, 671 passed, 1 skipped.
- Signed-check re-pin, review fixes (SM6b; tests and docs only; branch `sm6b/signed-checks-repin`; review of `35e35fe..2f53a16`: SM7 CLEAR, code review FIX FIRST on two P2s). (1) `signed-reading-tools-workflow.test.ts` titled a test "detects drift" that no longer could, since the current source now equals the signed source. A new test runs `Assert-SignedReadingSourceParity` from the real verifier against real git: unmodified pins pass, a zeroed `src/Viewer.tsx` feature pin throws the feature-blob error, and a zeroed `SignedApplicationShellBlob` throws the shell-blob error. (2) `docs/releases.md` (two paragraphs on the upgrade and launch checks) and `docs/ocr-feasibility.md` now name run 39969839829, artifact 12510717103 and source `4a0f89c` (installed version 0.3.0), keeping run 36499724415 / `67d2382` as history. (3) P3: `docs/license-inventory.md` and `docs/ocr-feasibility.md` note the new pin next to the historical run. No workflow or verifier values changed.

- Signed installed checks re-pinned to a signed 0.3.0 OCR artifact (SM6b; workflows, verifiers and tests only; branch `sm6b/signed-checks-repin`). The five checks (`expanded-page-tools`, `ocr-installer-upgrade`, `persistence`, `print-dialog`, `reading-tools`) were hard-pinned to artifact 11005152678 (run 36499724415, source 67d2382, 0.2.6) and so said nothing about current source; the 0.3.0 release was published without them (Joshua, 2026-10-23). New pins: `ocr-signed-artifact.yml` run 39969839829 on master `4a0f89c` (app source identical to the 0.3.0 tag `7d61748`), artifact 12510717103 `signed-ocr-installer-4a0f89c5...`, 11,182,480 bytes, digest sha256:3e5c1003...a689; installer `PDF Workstation_0.3.0_x64-setup.exe` 11,201,296 bytes, SHA-256 3DE99F11...FE5; `artifact-verification.json` 7,715 bytes, SHA-256 0383DAE8...5C (both hashed locally from the downloaded artifact). Verifiers expect version 0.3.0 and 28 base resources (were 26). Feature and app-shell git blobs for expanded-page-tools (16 files) and reading-tools (11 files) were regenerated with `git rev-parse 4a0f89c:<path>`; 10 of the 16 and 8 of the 11 had changed since 67d2382, and the app shell `src/App.tsx` is now 2af97705. Tests: the "current source moved" drift checks assumed source had moved past the signed revision; they now inject a wrong pin (expanded-page-tools) or only assert the pins match the signed revision (reading-tools); the OCR upgrade receipt fixture builds 28 base resources. Record field `licensesArePackagedSidecarsNotNoticeDialogContent` is deliberately unchanged (SM0 ruling). Persistence, print-dialog, reading-tools and expanded-page-tools source-binding functions were also run against real git at the new revision and pass (2 / 8 / 11 / 16 blobs, 18 selector markers). Verified locally: the 7 affected vitest files; full vitest 656 passed / 11 failed / 1 skipped, the 11 failures (pdfium-provenance, mpl-source-archives, ocr-installer, default-draft-verification) are environmental in this worktree (no PDFium/OCR setup, no cargo fetch, Git Bash tar) and the three files checked also fail on unmodified `7d61748`. Not run: any of the five workflows hosted. No pushes or dispatches.

- Step 2 installed-app verifier fixes, second round (SM6; scripts and tests only; branch `sm6/step2-verifier-fix-2`; no parity path or version source changed). (1) Expanded page tools (run 39969829122) and comments and forms (run 39969832869) failed in `installed-image-page-tools.ps1` with "The term 'Wait-ImagePageWebDriverOracle' is not recognized", although that function is defined in the same dot-sourced file. Cause: the session callback was built with `.GetNewClosure()`. A closure is bound to a new dynamic module that sees global functions but not the script-scope functions of a verifier that GitHub's runner starts as `./scripts/….ps1` from its own dot-sourced step script. Started with `pwsh -File`, as local tests do, the same closure works, which is why no local test caught it; a new test reproduces both behaviours. The closure in `Invoke-RealInstalledImagePageTools` and the four in `verify-default-comments-forms.ps1` (comments create, comments reopen, form write, form read) are now plain scriptblocks. They read their values (`$CreatedPdfPath`, `$ExportedPngPath`, `$PdfiumPath`, `$SourceImagePath`; `$commentSource`, `$commentOutput`, `$fixture`, `$output`) through normal dynamic scope; `Invoke-FreshInstalledImagePageToolsSession`, which invokes them synchronously, defines none of those names, so nothing shadows them. (2) New `scripts/verifier-reachability.ps1` and a rewritten `verifier-function-imports.test.ts`. Starting from every script the four default verifier workflows run, it follows dot-sourcing and `& (Join-Path $PSScriptRoot …)` child scripts transitively (seven entry scripts today, including `verify-default-draft.ps1`, `assert-updater-signing-key.ps1`, `setup-installed-app-webdriver.ps1` and `prepare-default-draft-pdfium.ps1`). It checks every call in every reachable file and in every helper imported by name against what that script context defines, dot-sources or imports, and flags any `.GetNewClosure()` scriptblock that calls repository functions. After these fixes it reports nothing; negative controls (a removed helper import, a closure calling a helper) are reported. (3) Recovery (run 39969836498) failed with "WebDriver response headers exceeded their deadline" from `installed-app-launch.ps1` about 51 seconds into the step, which means a WebDriver request inside a session got no response within its 10-second limit. The log has no stage information, so the request cannot be identified, and nothing in the logs points to an application fault. The verifier now writes `RECOVERY_STAGE` lines (session launch, each step of opening the fixture, every oracle by name, and the intentional termination) so the next run shows where it stops. A related latent fault was found and fixed: Phase A deliberately kills the application inside its session, after which the shared session helper's WebDriver session delete cannot succeed, and the strict cleanup check would always fail. `Invoke-FreshInstalledImagePageToolsSession` now accepts a failed session delete only when the callback result reports `abruptTermination = $true`; the recorded `sessionDeleted` stays truthful, and every other cleanup check (driver exit, no residual processes, captured process outcomes) stays strict. (4) Static review of the rest of the flows: every environment variable the reachable scripts read is a GitHub default (`RUNNER_TEMP`, `GITHUB_*`, `RUNNER_*`, `ImageOS`, `LOCALAPPDATA`, `ProgramFiles`), is set by the workflow step (`GITHUB_TOKEN`, `CARGO_HOME`), or is set internally for a child process; the driver, receipt and source paths (`tauri-driver-install/bin/tauri-driver.exe`, `edge-driver/msedgedriver.exe`, `webdriver-receipt.json`, `target-source`, the Step 1 proof root) match what the setup steps create; the GitHub token is cleared only after the last child run that needs it. Evidence (source, Windows): `verifier-function-imports` (7), `default-recovery-workflow`, `default-comments-forms-workflow`, `default-expanded-page-tools-workflow`, `installed-image-page-tools-workflow` and `installed-app-launch-workflow` pass; full Vitest 107 files, 667 passed, 1 skipped. The page-tool, comments-and-forms and recovery flows inside the installed app have still never run hosted.

- Step 2 installed-app verifier fixes (SM6; scripts and tests only; branch `sm6/step2-verifier-fix`; no parity path or version source changed). The first hosted Step 2 runs against the 0.3.0 draft all failed after Step 1 passed. (1) Expanded page tools (run 39966694409) and comments and forms (run 39966698262) stopped right after installing in `Get-InstallFacts`: "The term 'Convert-RegistryFilePath' is not recognized". Both verifiers import `Get-InstallFacts` from `verify-signed-ocr-upgrade.ps1` one function at a time but never imported the helper it calls; the recovery verifier already did. These two verifiers had never run past installation on a hosted runner. Both now import `Convert-RegistryFilePath`. The comments-and-forms verifier also imported `Invoke-OwnedCleanup` without the `Invoke-SilentUninstall` it calls, which would have failed at cleanup; it now imports that too. (2) Recovery (run 39966701887) stopped at "Exact Open control was unavailable." Its click script required exactly one enabled button whose text is "Open a file", but on a fresh profile with no recent files the app shows two: the toolbar button and the empty-state button. The reading-tools verifier already scopes the click to the toolbar (the button whose parent also holds "Toggle theme"); the recovery verifier now uses the same scope and first waits until exactly one such control is present before taking its UI baselines. (3) New `scripts/verifier-function-imports.test.ts` parses the three default installed-app verifiers, everything they dot-source and every helper they import, and fails if a repository function called by the verifier or by an imported helper is neither defined, dot-sourced nor imported. Before the fix it reported exactly the two `Convert-RegistryFilePath` gaps and the `Invoke-SilentUninstall` gap; a negative control removes the import again and expects the report. The same file pins the scoped Open selector against the toolbar markup in `App.tsx`, and `default-recovery-workflow.test.ts` now expects the toolbar wait as the first call of `Open-RecoveryFixture`. Evidence (source, Windows): the four verifier test files pass (25 tests); full Vitest 107 files, 664 passed, 1 skipped. What the verifiers do after these points (the page tools, comments and forms, and recovery phases in the installed app) has still never run hosted.
- Signed OCR artifact workflow: full-history checkout (SM6b; workflow and test only; branch `sm6b/signed-artifact-fetch-depth`). Dispatch run 39967979362 on `7d61748` failed its vitest step (658 passed, 3 failed): `signed-expanded-page-tools-workflow`, `signed-persistence-workflow` and `signed-reading-tools-workflow` read pinned commit 67d2382 from git and the shallow checkout (`fetch-depth: 1`) lacked it. No artifact was produced. `ocr-signed-artifact.yml` now uses `fetch-depth: 0` with `persist-credentials: false`, the same fix `ci.yml` got in `b42db37`; `signed-ocr-artifact-workflow.test.ts` asserts the checkout block. Nothing else in the workflow depends on a shallow clone (`git rev-parse HEAD` and `git diff` work on full history). Other workflows: all that run vitest already use `fetch-depth: 0`; `ocr-installer-upgrade.yml` and `hosted-npm-diagnostic.yml` keep `fetch-depth: 1` and run no vitest.

- New updater public key in 0.3.0 (SM6; config and tests; branch `sm6/key-rotation`). The maintainer generated a new password-protected updater key offline; only its public key entered the repository. `src-tauri/tauri.conf.json` `plugins.updater.pubkey` is now that key (minisign key id `3E6DD54916A4E5E8`), so 0.3.0 and later trust only it. `scripts/updater-signing-key.json` is unchanged: 0.3.0 must still be signed with the old key `85970C34AEABD775`, which 0.2.0 trusts, and must not embed it. The `plugins.updater` pin in `release-workflow.test.ts` and a new key-id assertion in `updater-signing-key.test.ts` pin the new key. Evidence (source, Windows): `assert-updater-signing-key.ps1` on this source prints `UPDATER_SIGNING_KEY_ID 85970C34AEABD775 EMBEDDED_KEY_ID 3E6DD54916A4E5E8` and exits 0, without a signature and with the published 0.2.0 signature (made with the old key). `updater-signing-key`, `release-workflow` and `default-draft-verification-workflow` pass; full Vitest 106 files, 661 passed, 1 skipped; `cargo test --locked --bins` 406 passed, 0 failed, 31 ignored. The new key's private part and password go into the `release` environment only after 0.3.0 is published and the in-app update check passes (see `docs/releases.md`, Updater key rotation in 0.3.0).

- Updater key rotation follow-up (SM6; docs, workflow, scripts and tests; branch `sm6/key-rotation`; `tauri.conf.json` still unchanged, the new public key is pending). (1) Security review B-1: four places still gave pre-rotation instructions that would have stranded 0.2.0 users or used up the tag: `docs/releases.md` told the maintainer to store the updater key as a `release` environment secret, said the signing secrets had to be moved into the environment before the next tag, listed `TAURI_SIGNING_PRIVATE_KEY` among the environment secrets, and release step 5 required the repository-level key to be deleted; `release.yml`'s validation error told the maintainer to add the key to the environment. All now say that for 0.3.0 the old key stays repository-level and no same-named environment secret may exist until the swap after the in-app update check; the error message points to `scripts/updater-signing-key.json` and the rotation section. (2) Key ids are printed in minisign's display order (`85970C34AEABD775`), not byte order. (3) `scripts/updater-signing-key.json` gains `forbiddenEmbeddedPublicKey` (the old key for 0.3.0). `assert-updater-signing-key.ps1` refuses a `tauri.conf.json` that still embeds it, and `release.yml` runs that check in a new secret-free step right after tag validation, before npm, tests or any signing, so a 0.3.0 tag without the new key cannot build or sign. The swap procedure in `docs/releases.md` sets the signing key to the new key and the forbidden key to `null` or leaves the old key there. (4) New `scripts/verify-updater-signature.mjs` verifies the Ed25519 minisign signature with Node's crypto (BLAKE2b-512 prehashed file signature plus the global signature over the trusted comment) against the signing key; `release.yml` runs it on the built installer before upload. Tests (`updater-signing-key.test.ts`, 4): the pin is the key inside the published 0.2.0 signature and that signature's trusted comment verifies against it; a changed key id or edited trusted comment is refused; a file signed with a throwaway key made by the Tauri CLI verifies, and changed bytes or the wrong key are refused; the embedded-key check accepts a new key, refuses the old one, and accepts it again once the forbidden entry is `null`; the early step comes before `npm ci` and the signature checks before the artifact upload. Evidence (source, Windows): those tests plus `release-workflow`, `default-draft-verification-workflow` and `workflow-action-pins` pass; `tsc -b` passes; full Vitest in the commit message.

- Updater key rotation, staging (SM6; scripts, workflow check, tests and docs; branch `sm6/key-rotation`; the new public key is not in this commit and `tauri.conf.json` is unchanged). The 0.2.0 updater key has no backup, so the maintainer chose to rotate inside 0.3.0: 0.3.0 is signed with the old key and trusts a new one. Audit of what ties the signature to the configured key: `release.yml` and its publish job check only the `.sig` trusted comment and the `latest.json` binding; `release-manifest.mjs` copies the signature; `verify-default-draft.ps1` binds the signature text but did no key check; the other default verifiers and `verify-in-app-update.ps1` check versions, publisher signatures and file receipts, not the updater key; the Rust tests parse `PUBLISHED_0_2_0_SIGNATURE`'s trusted comment and never verify it against `tauri.conf.json`; `release-workflow.test.ts` pins `plugins.updater` including the old public key. So nothing breaks when the signing key and the configured key differ, and nothing checked which key signed a release. New `scripts/updater-signing-key.json` names the key that must sign the release being built (for 0.3.0 the old key, `85970C34AEABD775`), and new `scripts/assert-updater-signing-key.ps1` compares the minisign key id in a `.sig` with it. `release.yml` runs it before uploading assets (no secret involved); `verify-default-draft.ps1` runs it on the downloaded draft and also requires the packaged application to embed the tagged `plugins.updater.pubkey`, recording two new verification flags. New `updater-signing-key.test.ts`: the pin equals the key id inside the published 0.2.0 signature; the script accepts that signature and refuses one with a changed key id, malformed input and a malformed pin file; both call sites are pinned. `docs/releases.md` gains "Updater key rotation in 0.3.0": what each installed version trusts, the order (new public key in before the tag, release with the repository-level old key, no `release` environment key until the in-app update check has passed, then one reviewed swap commit that moves the pin to the new key and makes `release.yml` pass the new key's password secret), and the consequence for 0.2.0 installs that skip 0.3.0 (the updater offers only the latest release, a release carries one signature and the plugin trusts one key, so such installs must install manually). Evidence (source, Windows): with two throwaway keys (deleted afterwards), a local `tauri build` signed with one key while `plugins.updater.pubkey` named the other printed Tauri's "if you are not rotating keys" warning and still wrote the `.sig`; the executable embedded the configured key and not the old one; the assert script accepted the `.sig` against its signing key and refused it against the configured key. Tests: `updater-signing-key`, `default-draft-verification-workflow`, `release-workflow`, `workflow-action-pins` pass (32).

- Default draft verifiers: 28 base resources, not 26 (SM6; scripts and tests only; branch `sm6/preflight-fix`). Found while checking the pre-flight fix with a real local unsigned installer build, whose receipt listed 28 base resources. `1ab8196` added `resources/pdfium/VERSION` and `resources/pdfium/args.gn` to the bundle, but `verify-default-draft.ps1` still required exactly 26 base resources from the tagged source and `Assert-DefaultDraftReceipt` (shared by the expanded page-tools, comments-and-forms and recovery checks) exactly 26 in the Step 1 receipt, so release step 8 would have failed on the 0.3.0 draft. Both now pin 28; `verify-signed-ocr-upgrade.ps1` keeps 26 because it checks the older signed artifact from `67d2382`, which had 26. `default-draft-verification-workflow.test.ts` now also computes the live base resource map from `tauri.conf.json` and fails if it is not 28, so the next bundle change has to update the pin. Evidence (source, Windows): the four default verifier test files pass (27 tests); a local `build-installer.ps1 -UnsignedLocal` build at `2f4ccf4` produced an installer whose extraction proof matched 28 base resources and, with the Cargo.toml restore, left the tracked tree clean (`git diff` and `git diff --cached` both exit 0; the script logged that it restored the Tauri rewrite).

- Pre-flight and CI fixes before the 0.3.0 tag (SM6; build script and tests only; branch `sm6/preflight-fix`; no parity path or version source changed). (1) Hosted `windows-validation.yml` on Windows 2022 (run 39669577207) passed every step and then failed "Recheck tracked source". Cause, reproduced locally: the Tauri CLI rewrites `src-tauri/Cargo.toml` at the start of `tauri build`, re-serialising the `windows` crate feature array (`"Win32_System_ErrorReporting","Win32_System_IO"` gains a space after the comma) and writing LF line endings. The change is whitespace only, but it is a tracked-file change, made inside `build-installer.ps1`'s Tauri build. `release.yml` checks the clean tree before that build (inside `build-installer.ps1`) and has no recheck after it, so the tag run would not have failed on it, but its source tree would have been dirty after the build. `build-installer.ps1` now snapshots `Cargo.toml` before the Tauri build and, in a `finally`, restores the exact original bytes; if the rewrite changed anything other than whitespace it still restores the file and then fails, because the build would not match its source. Fixing the missing space in `Cargo.toml` itself would avoid the rewrite, but `Cargo.toml` is a version source under the release freeze. A new `signed-ocr-infrastructure` test runs the restore function on the observed rewrite (restored byte-exact), an unchanged file, and a semantic rewrite (restored, then refused), and pins the try/finally around the build. (2) CI run 39669567778 failed two tests that this branch's earlier work did not touch and that pass locally in about 1 s each. `installed-print-dialog-workflow`: the positive harness calls gave fake UI enumeration a 1-second wall-clock deadline, and the first UI Automation call in a fresh PowerShell process loads its assemblies, which took longer on the hosted runner; the 12 positive-path deadlines (none involves a deliberate delay) are now 30 seconds, while the delayed-query rejection case keeps its 250 ms deadline against a 350 ms fake delay. `signed-ocr-upgrade-workflow`: "rejects untrusted runner state…" runs one trivial Windows PowerShell 5.1 process but timed out at 30 s on the runner (35.3 s); the file's own spawn bound is 45 s, so its five explicit test timeouts (30 s and 15 s) are now 60 s, above that bound. Evidence (source, Windows): the restore was reproduced against a real `tauri build` start (the same one-line diff, then `git checkout`); the five affected test files pass (48 tests).

- 0.3.0 release notes and README trim (SM0; docs only). `docs/release-notes.md`, the text the update manifest carries, now lists the Fill-forms additions merged for 0.3.0: single-line text including accented Latin letters, and single-choice lists including scrolling list boxes (SM3 `3df0a8c..afdc0b6`). The README's Recent changes section is trimmed to the newest 15 one-line summaries; every removed line's full entry stays in this file.

- Removed-page references in page-edit and Replace copies (SM2; SM7 F-1 LOW and two P3s; source build; branch `sm2/save-copy-prune`). New `editor::remove_page_references` replaces the plain nulling in structural exports: a `/Dest` or a GoTo `/A` (inline or indirect) whose destination targets a removed page is dropped instead of being left as `[null ...]`, a link annotation left with neither is dropped, nulls left in `/Annots` arrays are removed, and every other reference to a removed page or old page-tree node becomes null before pruning. Replace Pages (`combine::assemble_sequence`) now applies the same function to the replaced pages and to both sources' old page-tree nodes before its prune. That step is defence in depth: Replace's existing `supported_shape` guard already refuses custom catalog and page keys and any resource or metadata reference into the page tree, so no supported input can reference a replaced page; the new test `replaced_pages_referenced_from_kept_pages_or_the_catalog_are_refused_or_leave_no_trace` pins those three refusals and checks that an accepted replacement leaves no trace of the replaced page, no extra page object and a single page-tree node. Because the guard blocks every such input, switching off the Replace step does not fail a test; switching off the link handling fails `removed_pages_referenced_from_kept_pages_or_the_catalog_do_not_survive_export`, which now also covers links by `/Dest`, inline and indirect GoTo actions, a link to the kept page (kept, destination intact) and a non-link dictionary (dead `/Dest` dropped, dictionary kept). Links can reach a structural export only through such custom references, because deleting or extracting pages with annotations other than app-owned comments is refused. The 1,500-page export timing bound is tightened from 20 s to 5 s (measured about 130 ms in a debug build). Evidence (source, Windows, E: build): `cargo test --bins -- editor:: replace` 41 passed; parallel `cargo test --bins` 395 passed, 0 failed, 31 ignored.

- Page-edit copies drop unreachable source objects (SM2; fixes SM7 L-1, LOW privacy; source build; branch `sm2/save-copy-prune`). `EditSession::export` (Save a copy, extract, split parts) pruned only after structural edits, and `EditSession::note_source` (the comment-overlay source used for the viewer, print, export and redaction renders) never pruned, so a copy after rotate, crop or comments alone carried every object lopdf loaded from the source, reachable or not: the content superseded by an incremental update, unreferenced objects, and freed objects reloaded from an object stream. Both now call `pdf_load::prune_unreachable` (the existing linear trailer walk, not lopdf's quadratic `prune_objects`) before saving, for every edit kind. For structural exports, references to removed pages and to the old page-tree nodes (whose Kids list every source page) are first replaced with null wherever they appear (a custom key on a kept page, the catalog, the trailer), so such a reference can no longer keep a removed page alive. Tests in `src-tauri/src/editor.rs`: `copies_never_carry_unreachable_source_objects` builds a two-page source with an unreferenced marker object and a superseded content stream, shows a plain lopdf load and save keeps both markers (positive control), then checks rotate, crop, comment and move copies (`export` and `note_source`) carry neither marker in raw or decompressed bytes and keep every page's content unchanged; `removed_pages_referenced_from_kept_pages_or_the_catalog_do_not_survive_export` deletes a page referenced from a kept page and the catalog and checks its content and page object are gone; `pruned_export_of_the_1500_page_fixture_stays_fast` (rotate-only export of `test-corpus/synthetic-text-1500.pdf`: 130 ms in a debug build). Disabling either change makes the matching test fail. Evidence (source, Windows, E: build): parallel `cargo test --bins` 394 passed, 0 failed, 31 ignored; `--test recovery_journal --test dialog_fixtures` 7 passed, 1 ignored; no installed app or real-world documents.
- Release 0.3.0 review follow-ups (SM6; build script, workflow, tests and docs; branch `sm6/release-0.3.0`; never run hosted). (1) `build-installer.ps1` extracted the installer for its proof with a plain `7z x`. The hosted 7-Zip 26.03 lists the NSIS-generated root `uninstall.exe` as an extra entry, which the draft verifier already had to exclude; a non-zero exit there would fail the release run after signing and use up the version number. Extraction arguments now come from `Get-InstallerExtractArguments`, which adds `-x!uninstall.exe` when the archive inventory has a root `uninstall.exe` (a nested one does not trigger it); nothing in the proof needs that file. (2) The release-mode single-signature check and the staging copy moved into `Assert-ReleaseOcrBundle` and `Copy-ReleaseOcrAssets` (behaviour unchanged, plus a final check that the release folder holds exactly the staged files). A new `signed-ocr-infrastructure` test runs them with fake files and no signing: missing, empty or extra signatures, a `latest.json` or a second `.sig` anywhere in the bundle, a version mismatch, a non-empty release folder, and tampered, missing or extra copies are all refused for the expected reason, and a correct staging produces exactly the installer and `.sig` with identical hashes. Removing the hash recheck makes the test fail. (3) `windows-validation.yml` takes a `runner` choice input, `windows-2022` by default (the release `installer` job's image) or `windows-latest`; the concurrency group includes it. It already runs `setup-ocr.ps1` and an unsigned installer with OCR, read-only and without secrets, so release step 3 (the hosted pre-flight) now runs on the release image. (4) Release notes: the searchable OCR copy is limited to 32 pages (`searchable_pdf_max_pages: 32`), and the update confirmation reads as new in 0.3.0 for future updates, since a 0.2.0 install updating to 0.3.0 does not show it; the required test phrases are unchanged. (5) `docs/releases.md`: step 3 names the Windows 2022 runner and the OCR steps; step 8 says the five signed installed checks are still pinned to an old artifact and re-pinning or waiving them is the maintainer's call (cut-plan step 12). (6) The vite byte-identity claim was re-measured with the installed version read from `node_modules`: vite 6.4.3 and 6.4.4 builds of the same source give identical `dist/` (`index.html` 363 bytes, CSS 33,396 bytes, JS 351,243 bytes, same SHA-256 each). Evidence (source, Windows, pwsh 7, E: paths): affected tests (`signed-ocr-infrastructure` 6, `windows-validation-workflow` and `workflow-action-pins` 20) pass; full Vitest 105 files, 656 passed, 1 skipped; notices `--check` and `npm run build` pass. No Rust changed, so `cargo test` was not rerun.

- OCR in the signed release path, release-cut step 1a (SM6; workflow, build script, verifiers, tests and docs; branch `sm6/release-0.3.0`; never run hosted, nothing tagged, built or signed). Before this, `release.yml` built `build-installer.ps1 -AzureSigning` without OCR and the script refused `-AzureSigning -OcrSetupRoot` unless `-ArtifactOnly`, which drops the updater artifacts, so a 0.3.0 tag would have shipped without OCR against the 2026-10-14 ruling and against its own release notes. `build-installer.ps1` gains a release mode with OCR (`-AzureSigning -OcrSetupRoot -OutputRoot -ExpectedPublisher -SourceRevision`, no `-ArtifactOnly`). It reuses the proven artifact-only signing path: a clean tracked tree bound to `SourceRevision`; the staged unsigned engine is signed with the same Azure Artifact Signing profile as the app before compilation, so the app embeds the signed engine's identity; the OCR-aware Tauri sign helper signs the app, PDFium and installer and skips only that engine. Unlike artifact-only it keeps the updater key, requires exactly the installer's `.sig` and no manifest in the bundle, extracts the installer and requires the 26 base resources, the five OCR resources under the signed identity, the embedded identity in the app, valid timestamped publisher signatures on installer, app and engine, and an unsigned original engine; writes `installer-verification.json` (mode `azure-signed-release-ocr`) and logs the identity, setup manifest hash and original engine receipt. It then copies exactly the installer and `.sig` into the empty `src-tauri/target/release/bundle/nsis`, rechecks their hashes and runs `release-manifest.mjs`, so `release.yml`'s three-asset checks, artifact upload and publish job are unchanged. `release.yml`: the `installer` job moves to `windows-2022`, the only host where the engine has been built hosted (`ocr-signed-artifact.yml`, which also ran the tests, native gates and signing there); a new `setup-ocr.ps1` step after the native gates builds the pinned engine and reads no secret; the build step passes the OCR arguments with `EXPECTED_PUBLISHER: Joshua Beel` (a workflow constant, the publisher the verifiers already pin) and reads the same seven secrets as before (`TAURI_SIGNING_PRIVATE_KEY`, `AZURE_TENANT_ID`, `AZURE_CLIENT_ID`, `AZURE_CLIENT_SECRET`, `AZURE_SIGNING_ENDPOINT`, `AZURE_SIGNING_ACCOUNT`, `AZURE_SIGNING_PROFILE`); a new proof-check step with no secret requires that mode, `GITHUB_SHA`, five OCR resources and Valid/NotSigned signature states, and prints the identity. Environment gate, tag and version checks, `permissions: {}` with job-scoped `contents: read`/`contents: write` and the publish job are unchanged. Verifier flips: `verify-default-draft.ps1` requires exactly five OCR resources under one lowercase `resources/ocr/<identity>/`, checks the model and three licence texts against the tagged `scripts/ocr/pins.json`, the engine's valid timestamped signature, engine SHA-256 equal to the identity, and the identity embedded in the packaged app; its receipt is schema 2 with an `ocr` section and `ocrBundled=true`. `verify-default-expanded-page-tools.ps1`'s `Assert-DefaultDraftReceipt` and `Assert-InstalledDefaultResources`, which `verify-default-comments-forms.ps1` (SM3) and `verify-default-recovery.ps1` (SM5) import unchanged, require that section and check the five installed OCR files and the engine signature; none claims OCR recognition.
