Tests updated or added: `release-workflow` (OCR steps, exact secret set per step and workflow-wide, `windows-2022`), `signed-ocr-infrastructure` (release-mode guard matrix), `ocr-installer`, `installer-source-revision`, `signed-ocr-artifact-workflow`, `default-draft-verification-workflow` (OCR inventory cases, an OCR package check with a fake engine and pins, real pins shape), `default-expanded-page-tools-workflow` (schema 2 receipt and OCR rejections). Deferred as planned: renaming `licensesArePackagedSidecarsNotNoticeDialogContent` waits for the re-pin of the signed checks (cut-plan step 12); dropping the unused licence build variables in `ocr_build.rs` is handed to SM4. Evidence (source, Windows, pwsh 7, E: cache and target paths): all three edited scripts parse in PowerShell 7 and `build-installer.ps1` also in 5.1; Vitest 105 files, 655 passed, 1 skipped; `npm run build`; `cargo test --locked --bins` 391 passed, 0 failed, 31 ignored; `run-native-recovery-gates.ps1` exit 0 with all six required probes; notices and MPL archive checks; `npm audit` 0. The release mode with OCR has not run end to end: Azure signing, the bundler's `.sig` next to an OCR build, the staging copy and the first `setup-ocr.ps1` run inside `release.yml` are proven only by a hosted run, and a failed tag run uses up its version number.

- vite 6.4.3 to 6.4.4 (SM6; dev dependency only; branch `sm6/release-0.3.0`). `npm audit` reported one high-severity advisory for `vite <=6.4.3` (its bundled launch-editor: missing `%` escaping on Windows, editor CLI flag misinterpretation, an existence check that can be bypassed on Windows), which affects only the development server. 6.4.4 is outside the affected range and was published on 2026-10-06; 6.4.5, which `npm audit fix --force` would pick, was published on 2026-10-21 and was not taken because it is too new. Only `vite` moved in `package-lock.json` (4 lines). The dependency inventory's input hashes for `package.json` and `package-lock.json` changed (2 lines of `inventory.json`); no package record or notice text changed, because dev dependencies are not recorded. Evidence (source, Windows): `npm audit` 0 vulnerabilities; `npm run build` before and after gives byte-identical `dist/` (3 files, same SHA-256), so the frontend shipped in the app is unchanged; notices `--check` passes (368 packages); full Vitest in the release-branch run recorded in the step 1a entry.

- Release notes keep LF line endings on every checkout (SM6; config and tests only; branch `sm6/release-0.3.0`). The 2026-10-14 release dry run found that a Windows checkout turns `docs/release-notes.md` into CRLF, and the whole file becomes the `notes` text of `latest.json`. `.gitattributes` now has `docs/release-notes.md text eol=lf`; the repository copy was already LF, so no file content changed. `release.yml`, `release-manifest.mjs` and `verify-default-draft.ps1` read the same checkout, so the manifest-to-notes comparisons are unaffected. `release-workflow.test.ts` asserts the rule and that the checked-out notes contain no CR. Evidence (source, Windows): after deleting and re-checking out the file with `core.autocrlf=true` it stays LF; `release-workflow` and `release-manifest` tests, 2 files, 10 passed.

- Version 0.3.0 prepared, release-cut step 1 only (SM6; branch `sm6/release-0.3.0`; nothing tagged, built, signed or published). All six version sources now read 0.3.0: `package.json`, `package-lock.json` (both entries, via `npm install --package-lock-only --ignore-scripts`), `src-tauri/tauri.conf.json`, `src-tauri/Cargo.toml` and the `pdf-workstation` entry in `src-tauri/Cargo.lock` (via `cargo update -p pdf-workstation --offline`); no other lockfile line moved, and `assert-release-version.ps1 -Version 0.3.0` passes. The version-pinned tests already derive the version from `package.json`, so none needed a bump; the remaining literal 0.2.12 values in `default-draft-verification-workflow.test.ts` are synthetic fixtures that never read the repository version, and `update_commands.rs`'s `is_upgrade` fixture is left. `docs/release-notes.md` now holds only a user-facing 0.3.0 section in source-build language. Before, the whole file, with every section back to 0.2.0, became the `notes` of `latest.json`, so 0.2.0 users would have seen all of it in the update offer; the earlier sections moved unchanged (heading level only) to [Earlier release notes](releases.md#earlier-release-notes). `release.yml`, `release-manifest.mjs` and `verify-default-draft.ps1` still bind the manifest to the whole file, which is now that one section, so no workflow changed. `release-workflow.test.ts` replaces the four old required phrases, three of which became false for 0.3.0 ("does not bundle OCR", "does not create searchable/document OCR", "makes no redaction … claim"), with: the header `## <version>`, exactly one heading, "tested in source builds", "unverified in an installed <version> build", OCR accuracy unmeasured, no PDF signing or signature validation, "every save writes a new copy", and under 4,096 characters. The notes describe OCR as included in the installer, per the 0.3.0 ruling; that is true only once cut-plan step 1a (OCR in the signed release path) lands before the tag. The dependency-notice coverage text no longer says the collection "does not determine the app license"; it says PDF Workstation is MIT-licensed and the collection is not a legal licensing review. Notices regenerated: 368 packages, 0 review items; `inventory.json` input hashes and the coverage line changed. `docs/releases.md` § Release steps rewritten to follow the cut plan: release commit, reviews, hosted pre-flight, push with the parity freeze, signing secrets in the `release` environment, tag, draft record, installed checks, publish, the in-app update check right after publishing, and rollback to draft. Evidence (source, Windows, pwsh 7, E: cache and target paths): Vitest 105 files, 654 passed, 1 skipped; `npm run build` passed; `cargo test --locked --bins` 391 passed, 0 failed, 31 ignored; `run-native-recovery-gates.ps1` exit 0 (serialized bin 391 passed / 31 ignored, every integration target green, all 6 required probes one exact pass each); notices `--check` and `mpl-source-archives.mjs --check` (5) passed; tracked tree unchanged by the suite. The 0.3.0 installer, its signatures and every installed behaviour in the notes remain unverified.
- Fill forms: review fixes for multi-select and editable combos (SM3; source tests only; branch `sm3/forms-multi-combo`). (1) `form_values` in `pdf_engine.rs` no longer echoes the expected value for choice fields: it takes the value from the options PDFium reports as set (their export values, joined with a newline for a multi-select), cross-checks the combo API's own `value()` against the selected option, and requires a typed combo value to read as nothing selected. PDFium's public combo API cannot return typed text, so that one value still comes from the expected field; our own re-parse of the written `/V` is its independent check. The helper wire (`pdf_helper/protocol.rs`) now carries each option's export value and the typed text so the helper process can do the same. (2) A typed combo value matches option labels only, as the dialog does; text equal to an option's stored value but to no label is refused instead of silently selecting an option whose label differs. Test: an option whose export equals another option's label (typing that label selects the option with the label; typing an export is refused). `cargo test --bins` full run and the forms and helper filters pass; vitest unchanged. Unverified: typed combo text read back by PDFium itself, artwork from other producers, the installed app.

- Fill forms: multi-select list boxes and editable combo boxes (SM3; source tests only; branch `sm3/forms-multi-combo`, off `sm3/forms-breadth`; Joshua's ruling 2026-10-21, merges after the 0.3.0 release checks because it touches `bridge.ts` and the forms files). **Multi-select:** `/Ff` multi-select (bit 22) list boxes with one through 32 options; `/V` is a string or an array of export strings, `/I` must list the same indices in ascending order; the copy writes no `/V` or `/I` for none, a string and one index for one, and an array and an ascending index array for several. The appearance highlights every selected visible row; `/TI` is validated as before and a multi-select never scrolls. The dialog shows a checkbox list; the patch is `{ kind: 'choices', optionIds }` (clearing is an empty list), kept in option order, duplicates and unknown ids refused, and a single-choice patch on a multi-select (or the reverse) is refused. **Editable combo:** `/Ff` combo plus edit (bit 19) dropdowns. A `/V` that matches no export is typed text (Latin-1 rules as text fields, must fit the field width at its fixed size, stored as a PDF text string, shown exactly as typed); `/I` must then be empty. The dialog shows a text input with the options as suggestions and a **Clear text** button; the patch is `{ kind: 'combo', text }`. Typing a listed label or export picks that option as before; picking via the existing `choice` patch still works and clears typed text; empty text clears the field. Option labels and exports stay ASCII. **Still refused:** sorted, commit-on-change, spell-check, read-only flags, multi-select combos, `/TI` on dropdowns. **Engine check:** `form_values` in `pdf_engine.rs` (and the helper wire in `pdf_helper/protocol.rs`, which now carries `multiple`, `editable` and the selected ids) compares PDFium's editable and multi-select flags and per-option selected state with the expected field, so a filled copy PDFium reads differently is refused. Wire: `FormField` choice gains `multiple`, `editable`, `selectedOptionIds`, `text`; `FormPatch` gains `choices` and `combo`; no `App.tsx` change. Tests: `src-tauri/src/forms_multi_combo_tests.rs` (parse, fill, clear, round trip, wrong-kind and malformed refusals, flag combinations), a PDFium end-to-end test in `service.rs` (`multi_select_lists_and_editable_combos_fill_and_read_back_through_pdfium`), and vitest cases in `FillFormsDialog.test.tsx`. Unverified: multi-select or combo artwork written by Acrobat or other producers (refused unless it matches ours), how other viewers present the typed text, the installed app. Not started: Unicode beyond Latin-1 (Joshua: not now).

- Forms breadth follow-ups from the code and SM7 reviews (SM3; source tests only; branch `sm3/forms-breadth`). (1) U+00A0 is now refused (it was accepted while `full_winansi` checked only codes 161 to 255, and a font may remap code 160, for example to the euro sign); the allowed range is U+00A1 to U+00FF without the soft hyphen, and the dialog regex matches. (2) `full_winansi` advances the `/Differences` code with `checked_add` and treats an overflow as not WinAnsi, so an `i64::MAX` index cannot panic a debug build. (3) List-box option labels are written with `win_bytes` like every other text (labels and exports are still enforced as ASCII, so the bytes are identical today). (4) Tests: the full Latin-1 table is compared glyph by glyph (95 names and widths) against a constant written from the published Helvetica metrics, plus the length of the ASCII table; scrolling up (a choice above the top row becomes the new top); a font whose encoding is WinAnsi plus `/Differences` is accepted, refused when it remaps code 233 and accepted when it restates `eacute`; `( ) \` mixed with a Latin-1 letter round-trips with the right appearance bytes; an overflowing difference index is refused without a panic; U+00A0 is refused in Rust and in the dialog. Not started, awaiting Joshua: multi-select lists, editable combo boxes, Unicode beyond Latin-1.

- Fill forms: scrolling list boxes (SM3; source tests only; branch `sm3/forms-breadth`). A noneditable single-select list box with more options than visible rows is now supported (still 1 to 32 options). The source `/TI` top index is read and the expected appearance is drawn from that row; it must be 0 when every option fits and at most `options - rows` otherwise, and a dropdown must still have `/TI` 0. Choosing an option that is out of view sets `/TI` to `min(option index, options - rows)` and writes it together with `/I`; choosing a visible option leaves the scroll alone; clearing keeps it. Unknown or mismatched source appearances are still refused whole, so a producer whose scrolled-list artwork differs from ours is refused rather than rewritten. No wire or UI change (the dialog still shows every option as a list). Tests in `src-tauri/src/forms_breadth_tests.rs`: scroll-to-selection, no scroll for a visible option, clear, reparse of the filled copy, a source with `/TI` 3 accepted, `/TI` 9 and a `/TI` that disagrees with the drawn rows refused. `cargo test --bins -- forms choices radios checkboxes breadth` 56 pass, 4 ignored. Unverified: scrolled list artwork written by Acrobat or other producers, installed app, other viewers' rendering.

- Fill forms: Latin-1 text values (SM3; source tests only; branch `sm3/forms-breadth`). Text fields now accept printable ASCII and the Latin-1 Supplement (U+00A1 to U+00FF, no soft hyphen, no tabs or newlines; characters beyond Latin-1 such as euro or CJK are refused). The appearance uses WinAnsi bytes with Helvetica Latin-1 advance widths for the fit check, MaxLength counts characters, and a non-ASCII value is stored as a UTF-16BE text string (ASCII stays a byte string). The field's font must really show the Latin-1 glyphs: plain `WinAnsiEncoding`, or an encoding dictionary whose `BaseEncoding`/`Differences` assign every code 0xA1 to 0xFF (except 0xAD) its Latin-1 glyph; otherwise a non-ASCII value is refused with an explicit message while ASCII still fills. The ReportLab fixture's Acrobat-style `Helv` encoding qualifies. Choice option labels and exports stay ASCII. Wire change: `FormFields.input` is now `printable-latin1` (was `printable-ascii`); `bridge.ts`, `FillFormsDialog.tsx` (validation regex, message, intro copy) and their tests follow; no `App.tsx` change. Faithful-load, H1 and H2 checks untouched. Tests: `src-tauri/src/forms_breadth_tests.rs` (round trip with WinAnsi appearance bytes and UTF-16 value, refusals for CJK, euro, tab, newline, soft hyphen, C1 control and emoji, width refusal, non-WinAnsi font, width table spot checks); the existing non-ASCII refusal test now uses a CJK character; `cargo test --bins -- forms choices radios checkboxes breadth` 54 pass, 4 ignored; vitest `FillFormsDialog.test.tsx` and `App.forms.test.tsx` 13 pass. Unverified: installed app, how other viewers render the filled copy, and the Latin-1 widths against a second source (they are Adobe's published Helvetica metrics written from memory and only drive the clipping check).

- Split folder-picker unit test made robust to 8.3 short paths (SM2; test only; branch `sm2/split-test-shortname`). Hosted run 39081767760 failed `split::tests::folder_picker_result_refuses_virtual_locations_with_a_clear_message` at `src-tauri/src/split.rs:186`: the runner's TEMP is `C:\Users\RUNNER~1\AppData\Local\Temp`, while the shell item's file-system path for the same temporary folder is spelled `C:\Users\runneradmin\...`, so the textual comparison failed although both name one folder. The test now checks that `folder_path_of` returns an existing directory and that `std::fs::canonicalize` of it equals `std::fs::canonicalize` of the temporary folder; the Libraries root and This PC are still refused with the same message. Product behaviour is unchanged and not affected by short names: `new_split_folder` joins the folder name onto the path the picker returned, and `split::prepare_and_publish` canonicalizes that parent before it checks, stages and publishes, so Split writes into the folder the user chose whichever spelling Windows gives. Evidence (source, Windows, E: build): `cargo test --bins split` passed; not yet rerun hosted.

- CI fix after the first hosted run of `ci.yml` failed (SM6; workflow and tests only; branch `sm6/ci-fix`). Hosted run 39076724755 on `19934f5` passed setup, `npm ci`, fixtures, PDFium setup, `tsc -b` and the notices check, then failed Vitest with 4 failed and 650 passed, so build and `cargo test --bins` did not run. Three failures came from the default shallow checkout: `signed-expanded-page-tools-workflow`, `signed-persistence-workflow` and `signed-reading-tools-workflow` resolve the pinned signed-artifact commit `67d2382` from git history and got "Not a valid object name" or "not in" that commit. `ci.yml` now checks out with `fetch-depth: 0`, as `windows-validation.yml` does (still `persist-credentials: false`), and `scripts/ci-workflow.test.ts` asserts it. The fourth was a timeout: in `signed-ocr-upgrade-workflow.test.ts`, "enforces bounded process and installed-registry facts and writes one sanitized record" took 6.3 s hosted against Vitest's default 5 s. It is the only PowerShell harness test in that file without its own timeout; it now has 15 s like its siblings. The suite-wide timeout is unchanged (`windows-validation.yml` passes none either), and no test is skipped. Evidence (source, Windows, full-history worktree, CI Vitest flags): the four affected files plus `ci-workflow` and `workflow-action-pins`, 6 files, 49 passed. The fix remains unverified hosted until the next run of `ci.yml`.

- Repo renamed `Joshua-Beel/Smacrobat` → `Joshua-Beel/PDF-Workstation` (SM6; branch `sm6/repo-rename`). Updated every exact-string use: GITHUB_REPOSITORY guards and checkout repository lines in 10 manual workflows; release.yml expected asset URLs; `scripts/release-manifest.mjs` latest.json installer URL; `src-tauri/tauri.conf.json` updater endpoint (new builds poll `https://github.com/Joshua-Beel/PDF-Workstation/releases/latest/download/latest.json`); repository pins and pinned api.github.com paths in `verify-*.ps1` and `setup-installed-app-webdriver.ps1` (artifact and asset IDs unchanged); matching script test assertions, plus a draft-verifier case that refuses an old-name asset URL. [Releases and signing](releases.md) now uses the canonical URLs and documents that installed 0.2.0–0.2.4 copies (F-CK-Adobe endpoint) and later builds (Smacrobat endpoint) reach updates only through GitHub's rename redirects, so repositories with those names must never be created under `Joshua-Beel`, and the account must never be renamed or deleted. Historical run links in `docs/` are left (they redirect). Source build: vitest release/workflow/verifier subset (32 files) 261 passed; cargo test --bins 391 passed / 31 ignored; dependency-notices --check 368 packages. On 2026-10-17 the `latest.json` URL under all three names returned HTTP 200 for the same release asset; the in-app update path from installed copies remains unverified until the in-app update check runs from published 0.2.0.

- README restructured (SM0; docs only; branch `sm0/readme-slim`). The README now carries one-line feature summaries grouped by area and one-line summaries of the newest changes. The full "What works" bullets, the release and signing paragraph from Install and the "What's still missing" paragraphs moved verbatim to [docs/features.md](features.md); every earlier Recent-changes entry moved verbatim, in order, to this file. Only relative link paths were adjusted for the `docs/` folder. No code or test changed.

- Tests on every push and pull request (SM6; workflow and tests only, never run hosted; branch `sm6/ci-push`). New `.github/workflows/ci.yml` runs on pushes to `master` and pull requests into `master` (never `pull_request_target`), on `windows-latest`, with `permissions: contents: read`, no secrets, no environment and `persist-credentials: false`. It reuses these steps from the hosted-proven `windows-validation.yml` setup: `npm ci`, fixtures, `setup-pdfium.ps1`, `cargo fetch --locked` then `dependency-notices.mjs --check`, single-thread Vitest (`--pool=threads --maxWorkers=1 --no-file-parallelism`) and `npm run build`. Two steps are new compared with `windows-validation.yml` and have never run hosted: a separate `tsc -b` type-check and `cargo test --locked --bins` (the validation workflow runs the full serialized native gate instead). A tracked-source recheck runs last. It builds no installer and runs no OCR setup or native recovery gates (those stay in the manual validation workflow). `concurrency` cancels superseded pull request runs; pushes to `master` are never cancelled, so every push gets a result. Timeout 45 minutes. Caching: npm through `setup-node`; Cargo registry, git db and compiled dependencies in `src-tauri/target` through `actions/cache` 4.3.0 (SHA-pinned restore/save), keyed on the Rust toolchain (`dtolnay/rust-toolchain`'s `cachekey` output, a hash of the rustc version) plus the `Cargo.lock` hash, with no fallback keys. The cache is written only from a successful `master` push when that key does not exist yet, after removing `incremental`, the app's own binaries, fingerprints and build-script output, so each entry holds only dependencies for one toolchain and lockfile; it logs the remaining target size. `CARGO_INCREMENTAL=0`. New `scripts/ci-workflow.test.ts` pins the triggers, read-only permission, absence of secrets and release steps, step order, concurrency rule, cache key and cache-save condition. No test is skipped for the runner; the one existing skip (opt-in OCR accuracy run without an OCR setup root) is unchanged. Release and tag workflows unchanged. Evidence (source, Windows, local dry run of every step with E: cache and target paths): Vitest 105 files, 654 passed, 1 skipped; `cargo test --bins` 391 passed, 0 failed, 31 ignored; notices check, `tsc -b` and build passed; tracked tree unchanged. Expected hosted duration about 15 to 20 minutes cold, less with a warm Cargo cache; the workflow, its cache and its duration remain unverified until the first hosted run.

- Viewer scroll follow-ups (SM1, tests and docs only; branch `sm1/scroll-followups`). The Find cancellation test now uses a 12-page document and asserts the loop stopped after the first 8-page window instead of reading every page; a new Viewer test asserts a revision change clears the shown page image (no stale page) and releases its URL when the new render arrives. Behaviour note, unchanged: the 8-page `page_text` window is requested in one burst when a search starts, so after Stop, a query change or the 500-page cap up to 7 already-requested calls still run to completion in the worker and their results are discarded; no new request is issued once the search is cancelled. A smaller first burst would give back some of the round-trip hiding, so it was left as is.

- Find in document: pipelined page text and throttled progress (SM1, source build; branch `sm1/viewer-scroll`). The search loop used to await one `page_text` IPC call per page and re-render the panel for every page. It now keeps up to 8 `page_text` requests in flight, consumed strictly in page order (hits, the 500-matching-page cap, Stop and query-change cancellation behave as before; up to 7 already-requested pages may be fetched and discarded after a stop or the cap), and updates the "Searching page N of M" line at most every 120 ms. No backend change: the worker still extracts one page at a time (about 1.5 ms per page measured with the ignored `viewer_perf` harness), so a whole-document scan is still bounded by that (about 13 s at 10,000 pages); only the IPC round-trip gaps and the per-page React commits are removed. Proxy (`src/SearchPanel.perf.test.tsx`, 400 pages, mocked `pageText` with a simulated 2 ms round trip, react-test-renderer): 403 panel commits and 1.13 s before, 5 commits and 0.14 s after. The round trip is simulated, so the wall-time figure shows latency hiding in the loop, not a measured Tauri IPC cost. A single batched or streaming backend search command is not built.

- Viewer scroll cost and zoom flash (SM1, source build; branch `sm1/viewer-scroll`). The on-page text layer no longer re-renders every glyph on every scroll event: `Page`, `TextLayer`, the glyph list and the search-highlight layer are memoised, `Viewer` hands `Page` stable callbacks and per-page annotation arrays, and glyph sizing and placement moved from one React component with state and a layout effect per character to one batched DOM pass per page (same span-per-character DOM, same `data-geometry-index`, `data-angle`, `left/top/transform` result, same two position corrections; geometry shape and `text_geometry.rs` untouched). A zoom step now keeps the old page image on screen (scaled by CSS) until the new render arrives instead of blanking to "Rendering page..."; a failed render still drops the image and shows the error with Try again, and superseded or replaced image URLs are released. Proxy measurement (`src/Viewer.scroll-perf.test.tsx`, react-test-renderer, development React, 3 mounted pages of 3,430 glyphs, a parent re-render with fresh inline props per scroll event, mocked layout reads): median 121 ms per scroll event before, 3.5 ms after; mount plus layout 480 ms before, 80 ms after. These are Node render-phase figures with mocked layout, not frames per second: real scroll smoothness, real layout cost and glyph placement in the webview remain unmeasured and need a Chrome trace in the installed app.

- PDF-engine helper: no stale document handle after a helper restart (SM5; code review P3 of step 6; debug-only, off by default). If the helper died after a document looked up its handle but before the request was sent, the replacement helper received the old handle and answered "no such document" (never another document's). Now each document request re-checks, after the supervisor has replaced any dead helper, that the helper generation still matches; if not, the document reloads into the new helper and looks up again (once). The generation is also passed into the call, which refuses to send on a mismatch. New deterministic test (a test-only hook kills the helper exactly between the lookup and the send, for a plain request and for one that shares a render section): both succeed in the replacement helper and the document is not set aside; with the old behaviour patched back in, it fails with "The PDF engine has no such document.". Evidence (source build, Windows, debug): helper tests 33/33 three runs; parallel `cargo test --bins` 391 passed, 0 failed, 31 ignored.

- Isolated PDF-engine helper, step 6b: output validation verified through the helper, and the step 5 redaction test tightened (SM5; tests only). New service tests, run as child processes with the engine forced: Save a copy of chosen pages, Save all pages, Split, Combine, Insert pages, Replace pages and Fill form copy write byte-identical files with the helper on and in process (9 files); a helper crash during output validation fails that Save or Split, publishes nothing (no copy, no split folder), leaves the source usable, and the same operation then succeeds. The step 5 redaction comparison now runs both sides as children with the engine forced (before, a parent run with the helper already on compared the helper with itself), and on each side the secret run must equal the control run (code review P3, SM7 suggestion). Evidence: as step 6a (same runs).

- Isolated PDF-engine helper, step 6a: a helper that dies while idle is now replaced (SM5; debug-only, off by default). Found by the step 6 crash test: when the helper died between requests, the next request that shares a section (any load or render) failed to duplicate its handle into the dead process and reported it as an engine error, so the dead helper was never replaced and every later load or render failed. Now the supervisor checks whether the helper process has exited before each request and before a document decides whether to reload; a dead helper is recorded and replaced under the usual restart policy (the restart counts toward the crash-loop cap), no document is blamed, and documents reload on their next call. A handle share that fails because the helper died a moment earlier is treated as a helper failure. Test-only: a fault that makes the helper exit when its next request arrives (a crash with that request in flight), now used by the worker's `TestEngine` kill. New helper test: after an idle crash a render and a load-path request both succeed in a new helper and the document is not set aside. Evidence (source build, Windows, debug): helper and helper-named service tests 38/38; full native suite with the helper off 390 passed, 0 failed, 31 ignored and with `SMACROBAT_PDF_ENGINE_HELPER=1` 390 passed, 0 failed, 31 ignored; `run-native-recovery-gates.ps1` exits 0 (serialized 390 passed, 0 failed, 31 ignored); `redaction_adversarial` 36/36 on and off; vitest (CI flags) 100 files, 640 passed, 1 skipped.

- Test for a non-zero OCR exit that never reads its input (SM4; test only). New fake child behaviour `nonzero-unread` in `src-tauri/src/ocr_process.rs` prints its stderr line and exits 7 without reading stdin, and `nonzero_exit_is_reported_ahead_of_a_writer_error_when_input_is_never_read` feeds it a 3 MB page, far over the pipe buffer, so the host's input writer blocks and then sees the closed pipe. The test asserts the result is always `ChildExit { code: 7, .. }` with the stderr text, whether or not the writer error was observed, so it cannot depend on timing. No OCR code changed. Source build only.

- Isolated PDF-engine helper, step 5b: viewer copy cost recovered (SM5; debug-only, off by default). The viewer render is still copied out of the shared section before PNG encoding (SM7 L-a), but into an app-private buffer kept between renders (released past 64 MiB), so the copy lands in memory that is already faulted in instead of a fresh allocation. Row-by-row streaming into the encoder was not needed and would risk different PNG chunking, so the PNG bytes would no longer match the in-process render. Evidence (source build, Windows; T16 in release, three corpus fixtures plus the 12 largest real work PDFs, 15 timed renders each): 45 of 45 rows within the design budget again (36 of 45 with a fresh copy per render); overhead p50 800 px -4.7 to +2.6 ms, 1,600 px -4.8 to +4.0 ms, 3,000 px -0.8 to +5.1 ms; p95 at most +5.1 ms; helper start p50 12 ms; restart plus re-render 31 ms; equivalence 384 renders byte-identical; helper tests 31/31.

- Isolated PDF-engine helper, step 5a: print, page-image export, raster redaction and OCR rasterisation verified through the helper (SM5; debug-only, off by default; tests only, no product change). With the setting on, every engine call already goes through the helper and every bitmap through an app-owned section (steps 3-4), so this step proves those paths. Redaction keeps `RenderMode::Viewer` and the drift guard unchanged. New test-only worker request `TestEngine` (which engine is in use; kill the running helper as a crash would). New service tests, each rerunning itself in a child process with the helper on: raster redaction through the helper gives byte-identical page images to the in-process worker for SM7's print-only and screen-only vectors (secret and control) and for an edited page with a highlight; T8, a helper crash fails the print page in flight without a hang, and after Try again four new print jobs render the same pixels (the four-job admission is free); T9, a crash fails the OCR rasterisation in flight, and after Try again OCR returns the same raster (its worker hold was released; a second document is used because a second death closes a document's engine side for good). Evidence (source build, Windows, debug): the whole native suite run with `SMACROBAT_PDF_ENGINE_HELPER=1` (every service test, including print, export, redaction and OCR, through the helper) 384 passed, 0 failed, 31 ignored; `redaction_adversarial` 36/36 with the setting on and off (it is a self-contained replica that does not use the engine, so the byte-identical worker comparison above is the helper-on redaction evidence); the new tests pass.

- Isolated PDF-engine helper, step 4 review follow-ups (SM5; code review P3s and SM7 L-a, L-b of `7a0a784..a6b6b06`). In the helper, a render request now closes the section handle the app passed with it on every path, including a request for a document the helper does not have and a refused bitmap size. In the app, a viewer render is copied out of the shared section before it is PNG-encoded, because the helper keeps write access to that section (SM7 L-a); this gives back part of step 4c's speed-up (T16, release: 36 of 45 rows within the design budget instead of 45; the misses are at 3,000 px, +2 to +15 ms at the median and up to +29 ms at p95 on one file, plus one 1,600 px row 0.4 ms over; 800 px and the rest unchanged). Page sizes, crop boxes and destination views from the helper must be finite (page sizes also positive) or the reply is malformed (SM7 L-b). The viewer's page error text has `role="alert"`, and each Try again button is labelled with its page ("Try again to render page N"). A comment on `MAX_BGRA_BYTES` notes that viewer renders wider than about 13,400 px exceed it although `MAX_VIEWER_WIDTH` allows 16,384 (no caller asks for more than 3,000; left for step 5). Evidence (source build, Windows, debug unless noted): helper tests 31/31 three runs (new: finite-float decoders; a refused render size closes the passed handle, checked by the section's own handle count); parallel `cargo test --bins` 384 passed, 0 failed, 31 ignored; vitest (CI flags) 100 files, 640 passed, 1 skipped; `tsc -b` passes.

- Flaky OCR child-process test fixed (SM4; test only). `ocr_process::tests::stdout_and_stderr_caps_are_exact_and_fast_exit_cannot_bypass_them` could fail under load because the fake OCR child for the output-cap, invalid-UTF-8, non-zero-exit and unexpected-stderr behaviours wrote its output and exited without reading standard input, so the host's input writer could find the pipe already closed and report "Could not write OCR input: The pipe is being closed". A real tesseract reads its input first, so the OCR code is unchanged (a non-zero exit is still reported ahead of a writer error); those fake behaviours now read standard input to the end before acting. The process-reaped wait in the test helper is widened from 1 s to 10 s (it returns as soon as the child is gone). The failure was seen once in about 200 runs under heavy contention and not reproduced in 100 later runs of the single test under 24 to 40 busy threads, so the missing stdin read is the identified race, not a reproduced-and-cleared one; a separate load-sensitive test, `timeout_and_unwind_drop_kill_and_reap_the_owned_child` (a 200 ms timeout that can kill the fake child before it publishes its PID), fails under that same heavy load and was fixed in the same change: the test now waits for the fake child's PID in the `after_resume` hook, before the host's 200 ms clock starts, instead of lengthening the timeout. Source build only.

- Isolated PDF-engine helper, step 4c: viewer renders at in-process speed, and the memory, real-file and performance checks (SM5; still debug-only and off by default). Viewer pages now come from the helper in RGBA order into the app's render section (new request kind 21, rendered by a new `InProcessDocument::render_viewer_rgba_into` with the viewer's flags; `render_bgra_into` and `RenderMode` are unchanged), and the app PNG-encodes straight from the section with the same encoder settings as before, so there is no channel swap or extra copy; the render section is kept between renders up to 64 MiB (the largest viewer render). The supervisor reports the helper job's peak commit. New tests: T1, a render whose decoding needs more memory than the helper may use (a 3,000 x 3,000 photo-like JPEG page; cap set halfway between the measured 5 MiB baseline and the measured need), fails with the memory message, leaves the caller's buffer untouched, sets that document aside, and another document re-renders identically within about 30 ms; T2, opening a 65,536-page document under a cap between its measured need (60 MiB) and the baseline fails with the memory message and other documents keep working. Measurement harnesses (ignored, run on demand with read-only file lists): T15 and T16. Evidence (source build, Windows; T15 and T16 in release): helper tests 29/29 plus 2 ignored; equivalence 384 renders byte-identical; `redaction_adversarial` 36/36; T15 on 100 real work PDFs (the 12 largest under 1 GiB plus 88 at random, seed 1014; 11 KB to 538 MB, 5,038 pages): all open through the helper with identical page sizes and page 1 at 1,600 px is the same PNG, helper peak 65 MiB with all open; T16 on the three corpus fixtures and the 12 largest real files at 800, 1,600 and 3,000 px (15 timed renders each): every row within the design budget (p50 overhead at most max(5 ms, 5%), p95 at most max(15 ms, 10%)), overhead p50 -4.3 to +6.8 ms and p95 -9.4 to +9.3 ms; helper start p50 14 ms (one cold start 366 ms); idle helper 2.0 MiB committed; restart plus re-render of the active page 28 ms.

- Isolated PDF-engine helper, step 4b: "Try again" on a page that failed to render (SM5; the button appears only where the viewer already showed a render error). `Viewer.tsx` adds a Try again button next to the error text in the page placeholder; it calls the new `retry_pdf_engine` command (`bridge.ts` `retryPdfEngine`) and then renders the page again. The command sends `RetryEngine(id)` to the pdf-worker, which lets a crash-looped helper start again (`reset_after_stop`) and lets that document (and its comment copy) reload after a helper failure (`retry`). Both are new default no-op methods on the engine traits, so with the in-process engine (the default) the button only re-requests the render. Evidence (source build, Windows, debug): new vitest case (failed render shows Try again; clicking it calls the command for that document and renders again; the button goes away) and `Viewer.test.tsx` 10/10; `tsc -b` passes; new service test (retry accepted for an open and an unknown document, render unchanged); the helper's suspect-document and crash-loop tests (T5) still pass through the trait methods.

- Isolated PDF-engine helper, step 4a: sources and bitmaps in app-owned shared sections (SM5; still debug-only and off by default, no user-visible change). New `src-tauri/src/pdf_helper/section.rs`. A document's source now lives in a pagefile-backed section the app creates; the helper gets a handle that allows reading only, maps it and loads the PDF over the mapping without copying it, so source bytes are no longer charged to the helper's 1 GiB cap. BGRA renders go into an app-owned section the helper gets write access to for that request; the app copies the pixels out. Viewer renders are now sized and PNG-encoded in the app: the app asks for the planned page size, computes the pixel size the way pdfium-render does, has the helper render with the viewer's flags into the section, and encodes the PNG itself, so no helper-made image reaches the webview. The load request carries a section handle and length instead of the bytes (request frames are capped at 64 MiB); the helper's viewer-PNG request is retired and its wire value 7 is not reused. Read-only sharing is a guard against mistakes, not a privilege boundary (the helper runs as the same user). Evidence (source build, Windows, debug, machine at 100% CPU from other work): helper tests 27/27; `RemotePdfEngine` byte-identical to `InProcessEngine` on 8 fixtures, 384 renders (viewer PNGs now encoded in the app included) and 673 checks; T13 read-only half: a write mapping through the shared source handle fails while a read mapping sees the app's bytes; a 6,000 x 6,000 bitmap (144 MB) renders under a 128 MiB helper cap, and a 201 MB source (the welcome PDF plus an unused 192 MiB stream) loads and renders under the same cap (both were refused or impossible in step 3).

- Helper protocol carries the new viewer render mode (SM5; fixes the build after merging SM2's `RenderMode::Viewer`, `1653971`, with the helper step 3 merge `7277a36`). `pdf_helper/protocol.rs` encodes `RenderMode::Viewer` as wire value 2; Print (0) and Plain (1) are unchanged, and any other value is still refused. The `RemotePdfEngine`-vs-`InProcessEngine` equivalence test now also renders every fixture page in Viewer mode (the raster-redaction path): 384 renders byte-identical (was 288), 673 other checks equal; a new test pins the three wire values and rejects others. Evidence (source build, Windows, debug): helper tests 25/25; parallel `cargo test --bins` 377 passed, 0 failed, 29 ignored (first run: one OCR process test failed under load, `ocr_process::tests::stdout_and_stderr_caps_are_exact_and_fast_exit_cannot_bypass_them`; the rerun passed); `cargo test --test redaction_adversarial` 36 passed; `run-native-recovery-gates.ps1` exits 0 (serialized 377 passed, 0 failed, 29 ignored; all integration targets; six probes).

- Containment-slack DPI linked to the OCR raster DPI (SM4; code only, no behaviour change). `RASTER_DPI` in `src-tauri/src/searchable_ocr.rs` stays a literal 150 because that file is also compiled into the integration tests, but `searchable_ocr_job.rs` now has a compile-time assertion that it equals `ocr::OCR_DPI` and `ocr_commands::OCR_DPI` (both made `pub(crate)`), so changing one without the others fails the build. Source build only.

- `zip` removed from the dependency graph (SM6; RUSTSEC-2026-0367/0368/0369 in `zip` 4.6.1, fixed only in `zip` 9, which every published `tauri-plugin-updater` 2.x/3.0-alpha rejects with `zip ^4`). `src-tauri/Cargo.toml` now builds `tauri-plugin-updater =2.11.0` with `default-features = false, features = ["rustls-tls", "system-proxy"]`, dropping only its `zip` feature (which also gated `tar`/`flate2`, already in the graph for other crates). Basis: the app ships NSIS only (`tauri.conf.json` `targets: ["nsis"]`, `createUpdaterArtifacts: true`, no `v1Compatible`), the release workflows assert the assets are exactly the installer, its `.sig` and `latest.json`, and in the plugin source the zip branch of the Windows `extract` is only for a zip payload while a bare `.exe` goes through `extract_exe`; the minisign check runs in `download` before any extraction. A zip payload would now be treated as an installer and rejected. Lockfile diff: removes `zip`, `arbitrary`, `derive_arbitrary` only; notices regenerated (368 packages, `--check` passes). Evidence (source build, Windows, debug): `cargo audit` reports no vulnerabilities (7 pre-existing allowed warnings); `cargo test --bin pdf-workstation update_commands` 26 passed; parallel `cargo test --bins` 340 passed, 0 failed, 25 ignored; `npm run build` passes. An installed-app update was not exercised.

- Viewer performance harness (SM1; test-only, no product change). New `src-tauri/src/viewer_perf.rs`, registered under `#[cfg(all(test, windows))]` in `main.rs`: three `#[ignore]` tests that drive the real `PdfService` worker in a release build and time open, per-stage open cost, render versus PNG encode, cache hits, deep jumps, page text and `text_geometry`, bookmarks and process memory on generated 10,000 and 65,536-page documents plus a read-only sample of 20 real work PDFs (11 KB to 538 MB; no names recorded). Findings are in the SM1 viewer-perf log: at 65,536 pages open takes 12 s and about 940 MiB, and 95 to 99 percent of every open is `page_sizes` loading each page (about 185 microseconds per page); in isolation `FPDF_GetPageSizeByIndexF` gave identical sizes 25 to 30 times faster (0.5 s, about 600 MiB less). Not measured: the webview (60 fps, PNG decode, DOM layout); the frontend figures are a code read plus a React render-phase proxy. Run on the shared target with `cargo test --release --bin pdf-workstation viewer_perf_service -- --ignored --nocapture --test-threads=1` (and `viewer_perf_stages`, `viewer_perf_split`, one process each) with `SM1_PERF_DIR`, `SM1_PERF_OUT`, optional `SM1_PERF_PAGES` and `SM1_PERF_REAL`. Code review and SM7 review clear.

- Searchable OCR copy now completes with the real engine (SM4, branch `sm4/real-engine-tsv`; code review and SM7 review clear). The TSV profile in `src-tauri/src/ocr_process.rs` passes `-c tessedit_create_tsv=1 -c tessedit_create_txt=0` instead of the `tsv` config name, because the packaged tessdata holds no `configs` folder and tesseract wrote `Can't open tsv` to stderr, which the app (correctly) treats as failure; the empty-stderr check is unchanged and the new arguments are pinned in `command_line_quotes_paths_without_using_a_shell` and in the accuracy harness drift guard. Measured against the local engine build (`ocr-identity-check-20261013`): exit 0, empty stderr, stdout is 12-column TSV only. The TSV parser now allows an overhang of 3.84 pt (8 px at the 150 DPI raster, derived from the raster DPI) for paragraph-in-block, line-in-paragraph and word-in-line boxes (a real Arial line box sat 4 px below its paragraph box) and still refuses larger ones; the slack compounds over those three levels, so a word can sit up to about 24 px outside its block at 150 DPI while staying exactly inside the page; page and block containment stay exact. Review follow-ups from the Unicode change: the embedded font's `hhea` `xMaxExtent` is 500 instead of 0, and the word filter also refuses format characters U+0600-0605, U+06DD, U+070F, U+0890, U+0891, U+08E2, U+110BD, U+110CD and U+13430-1343F. Test-only: the fake OCR child publishes `child.pid` by write-then-rename and `wait_for_pid` retries on an empty or unparseable read (the `Busy` cascade SM5 saw under parallel load). New ignored end-to-end test `searchable_ocr_job::tests::real_engine_creates_searchable_copies_that_pdfium_extracts` runs the real engine through the searchable coordinator on the known-text scan and on the repo fixture `reportlab-plain-fields.pdf`, publishes with the PDFium oracle, reopens the copy and compares PDFium text. Source build and one machine only; no accuracy claim, no installed-app or hosted verification.

- Review follow-ups on the redaction and Split changes (SM2; source build). Split's folder picker is now the Windows folder dialog shown directly (`split::pick_new_split_folder`, `IFileOpenDialog` with `FOS_PICKFOLDERS | FOS_FORCEFILESYSTEM`, owned by the main window, run with the "Split PDFs" name lookup on a blocking thread). By code reading, `rfd` 0.15.4 `pick_folder` replaced the dialog options with `FOS_PICKFOLDERS` alone and turned a selection without a file-system path (a Library, This PC) into the same `None` as Cancel, so the dialog closed with no message; now such a selection gives "Choose a folder on a drive or network share. Libraries, This PC and other virtual locations cannot hold split files." New `windows` features `Win32_UI_Shell` and `Win32_UI_Shell_Common`. A split folder that appears after the name check now reports "A split folder with that name already exists now (it appeared in the chosen folder during the split). Split again to use the next free Split PDFs name." The third-party notices inventory is regenerated for the changed `Cargo.toml` (`node scripts/dependency-notices.mjs --check` passes). The redaction drift guard now pins every statement in `render_bgra_into` that touches the render config whole and refuses a second `use_print_quality` or any raw `FPDF_` flag (SM7 G-1). The three cover tests also check that the cover itself is drawn in the output. New service test `raster_redaction_with_session_comments_and_rotation_matches_the_viewer_pixels` checks that, with a session comment and a rotation, the redacted page equals the viewer render outside the mark. New unit test `folder_picker_result_refuses_virtual_locations_with_a_clear_message` (the Libraries root and This PC are refused; a real folder is accepted). The folder picker itself was not shown (no app launch); the native dialog verifier still needs a rerun.

- Split now uses a real folder picker, and Save a copy has a dialog title (SM2; fixes the two defects found by `scripts/verify-native-page-dialogs.ps1`; source build). Split used a Save As dialog (`save_file()` with the file name "Split PDFs") whose typed name became the new folder; `split_document` in `src-tauri/src/main.rs` now calls `rfd` `pick_folder()` titled "Choose a folder for split PDFs", and new `split::new_split_folder` picks the first unused name among "Split PDFs", "Split PDFs (2)" ... "Split PDFs (99)" inside the chosen folder. The output location still comes only from the native dialog, nothing is created before publishing, and the atomic publish still refuses a folder that appears meanwhile, so an existing folder is never replaced. `save_copy` now sets the title "Save a copy as a new file", like its sibling routes (Windows showed "Save As"). The Split dialog text says where the files go; `docs/tools/organize-pages.md` is updated. The verifier now expects the new titles, picks a fresh parent folder for Split and checks that the dialog's default button selects a folder; its source test checks main.rs for `pick_folder()` and the new titles. New unit test `new_split_folder_picks_the_first_unused_name_inside_the_chosen_folder`; touched vitest files 3 passed (22 tests); `npm run build` ok. The verifier itself was not rerun (it opens app windows), so the real folder picker, typing a folder path into it, and the new title remain unverified in the running app.

- Raster redaction now renders with the viewer's flags (SM2; fixes SM7 R-1 and S-1, both MEDIUM; source build). The redaction worker rendered every page with `use_print_quality(true)` (`FPDF_PRINTING`), while the viewer where marks are drawn does not, so the copy could differ from what the user saw: print-only content (an optional-content layer off on screen with `/PrintState /ON`, a NoView+Print annotation) appeared outside every mark, and screen-only covers (an opaque annotation without the Print flag, a layer on for View with `/PrintState /OFF`) vanished and uncovered the text beneath. New `RenderMode::Viewer` in `src-tauri/src/pdf_engine.rs` applies exactly the viewer's flags (`render_annotations(true)`, no `FPDF_PRINTING`), and the `RasterRedact` arm in `service.rs` uses it. Print, page-image export and OCR input keep `RenderMode::Print` (unchanged). Evidence: the five former `documents_leak_*` tests in `src-tauri/tests/redaction_adversarial.rs` now assert no secret ink and are renamed; three new tests cover the consequences of the viewer flags (Hidden, NoView and Hidden+Print annotations stay out; a screen-only annotation or layer appears in the copy exactly as on screen and is removed when marked); the drift guard now requires the `RasterRedact` render to use `RenderMode::Viewer` and pins that arm to `render_annotations(true)` alone; the engine equivalence test checks `RenderMode::Viewer` pixel for pixel against `render_viewer_png` on its four corpus files, every quarter turn, with and without a crop; and new service test `raster_redaction_output_matches_the_viewer_not_print_mode` drives the real worker on an in-memory fixture carrying all four vectors (it fails when the worker is switched back to print flags). `cargo test --test redaction_adversarial` 36 passed, `--test raster_redaction` 4 passed, parallel `cargo test --bins` 341 passed, 0 failed, 25 ignored. Only synthetic fixtures were exercised; no installed app, real-world documents, XFA, transparency groups or blend modes. Guide: `docs/tools/redaction.md`.

- Forms hardening H2 follow-ups (SM3; source tests only, local branch `sm3/forms-h2-followups`). `forms.rs` now loads through `check_faithful_load`, which keeps H2 (every in-use cross-reference entry must have loaded) and adds three refusals plus one late-`/Length` refusal (a stream left empty by an indirect `/Length` that resolved late to 0 while data follows, located in the source from the `%PDF-` header). A reverse check (a loaded object must be backed by its own cross-reference entry) was tried and removed: it refused normal hybrid-xref files (a classic table plus `/XRefStm`), whose object-stream objects lopdf loads without a table entry; that gap stays open. (1) Stale copy: when the file has object streams, the document is reloaded with the object streams filtered out, and every direct (`Normal`) entry must still be present, so a newest copy that fails to parse can no longer be replaced silently by an older object-stream copy of the same number. (2) Unused `0000000000 00000 n` rows (offset 0 cannot hold an object) no longer count as in-use; header-number and generation mismatches stay refused. (3) A stream left empty because its `/Length` does not resolve (indirect length that is missing, dangling or not an integer, lopdf `reader.rs:1083`) is refused. Measured read-only on real work PDFs (4,552 files) plus repo fixtures (6): 4,558 loaded, 177 with an AcroForm, the old rule refused 0 and the new rule refuses 0, so no narrowing beyond the offset-0 row was needed. In-memory tests in `src-tauri/src/forms_h2_tests.rs` (stale copy, offset-0 and free rows, header mismatch and unloaded object, unresolved or missing `/Length` with an empty-by-design control; plus three for the later work: a late `/Length` of 0 with data following is refused, a stream start is located correctly when junk precedes `%PDF-` (the header is searched once, before the stream loop), and a hybrid-xref file with object-stream objects declared only by its `/XRefStm` is not refused). Not verified: installed app, other viewers' handling of these files.

- Follow-ups from the reviews of `3da4934..bb09ea1` (SM5). Helper reply limit (P2): the reader now takes the per-request limit with an atomic swap to 0 when a frame's length prefix arrives, so each request admits exactly one frame; before, a frame read just after a reply but before the limit was reset could be allocated at the previous request's limit (up to 256 MiB). The idle-frame test now asserts only the outcome (the helper is ended as malformed), which holds whichever way the stray frame arrives, and a new codec test shows one stored limit admits one frame and refuses the next. Form-fill output check (P3): since page sizes no longer load every page, the check after filling a form now loads each output page explicitly and compares its size with the page-size list ("Filled PDF page N could not be loaded" otherwise). Page errors (P3): a source page PDFium cannot load now reports "Unable to read page N: ..." instead of PDFium's text alone. Evidence (source build, Windows, debug): helper and engine tests 26/26 three runs; the idle-frame and one-frame tests 20/20 repeated; parallel `cargo test --bins` 365 passed, 0 failed, 25 ignored (first run).

- Faster page sizes at open (SM5, from SM1's viewer-performance findings). `InProcessDocument::page_sizes` in `src-tauri/src/pdf_engine.rs` now reads each page's size with `FPDF_GetPageSizeByIndexF` (`PdfPages::page_size`) instead of loading every page; the empty, over-65,536-page and invalid-dimension checks and their messages are unchanged. The helper runs this same code, so `RemotePdfEngine` gets it too. Behaviour difference: a page whose size can be read but whose content cannot be loaded no longer fails at open; it fails when that page is rendered or read. Evidence (source build, Windows): a new test compares the result bit for bit with loading every page, on every fixture PDF plus a constructed file with inherited MediaBox and rotation, every quarter turn and -90, CropBox with and without rotation, offset and reversed MediaBox, a CropBox past the MediaBox, UserUnit and a 45-degree rotation (10 documents, 1,635 pages, identical), and on 8 real work PDFs (1 to 764 pages, up to 538 MB, read only: 18 documents, 3,314 pages, identical); `RemotePdfEngine` still matches `InProcessEngine` (288 renders, 673 checks). SM1's ignored harness (`viewer_perf_stages`, release, two runs each, other builds running), page-size stage before and after: 1,000 pages 135-157 ms to 9-11 ms; 10,000 pages 1,432-1,493 ms to 82-97 ms; 65,536 pages 9,786-10,019 ms to 558-929 ms; real 761 and 764-page scans 135-185 ms to 15-22 ms. Parallel `cargo test --bins` 364 passed, 0 failed, 25 ignored (the first run hit the known OCR `wait_for_pid` flake, 2 OCR tests; the rerun passed); `cargo test --test redaction_adversarial` 33 passed.

- Isolated PDF-engine helper, step 3 review fixes (SM5; SM7 review of `3da4934..1a4c93e` M1, M2, L1, I1 and the code review's P2/P3s; still no user-visible change). M1: `main()` checks for `--pdf-engine-helper` only in debug builds (`#[cfg(all(debug_assertions, windows))]` plus `helper_mode_requested`, which is `false` in release), so a release exe ignores the flag and starts the app normally; and the helper no longer takes a PDFium path from the app: the init request carries no payload (one that does ends the helper with exit 3 before anything loads), and the helper loads `resources/pdfium/bin/pdfium.dll` below its own exe folder, which is the app's resource folder; with the debug setting on, the app refuses to start the helper if that is not the same file it would load. This replaces "binds PDFium from the path the app sends" in the step 3 entry below. M2 and code-review P2: the reader's event queue is bounded to one event (`sync_channel(1)`) and the reply limit is 0 between requests (set per request, reset after each reply), so a frame the helper writes while nothing is pending is refused before anything is allocated, and the next request ends that helper. L1: a load reply that reuses a handle still live in the same helper ends the helper. P3: a discarded helper records its own exit only if it exited by itself; otherwise the stats say the app ended it, and the reason is the recorded failure (a hang no longer reads as a resource-limit exit). P3: the wall-clock bounds in the T3, T4 and T5 tests are relative or widened. I1: the helper is a crash and memory boundary, not a privilege sandbox (it runs with the user's token, integrity level, file and network access); the module documentation says so and neither README nor docs claim otherwise. Not changed: `reset_after_stop` and `retry` still have no caller (step 4/7). Evidence (source build, Windows, debug unless noted): 4 new tests (release ignores the flag, path-carrying init refused, frames while idle end the helper, reused handle ends the helper), helper tests 23/23 three runs; the release-profile run of the flag test passes; the debug exe in helper mode binds its own PDFium on an empty init, loads a PDF and exits 0, and exits 3 with no reply to an init carrying a path; `cargo check --release` adds no warnings; parallel `cargo test --bins` 363 passed, 0 failed, 25 ignored; `run-native-recovery-gates.ps1` exits 0 (serialized 363 passed, 0 failed, 25 ignored; all integration targets; six probes).

- Isolated PDF-engine helper, step 3: helper mode, protocol and supervision behind a setting that is off (SM5; no user-visible change, `a813abe`, `6c053ba`). New `src-tauri/src/pdf_helper/`. `main()` checks for `--pdf-engine-helper` as the only argument before Tauri starts and runs the helper loop instead (no window): it calls `harden_job_child_process` and arms the allocator there only, exits with code 2 unless stdin and stdout are pipes, then binds PDFium from the path the app sends and answers requests. The exe's global allocator is `child_job::ModeSwitchAllocator`, which is the system allocator unchanged until the helper arms it (a global allocator cannot depend on the launch mode; the flag is read only after a refused allocation, so the app process behaves exactly as before). Protocol: a sync marker, then length-prefixed frames with version, kind and sequence; requests mirror `PdfEngine`/`EngineDocument` one to one; each reply's length is checked against a per-request maximum before anything is allocated, and decoders bound counts and strings, require exact consumption and check content against the request (page count, echoed ids, PNG header size, error class). Supervision: the helper runs in a hardened job capped at `HELPER_MEMORY_LIMIT` (1 GiB); `limit_violation()` is checked after every reply and a violation discards the reply and the helper; an exit, crash, missed per-request deadline, malformed or oversized frame, or cancelled pipe I/O fails the request and ends the helper; exits are also detected from the process handle, so a pipe end inherited by another process cannot hold a request; restarts back off (immediate, then 100 ms doubling to 2 s) and stop after 3 in 60 s. `RemotePdfEngine` implements `PdfEngine` over the helper; a document whose request was in flight when a helper died is not reloaded until retried, and a second death closes its engine side. The setting: `service.rs` now uses `pdf_helper::SelectedEngine`, which is the in-process engine unless a debug build runs with `SMACROBAT_PDF_ENGINE_HELPER=1`; release builds compile the check to off and no command can change it. Sources and bitmaps still cross the pipe; shared sections and routing come in step 4. Evidence (source build, Windows, debug): 19 new tests, 3 runs 19/19 each (exit mid-request fails in 13-93 ms; an 800 ms hang fails at 809-841 ms; helper gone 10-21 ms after its host is killed; an inheritable event stays unsignalled by the helper; ten malformed replies each end the helper and fail only that request; a reply sent after a refused allocation is discarded; crash loop stops after 3 restarts; `RemotePdfEngine` byte-identical to `InProcessEngine` on 8 fixtures, 288 renders and 673 checks); no new crash dumps or error reports on C:; the debug exe in helper mode answers hello (own pid), init and load over pipes and exits 0, and exits 2 when stdin is not a pipe; parallel `cargo test --bins` 359 passed, 0 failed, 25 ignored; `run-native-recovery-gates.ps1` exits 0 (serialized 359 passed, 0 failed, 25 ignored; all integration targets; six probes); `npm run build` passes.

- Redaction drift guard tightened after SM7 review B-1 (SM5; test only, `08ce409`). `worker_render_path_matches_this_replica` in `src-tauri/tests/redaction_adversarial.rs` now checks the print flags only inside the real `render_bgra_into` body and the viewer flags only inside `render_viewer_png` (the last definitions before the `#[cfg(test)]` module, so the equivalence-test replica in `pdf_engine.rs` cannot satisfy it), with self-checks that each slice is the real method. Evidence: `cargo test --test redaction_adversarial` 33 passed; `cargo test --bins pdf_engine` 1 passed; with `use_print_quality(true)` removed from the real Print branch the guard fails ("print render flags drifted"), and passes again once restored.

- PDF engine boundary inside the pdf-worker (SM5, isolated-helper step 2; refactor, no intended behaviour change). New `src-tauri/src/pdf_engine.rs` defines `PdfEngine` (load from bytes, with the password case typed) and `EngineDocument` (page count and sizes, protection, planned page size, viewer PNG render, BGRA render into a caller-provided buffer, visible text, text geometry, bookmarks, properties, crop and comment geometry, form values, output probes), implemented by `InProcessEngine` with the existing PDFium code moved verbatim. `service.rs` no longer imports `pdfium_render`: open, view/read, edit helpers, output validation, print, page-image export, raster redaction and OCR rasterisation all go through the trait, in five request-group commits plus a final one that removes the transitional access. Error messages and check order are kept; the `RasterRedact` drift guard in `tests/redaction_adversarial.rs` now reads the render flags from `pdf_engine.rs`. Also two step-1 review follow-ups in `child_job.rs`: a pipe worker that cannot get its own thread handle now does no blocking I/O (bounded, reported as `io_cancelled`), and `harden_job_child_process` is documented and debug-asserted to run once from a child's entry. Evidence (source build, Windows, debug unless noted): parallel `cargo test --bins` 340 passed, 0 failed, 25 ignored; `run-native-recovery-gates.ps1` exits 0 (serialized unit suite 340 passed, 0 failed, 25 ignored; every integration target and the six exact probes pass); `npm run build` passes; a new child-process test renders 112 pages (4 fixtures, every quarter turn, with and without crop, print and plain flags) byte-identical through the new caller-buffer path and the previous `render_with_config` path; release timing probe (median of 9) viewer renders 800/1,600/3,000 px unchanged within run-to-run noise (about ±10%), print renders at a 2400x3200 box 3-7 ms faster on vector pages (one fewer full-bitmap copy). Installed-app behaviour was not exercised.

- Merge follow-up for SM5 step 1 on top of SM6 (SM0): removed three leftover merge-conflict markers from this list, and regenerated the dependency-notices inventory because SM5 added `windows` crate features to `src-tauri/Cargo.toml` after SM6 regenerated it. Only the recorded `src-tauri/Cargo.toml` input hash changed; `node scripts/dependency-notices.mjs --check` passes (369 packages, 0 review items).

- Wording fixes after review of the OCR default-installer build side (SM6; documentation only). The README OCR feature headings now say "planned for the default installer from 0.3.0" instead of reading as a shipped claim, and `docs/license-inventory.md` describes where the OCR licence texts were copied from without a local machine path. No code or test changed.

- Recorded a local OCR build check and the size estimate for OCR in the default installer (SM6; documentation only, `docs/releases.md` § OCR in the default installer). On `2e7937f`, a release `cargo build --features tauri/custom-protocol` with `PDF_WORKSTATION_OCR_SETUP_ROOT` pointing at a local `setup-ocr.ps1` root copied exactly the engine, the English model and the three licence texts to `target/release/resources/ocr/<engine SHA-256>/`, and the app executable embeds that engine identity; the same build without the setup root does not. The five files are 8,527,213 bytes, 3,012,619 bytes as solid LZMA; the app executable grew by 7,168 bytes. Expected installer growth is about 3 MB, an estimate: no installer was built (the bounded installer proof was not run locally) and the app was not launched. The local engine hash is per-build evidence, not a release pin.

- Build side of OCR in the default installer (SM6; source build, workflow and tests; signed release path unchanged). Joshua ruled on 2026-10-14 that every installer includes Tesseract OCR from 0.3.0. `scripts/dependency-notices.mjs` now adds three `ocr-engine` records (Tesseract 5.5.3 Apache-2.0, Leptonica 1.87.0 BSD-2-Clause, tessdata_fast English 4.1.0 Apache-2.0) whose license texts live in `scripts/notice-supplements/` and must match the size and SHA-256 pins in `scripts/ocr/pins.json`, which joins the inventory's `inputHashes`; the inventory and notices were regenerated (369 records, 0 review items). Because the bundled notices now carry those texts, `notices.rs` returns the bundled text unchanged in every build and no longer appends the OCR licence sidecars (which stay packaged beside the engine). The manual `windows-validation.yml` now runs `scripts/setup-ocr.ps1` from its pinned inputs and `build-installer.ps1 -UnsignedLocal -OcrSetupRoot`, and fails unless the extraction proof packages the five OCR resources (timeout 60 to 90 minutes); it has not run hosted. `release.yml` is not changed: the Azure-signed release path still refuses an OCR setup, and wiring it in is a step of the release cut, done with the rest of signing; the engine identity still comes only from the setup manifest of the build (the signed engine's hash for a signed build). The engine links the Visual C++ runtime statically (`setup-ocr.ps1` refuses any import other than `KERNEL32.DLL`), so no redistributable DLL ships; see `docs/license-inventory.md`. Docs: `docs/releases.md` § OCR in the default installer (local steps), README OCR feature lines. Evidence: vitest, 11 files single-thread, 59 passed; `node scripts/dependency-notices.mjs --check` passed (369 packages); full parallel `cargo test --bins` 322 passed, 24 ignored (including the 4 rewritten `notices` tests); `npm run build` passed.

- `src-tauri/src/child_job.rs` review fixes (SM5). A one-shot child run can no longer hang when a pipe stays open after the child is gone, for example because another process inherited a pipe end: once the child has ended (exit, timeout, cancellation or a limit), the pipe threads get a 2 s grace period, then their blocked reads and writes are cancelled (`CancelSynchronousIo`, repeated until they return) and the run reports `io_cancelled`; OCR turns that into an error ("OCR output pipes did not close") instead of accepting partial text. Waits for a terminated process are capped at 5 s. Crash hardening is now described exactly: covered are error dialogs (`SetErrorMode`, and `WerSetFlags(WER_FAULT_REPORTING_NO_UI)` in the child), unhandled SEH exceptions (the child's filter writes one marker line to stderr and exits with the exception code before Windows Error Reporting sees it) and refused allocations (exit `0xE0000A11`); not covered are fail-fast exits (`__fastfail`, which Rust's `abort` uses, `/GS` stack-cookie failures, heap-corruption detection), which bypass exception filters and can still make Windows Error Reporting queue a report on the system drive. Exit codes from `0x80000000` up (for example `STATUS_BREAKPOINT`) are classified as exceptions. Evidence (source debug build, one Windows 11 machine): 3 new tests keep a duplicate pipe end open (stdout after a normal exit, stdout past a 300 ms timeout, stdin under a 4 MiB write) and each run returns well within its timeout with `io_cancelled`; the exception test now asserts the filter's marker, so it fails if only the job's own handling ran; `cargo test --bins child_job` 16 passed three times in a row, no Windows Error Reporting entries appeared; `cargo test --bins ocr` 36 passed / 5 ignored, 43 passed with a local engine; parallel `cargo test --bins` 340 passed / 0 failed / 24 ignored; `npm run build` passes.

- Moved the Job Object and child-process code out of `src-tauri/src/ocr_process.rs` into a new shared module, `src-tauri/src/child_job.rs` (SM5, step 1 of the isolated PDF-engine helper; no helper process yet). It holds process creation (suspended, no window, an explicit inherited-handle list of the three pipes), the job (kill on job close, process and job commit limits, one active process), assignment before resume, bounded stdout/stderr capture, timeout, cancellation and whole-job termination, and exit-code and peak-commit reads. OCR uses it with the same limits (256 MiB, one process), arguments, identity checks and error messages; the change in `ocr_process.rs` is the call into the module. New, off for OCR, for the future helper: optional `DIE_ON_UNHANDLED_EXCEPTION`, job UI restrictions, and an I/O completion port whose memory and process-count messages are read back as a typed limit violation (sticky, because Windows sends each once per process) that also ends a one-shot child; in-child hardening (`SetErrorMode`, an unhandled-exception filter that exits with the exception code, an allocator that exits with `0xE0000A11` when an allocation is refused) so an unhandled exception or a refused allocation does not reach Windows Error Reporting (fail-fast exits still can; see the next entry); and `HELPER_MEMORY_LIMIT` = 1 GiB in `open_limits.rs`, unused by the app. Evidence (source debug build, one Windows 11 machine): 13 new unit tests using the test binary itself as the child (exit code and stdin, timeout, cancellation, exact stdout cap, a memory-limit message under a 64 MiB test cap ending the child, the allocation-failure exit code, an unhandled exception's code, the one-process limit reported, drop and owner-exit kill, the helper limits read back from the job) pass, and no Windows Error Reporting entries appeared during them; `cargo test --bins ocr` 36 passed / 5 ignored, and with `PDF_WORKSTATION_OCR_SETUP_ROOT` set to a local engine 43 passed including the real-engine tests; parallel `cargo test --bins` 337 passed / 0 failed / 24 ignored (a first parallel run had one failure in an existing OCR test, whose fake child's `child.pid` was read while still empty, and four follow-on `Busy` failures; it passed on rerun); `scripts/run-native-recovery-gates.ps1` exit 0 (serialized main suite 337 / 0 / 24, every integration target and required probe passed); `npm run build` passes.

- Added `scripts/verify-native-page-dialogs.ps1`, a local UI Automation check of the real native file dialogs behind page tools (SM2; debug build from this tree, run on one Windows 11 machine, not in CI). It starts a copy of the debug executable with the web view data redirected to a work folder on E:, opens two generated PDFs, then drives Open, Combine, Insert Pages, Replace Pages, Split, Save a copy after a crop, Export page as image, Create PDF from one image and from several images. For each dialog it asserts the title, default file name, that the dialog is owned by the app window and the main window is disabled while it is open, then tests Cancel (same tabs, nothing written, main window enabled) and a typed E: path, and reopens the output with PDFium (page count and sizes) or decodes the PNG (625x834). Result of the last run: 96 pass, 0 fail, 7 not automatable (the Save As "save as type" combo is not exposed to UI Automation, so only the Open and image-pick filters are asserted). Findings, no behaviour changed: Split's folder chooser is a Save As dialog (`src-tauri/src/main.rs:300`) and Save a copy sets no dialog title (`src-tauri/src/main.rs:197`). The app keeps its recovery records under the per-user app-data folder, which cannot be redirected, so a run leaves small `.smacrec` files there. After the review the work root is confined to the repo's sibling `tmp` folder (default `tmp\sm2-dialogs\run-<time>`; other drives and UNC paths are refused), the two unconditional passes (combine preview, crop) now assert or log as info, a `-Strict` switch exits 2 when anything was not automatable, and the leftover-process check is skipped if the app never started; these edits were checked by a PowerShell parse and the vitest only, not a new app run. Pinned by `scripts/native-page-dialogs-verifier.test.ts`. Not exercised: Redact, Fill forms and image-replace save dialogs, installed or signed builds, high-DPI or other Windows versions.

- Added `scripts/verify-native-annotate-dialogs.ps1` (SM3; source debug build, not wired into CI). It launches a copy of the debug build (build it with `cargo build --features tauri/custom-protocol`, otherwise the window loads the dev URL), drives only that process through UI Automation (MSAA actions for the classic file dialog controls, no keyboard or mouse input), and checks each native dialog is owned by and modal to the app window and has the expected title, default file name and filter, that typing an E: path writes a PDF that PDFium opens with the expected content, and that Cancel writes nothing. The work root must be a new folder under the sibling `tmp` folder (default `tmp\sm3-dialogs\run-<time>`, trailing-separator prefix check, no UNC or other drives); each attempt uses a fresh `attempt-N` folder inside it and the script deletes nothing there; `-FixtureDir` is confined to `tmp` or the repo and `-AppPath`/`-PdfiumPath` to the repo or the shared cargo target; the WebView2 profile is set to `WEBVIEW2_USER_DATA_FOLDER` inside the run folder. The debug app shares the real `%APPDATA%\local.pdfworkstation.desktop\recovery` folder, so the script refuses to start when any `.smacrec` record already exists, closes (Discard and close) only the tabs it opened from its own input folder, answers a recovery offer only for such a document, and afterwards removes only record files that appeared during its own run (left in place if another dev build was running). It waits at most 30 minutes for, and aborts when it sees, any other `pdf-workstation` process whatever its path (an installed app shares the recovery folder); recovery records that appeared during a run are deleted only when no other such process was seen at any point, otherwise they are left in place and reported as unattributable (record contents are not read); `-Strict` exits non-zero when a check is not automatable. Last run on the debug build of `fe82e71` plus the verifier files, before these confinement changes (the changes were reviewed by reading and the pin test only, the app was not relaunched): 86 pass, 0 fail, 3 not automatable. Passed: Save a Copy of an annotated fixture after editing the sticky note in the app (note text, area highlight and text highlight read back with PDFium annotation APIs); Fill forms copies for text and checkbox, radio group and dropdown (field values and appearance states read back); strict text replacement (page text reads `67890`); strict image replacement, including the image picker, which opened only after the live document check (the unsupported document opened no dialog), then the save dialog (rendered pixels are the replacement colours). Not automatable: creating a sticky note or area highlight (pointer-only placement, `CommentLayer.tsx`) and a text highlight (the text layer is `aria-hidden`, `TextLayer.tsx:107`, so UI Automation cannot select it); the annotated fixture is written by the app's own annotation writer through the ignored test `comments::tests::write_dialog_annotation_fixture`, and the strict text and image fixtures by the new ignored `src-tauri/tests/dialog_fixtures.rs`. Observation: the Open and Save a Copy dialogs set no title, so Windows shows "Open" and "Save As" (`main.rs` `open_document`, `save_copy`). The script pins itself with `scripts/verify-native-annotate-dialogs.test.ts`.

- Fixed the searchable OCR PDFium integration test failing the native gate (SM4; tests only). `src-tauri/tests/searchable_pdfium.rs` called `Pdfium::bind_to_library` in each of its two tests, and PDFium bindings can be initialised once per process, so the second binding failed with `PdfiumLibraryBindingsAlreadyInitialized` under `scripts/run-native-recovery-gates.ps1` (`--test-threads=1`). Both tests now share one `OnceLock<Pdfium>` helper, as `redaction_adversarial.rs` does; assertions are unchanged. Evidence: `cargo test --test searchable_pdfium` passes 2 of 2.

- Added open-time lopdf load limits and a linear unreachable-object prune (SM5; source build and tests). Every open (plain, password, recovery restore) now loads the source through the new `src-tauri/src/pdf_load.rs` before PDFium, with limits next to the open limits in `open_limits.rs`: more than 1,000,000 indirect-object headers are refused before lopdf parses anything (lopdf's own repair scan stops at the same number), any stream decoded during the load stops at 32 MiB, object streams are measured before lopdf decodes them against a 256 MiB total and the object limit, and object streams whose entries overlap are refused. Each refusal ends "Nothing was opened." and keeps no document. Structural export (delete, move, extract, split) and Combine now prune with the forms reachability walk (moved to `pdf_load.rs`) instead of lopdf's quadratic `prune_objects`; the kept object set is identical on every fixture. Debug-build measurements: a 6.8 MB object-stream bomb that inflates to 1 GiB went from loading at a 1,675 MiB peak working set to refused in 0.25 s at 75 MiB; 1,000,001 tiny objects from 4.1 s and 363 MiB to refused in 0.23 s; 3,000 overlapping entries from 3.8 s and 1,130 MiB to 4 ms; pruning 200,000 objects from 190 s to 0.29 s; five real scanned PDFs of 151–538 MB load with the same objects. PDFium alone opened the 1 GiB bomb at a 2.1 GiB peak; the lopdf check now refuses it first.
