Encrypted sources get only the object count and per-stream cap (lopdf's encrypted loader skips the measuring filter), recorded in `docs/review-findings.md`. 11 new tests plus one service open test; parallel `cargo test --bins` 320 passed, 23 ignored; `run-native-recovery-gates.ps1` passed (serialized main suite 320 passed, 23 ignored; all integration targets and required probes passed); `npm run build` passed. Installed-app behavior is not exercised.

- Bookmarks now honour their destination's position and zoom (SM1; source build and tests). The native bookmark list returns a parsed `destination` per entry: mode (`xyz`, `fit`, `fit_h`, `fit_v`, `fit_r`, `fit_b`, `fit_bh`, `fit_bv`, or `top` as the fallback), the point or rectangle in PDF user space, the zoom, and the page's CropBox origin, size and effective rotation (including unsaved turns and crop edits). Named destinations (catalog `/Dests` and the name tree) and `/GoTo` actions resolve through PDFium. Coordinates clamp to ±1,000,000, zoom to 0.1-4.0, and a malformed view, short `/FitR` array, unknown mode or non-finite value becomes `top`. The viewer (`src/bookmarkDestination.ts`, `Viewer.tsx`) turns the point into the displayed page for all four rotations, applies the zoom or fit through the existing zoom state, and scrolls once the new scale has laid out. `cargo test --bins`: 312 passed, 0 failed, 22 ignored (3 pure destination tests and 1 service test over every mode, a named destination, a rotated cropped page and malformed values are new); vitest for the touched files 24 of 24 pass; `npm run build` ok. No GUI was run, so the scroll and zoom feel in the app is unverified.

- Gave the slow cases of `scripts/installed-print-dialog-workflow.test.ts` and `scripts/installed-reading-tools-workflow.test.ts` an explicit 30 s timeout (SM1; tests only), the same per-case pattern `signed-reading-tools-workflow.test.ts` uses. These cases spawn `pwsh.exe` with a 30 s process timeout but ran under vitest's 5 s default, so they timed out under machine load. CI configuration is unchanged. Both files, single-thread: 48 of 48 pass in about 60 s.

- Gave the searchable OCR copy a Unicode invisible text layer (SM4; opt-in source build). `searchable_pdf.rs` now writes one Type0 Identity-H font per document with an embedded, code-generated TrueType program (one box glyph, never painted in render mode 3) and a `/ToUnicode` CMap with UTF-16BE destinations, so accented, typographic and non-BMP characters reach copy and search; it replaces non-embedded Helvetica/WinAnsi and the printable-ASCII refusal. Words are refused, not dropped, for controls, whitespace, invisible format or bidi controls, private use, noncharacters or U+FFFD, over 512 characters, or past 8,192 distinct characters per document; the 32-page, 33,554,432-pixel, 100,000-word and 8 MiB limits are unchanged. Latin ligatures U+FB00 to U+FB06 are expanded to letters at TSV parse time, because PDFium extracts them as letters. Each word now takes its TSV line box as its vertical band (tesseract's renderer does the same), which keeps a thin word such as an em dash on its line in extraction. Text content streams are Flate-compressed; fixed overhead is about 1.1 KB per document. The capability string becomes `Unicode words without control or whitespace characters` (Rust, `searchableOcr.ts`, `App.tsx`, tests) and the dialog wording follows. Evidence, synthetic only: Rust `searchable_pdf` 8, `searchable_ocr` 16 and `searchable_pdfium` 2 pass, including exact PDFium extraction of accented, ligature, dash and non-BMP words with every character box inside its word span and line band; `cargo test --bins` 308 pass, 22 ignored; 25 Vitest tests across the 4 touched files pass; `npm run build` passes. A direct run of the 2026-10-13 engine on two accented GDI+ images found that the app's `tsv` config argument is missing from the packaged `tessdata` and that the parser's paragraph check refuses some real TSV; both are recorded in `docs/ocr-feasibility.md` and not changed here. Extraction in viewers other than PDFium, real scans and multi-column order are not verified.

- Made the release version tests follow the version sources (SM6; tests only). `scripts/release-workflow.test.ts` and the source-version check in `scripts/default-draft-verification-workflow.test.ts` now read the current version from `package.json` instead of hard-coding 0.2.12, and use `0.0.0` as the mismatch value instead of 9.9.9, which a future release could have taken. The six-source agreement check and the release-notes heading check now compare against that version; synthetic asset-URL fixtures keep their fixed literals. Both files: 12 tests passed (`--no-file-parallelism`).

- Removed the local release path from `docs/releases.md` § Release steps (SM6; documentation only). The steps no longer build release assets with `npm run installer` or offer a `gh release create` fallback, which would have bypassed the `release` environment approval, the `master`-ancestor guard, Azure publisher signing and the publish job's rechecks, and needed the updater key locally. The steps now list all six version sources and the notices regeneration, and a "Not for releases" note marks a local installer as a development build. No test pins this text.

- Bumped `rustls` 0.23.45 to 0.23.46 in `src-tauri/Cargo.lock` (SM6; source build and tests), fixing RUSTSEC-2026-0346, 0347, 0348 and 0349 in the updater's HTTPS stack (`tauri-plugin-updater` via `reqwest`). `cargo update -p rustls` moved only that package; the dependency notices and inventory were regenerated for the new version and lockfile hash. `cargo audit`: no vulnerabilities (7 allowed warnings, unmaintained or unsound advisories on other crates). Full parallel `cargo test --bins`: 308 passed, 22 ignored; `update_commands` tests: 26 passed. `node scripts/dependency-notices.mjs --check` (366 packages, 0 review items) and `npm run build` passed. Installed-app update behavior is not exercised.

- Hardened Fill forms copies (SM3; source build and tests). Large lopdf-generated fixtures reproduced two defects. First, a filled copy kept a changed or cleared text/choice field's replaced appearance stream, and objects from superseded incremental revisions, as unreferenced objects, so a cleared value stayed recoverable from the copy. `FormDocument::prepare` now writes only objects reachable from the trailer. Second, the lenient 1 MiB-per-stream loader silently dropped an oversized object stream or a malformed object that the copy would then have omitted. `FormDocument::parse` now refuses the whole form when any in-use cross-reference entry did not load. New `src-tauri/src/forms_hardening_tests.rs` (8 tests, 3 ignored measurement probes) also turns the hypotheses that did not reproduce into regression tests: field and widget budgets on any page layout up to 4,096 pages, shared, orphaned and non-widget annotations, deep and cyclic hierarchies, inheritance, reference cycles, 100,000-entry options, 1 MiB values and names, malformed and bomb appearances, duplicate names, NeedAppearances, RC4 encryption, and object/xref-stream sources. A new worker test fills 256 fields over 16 pages through `FillFormCopy`. `docs/review-findings.md` § Open risks records the measurements and the remaining load-time limits. `cargo test --bins -- forms choices radios checkboxes`: 43 passed, 4 ignored. Full parallel `cargo test --bins`: 308 passed, 22 ignored. `npm run build` passed. Installed-app behavior is not exercised.

- Extended the adversarial raster-redaction tests (SM7; test-only change, shipped behavior unchanged; follow-up to the code and security reviews of `7f43429..7095acc`). Three new `documents_leak_*` tests cover the reverse of the print-only case: a cover that hides text in the viewer but is dropped by the `FPDF_PRINTING` redaction render, so the covered text is rasterised into the copy unmarked. The covers are an opaque Square annotation and an opaque FreeText annotation without the Print flag, and an optional-content layer that is on for View and `/PrintState /OFF`; each leaks 1,875 pixels of the secret at 150 DPI while the viewer render shows none. A new source-text guard, `worker_render_path_matches_this_replica`, fails if the `RasterRedact` worker in `service.rs` changes its render flags, 150 DPI sizing, `bgra_to_rgb` conversion or the viewer render flags the tests model. There is no per-push CI: these tests run wherever the full native suite runs, which is `scripts/run-native-recovery-gates.ps1` (`cargo test --locked`, all targets) in the manual Windows validation, tag-triggered release and manual signed-OCR workflows. `cargo test --test redaction_adversarial`: 33 passed (29 plus 4 included `page_image` unit tests), parallel and `--test-threads=1`.

- Added adversarial raster-redaction evidence (SM7; test-only change, shipped behavior unchanged). New `src-tauri/tests/redaction_adversarial.rs` builds 2-page PDFs from literal syntax with a secret string, runs each through the same PDFium render flags, 150 DPI page sizes, `redact_raster_pages` and `verify_clean_raster_pdf` as the `RasterRedact` worker (unedited document, no session page plan or comment overlay), and compares against a control build with the secret blanked. 25 tests cover page text, ToUnicode-only text, `3 Tr`, white-on-white, 1 pt and shape-covered text, a reused Form XObject, FreeText, Text with an open Popup, form-field `/V` and appearances (normal, Hidden, NoView+Print), `/Info` and XMP, outlines and named destinations, embedded files, JavaScript, `/Thumb`, an earlier incremental revision, `/Alt` and `/ActualText`, hidden and print-only optional content, `/Rotate 90` and an offset CropBox. In every case the secret is absent from the output bytes (raw and decompressed, ASCII, UTF-16 and hex), PDFium extracts no text, and only `/Type` and `/Pages` remain in the catalog. Two vectors put pixels the viewer never showed into the output outside every mark, because redaction renders with `FPDF_PRINTING` and the viewer does not: an optional-content layer that is off on screen but `/PrintState /ON`, and a FreeText annotation flagged NoView+Print. Those two tests (`documents_leak_*`) assert the current behavior. A Form XObject reused on an unmarked page stays visible there, as the one-page marking contract states. `cargo test --test redaction_adversarial`: 29 passed (25 plus 4 included `page_image` unit tests); `--test raster_redaction` 4 passed.

- Added an OCR accuracy harness (SM4; source build, tests and one synthetic run only). `scripts/ocr-accuracy.mjs` reads a manifest of image and typed ground-truth pairs, verifies the built engine root against its setup manifest (same pins, model hash, executable receipt and recipe inputs as `ocr_build.rs`), runs the engine with the arguments from `ocr_process.rs` (`--oem 1 --psm 6 --dpi 150`, P6 on stdin), and prints and writes CER and WER on normalised text (normalisation in [docs/ocr-accuracy.md](ocr-accuracy.md)). Inputs are PNG or PNM; PDF pages must be exported to images first, and a `sourceDpi` entry is area-averaged to 150 DPI by the harness's own resampler, not the app's PDFium render. `scripts/ocr/generate-accuracy-fixtures.ps1` renders six synthetic variants (four fonts, one small size, one skewed and noisy) from `scripts/ocr/accuracy-synthetic.json` with GDI+; no image is committed. `scripts/ocr/accuracy.test.ts` has 27 engine-free tests (maths, manifest, decoding, symbolic-link and manifest-size refusals, drift guards against `ocr_process.rs` and `ocr_build.rs`) plus one end-to-end case that runs only with `PDF_WORKSTATION_OCR_SETUP_ROOT`; all 28 passed with a 2026-10-13 engine root, and `npm run build` passed (before the review follow-up). On the synthetic set the total was CER 2.19% and WER 8.48% (Arial 12, Times 11 and Courier 10 at 0.00% CER, Verdana 14 0.63%, Arial 9 0.94%, skewed and noisy variant 11.60%); that is clean computer-rendered text on one machine and says nothing about scans. No real-scan accuracy has been measured.

- Fixed the parallel native-test flake in `text_highlights_mixed_corpus_structural_copies_reopen_and_source_preservation` (SM5; test-only change, shipped behavior unchanged). Cause: `unchanged_recovery_checkpoint_skips_the_journal_write` sends `CheckpointRecovery` to the shared in-process `PDF_SERVICE` on a byte-identical copy of `welcome.pdf`, so while it ran the worker refused edits to every other open tab with that source identity ("Another open tab owns recovery for this PDF. Close or resolve it before editing this tab."), which is the intended rule for two tabs of one PDF. Running the two tests together with `--test-threads=2` failed 6 of 6 times before the fix and passed 5 of 5 after. The checkpoint test now appends a trailing PDF comment to its copy, so its source identity is unique; no other shared-worker test creates a recovery head. Evidence: parallel `cargo test --bins` 299 passed / 0 failed / 19 ignored three runs in a row, `--test-threads=1` 299/0/19, `scripts/run-native-recovery-gates.ps1` exit 0.

- Image replacement now checks the live document before the image picker opens (SM3; source build, tests only; code review and SM7 review clear). `replace_pdf_image_copy` calls the new `PdfService::image_replacement_source_via`, which runs the worker's image-replacement inspection (live revision and target) before the picker closure, so a stale selection is refused with "Document changed. Inspect the image again." without showing the picker. The later check before the save dialog and the worker's write-time check are unchanged. Test `image_replacement_refuses_a_stale_revision_before_the_image_picker_opens` proves stale, unsupported and closed requests make zero picker calls. Native picker behavior in the installed app remains unverified.

- A dropped network file whose name cannot be represented now gets its own message (SM1; source build, message-only change, code review and SM7 review clear, not pushed). A UNC path from the webview origin that contains U+FFFD (what a lone surrogate becomes) is still refused, since `grant_key` never records it, but the error now says the name contains characters PDF Workstation cannot handle for a network path and to pick the file in the Open dialog (true for a drop and for a recent-files reopen), instead of the generic network-path message. Decided at classify time (`webview_unc_refusal` in `open_path.rs`), which keeps the grant and consume logic and the gate's accept/reject results untouched; the text still starts "This path cannot be opened", so the `App.tsx` reopen routing is unchanged. New test `lossy_unc_name_gets_its_own_message_and_is_still_refused`. `cargo test --bins open_path` 17 passed; `cargo test --bins` 298 passed, 0 failed, 19 ignored; `npm run build` ok. A real drop of such a file was not exercised.

- Stale-check follow-ups for save-copy and split (SM2; source build, tests only plus one message restore; code review and SM7 review clear). A stale split refused before the folder dialog again says "Document changed. Start splitting again." (`PdfService::split_via`, mirroring `save_copy_via`); the frontend shows the rejection text verbatim. The closed-document test case uses `u64::MAX` and the tests close their document. New tests: stale split refused with zero dialogs and no output, and a save whose revision changes while the dialog is open is refused by `SaveAt` and writes no file. `cargo test --bins` 291 passed, 0 failed, 19 ignored. The real rfd dialogs were not exercised.

- The opt-in `ocr_commands` tests now find the opt-in OCR resources under the real Cargo profile directory (SM4; test-only change, source build, code review and SM7 review clear). They looked in `src-tauri/target/debug`, which is wrong whenever `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` is set (the shared target on E:). `ocr_build.rs` places the resources at `<target>/<profile>/resources/ocr/<sha>`, and the test binary lives in `<target>/<profile>/deps`, so the tests derive the directory from `std::env::current_exe()`. With a fresh engine setup root, `cargo test --bin pdf-workstation ocr -- --include-ignored` passed 43/43 including the 4 `ocr_commands` tests; normal `cargo test ocr` is 36 passed, 5 ignored. The `ocr_build` tests still run twice (once in the bin, once via `tests/ocr_build.rs` including the same file); left as is.

- The release `publish` job now rechecks the manifest's installer URL and the signature's trusted-comment file name too, so assets swapped after the installer job's check cannot reach the draft (SM6, review S-2 on `0f3d32e..8bf12b1`; source only, never run). The `release` environment now exists (2026-10-14) with Joshua Beel as required reviewer and a `v*` tag rule; moving the signing secrets into it is still Joshua's. Evidence: workflow Vitest files from pwsh, see SM6 charter Open 17.

- The updater now refuses an offer whose signature was made for a different installer (SM6, SM7 U-1; source build; code review and SM7 review clear). The minisign signature on every update carries a signed trusted comment naming the file it was made for (`file:PDF Workstation_<version>_x64-setup.exe`). The updater used to compare only the manifest's version label, so an older signed installer relabelled with a higher version would have been offered and installed. `check_for_update` now decodes the offered signature and requires exactly one `file:` name equal to `PDF Workstation_<offered version>_x64-setup.exe`, or it refuses with "The update signature does not match the offered version". The check runs on the signature text before anything is downloaded; the plugin's download-time verification covers that comment through the global signature, so it cannot be altered. Builds for platforms with no published installer accept no update. The `latest.json` format is unchanged, so installed 0.2.0 clients still update; they do not get this check. New direct dependencies `base64` 0.22.1 and `minisign-verify` 0.2.5 were already in the lockfile through `tauri-plugin-updater`; the notice inventory's input hashes are regenerated (366 packages, text unchanged). Evidence: `cargo test --bin pdf-workstation update_commands` 26/26 (6 new, including the published 0.2.0 signature accepted for 0.2.0 and refused for 9.9.9, 0.2.1, 0.2.13, 0.3.0 and other platforms, plus a pin that `productName` and `release.yml` use the same installer name); `dependency-notices.mjs --check` 366/0; `npm run build` ok. Not exercised: a real update check against a published release with the new client.

- Release workflow hardened (SM6, SM7 U-2, U-3 code side, U-7, U-11; source only, never run; code review and SM7 review clear). **The next release run waits for approval in the GitHub environment `release`** (created 2026-10-14 with a required reviewer and a `v*` tag rule; the signing secrets still need moving into it; see [docs/releases.md](releases.md#release-environment-and-gates)). `release.yml` changes: workflow default `permissions: {}`; the `installer` job runs in `environment: release` with `contents: read` and `persist-credentials: false` on checkout; validation now fails unless the tagged commit is an ancestor of `origin/master`; `TAURI_SIGNING_PRIVATE_KEY` reaches only the signing build step (validation sees only whether it is set); the never-created `TAURI_SIGNING_PRIVATE_KEY_PASSWORD` secret reference is replaced by an explicit empty value, which is what it already evaluated to; the asset check also requires the updater signature's trusted comment to name exactly `PDF Workstation_<version>_x64-setup.exe`. A new `publish` job (`needs: installer`, `contents: write`) checks out nothing and runs no npm, Cargo or repository script: it downloads the `windows-installer` and new `release-notes` artifacts, rechecks the three names and the manifest's version, signature and notes binding, and creates the draft with the unchanged `--verify-tag`/asset checks; only that step gets `GH_TOKEN`. The `release` environment is on the `installer` job only, so one approval covers a run. `docs/releases.md` now names environment secrets. Evidence: Vitest (release-workflow, workflow-action-pins, azure-signing-config, signed-ocr-artifact-workflow, signed-ocr-infrastructure, release-manifest, signed-in-app-update-workflow, Updates, updateCommands, capabilities, dependency-notices) 73/73 from pwsh, 2 new release-workflow cases; the PowerShell trusted-comment check run locally on the published 0.2.0 signature accepts 0.2.0 and refuses 9.9.9. Not exercised: any GitHub run, the environment gate, the cross-job artifact handoff.

- Parallel `cargo test` no longer trips the open-document cap (SM0 combined check on `f28db0f`; test-only behaviour, shipped caps unchanged). The in-process tests share one process-global worker and many of them leave their documents open, so under default parallel threads the 32-document cap from `open_limits.rs` refused 25-27 unrelated tests ("Up to 32 PDFs can be open at once…"); `--test-threads=1` and the CI gate script were clean. The worker now receives its document count as a parameter (`start_worker_limited`), like its render and queue limits: the app always passes `MAX_OPEN_DOCUMENTS` (32), and only `cfg(test)` builds give the shared test worker 4,096. The open and copy checks in `open_limits.rs` take that count instead of reading the constant. SM5's isolated-worker cap test still starts its worker with `MAX_OPEN_DOCUMENTS` and proves open, password retry and combine are refused at 32. The 1 GiB and 4 GiB limits are untouched.

- Save a copy and Split now check the document revision before opening the native dialog (SM2, P3 from the I-6 review of `d80196d`; source build; code review and SM7 review clear). A stale revision used to be refused only after the user had picked a path. A new service-thread `CheckRevision` request runs first; the post-dialog check in `SaveAt`/`Split` stays, because the document can change while the dialog is open. Test `save_copy_refuses_a_stale_revision_before_the_dialog_opens` drives `PdfService::save_copy_via` with a counting picker and asserts zero picker calls on a stale revision. The real rfd dialog itself was not exercised.

- Forms probe tests no longer depend on run order (SM0 review P3; test-only). The choice fill test wrote its cross-engine proof files into `src-tauri/target/forms-probe` without creating the folder, so in a fresh worktree it failed when it ran before the radio or checkbox test. It now creates the folder. The choice clear test only saved its proof when another test had already made the folder; it now creates it too and always writes `choice-cleared.pdf` and its PDFium render. No app code changed.

- Open-path gate follow-ups (SM0 review P3s; source build, unit-tested only, not exercised on a real share): the network-drop grant key now lowercases only host and share and keeps the rest of the path case-exact, so a drop grant cannot be spent on a differently-cased file on a case-sensitive share; a picked or dropped UNC path on the `pipe`, `IPC$` or `mailslot` share is refused (share names are compared with trailing dots and spaces trimmed, a host starting with `;` is refused, host and share fold ASCII-only, and a grant key containing a lossy U+FFFD is never recorded).

- `offerKept` only for a key that matches the downloaded update (SM0 delta review on `7d21bec..73937e5`). The "already waiting" refusal was checked before the key, so a key with no downloaded update could get `offerKept: true`. The key is now checked first, both inside the install and in `install_update` before the main-window check, so a stale or forged key gets an ordinary error. The entry below was reworded: `offerKept` is a retry hint, and the backend re-checks the key on every install. Evidence: adjusted Rust test, `cargo test --bin pdf-workstation update_commands` 20 passed; `Updates` Vitest from pwsh. Not exercised in a running or installed app.

- Install refusals keep the downloaded update, and a failed confirmation box is not a No (SM0 code review P3s on `c0a4def..7d21bec`; source build). Software updates treated every `install_update` rejection as a failed install: it released the offer and re-checked, so the "already waiting" and missing-window refusals forced a full download on the next press. `install_update` now rejects with `{message, offerKept}`. `offerKept` is a hint that the frontend may retry without downloading again; it is set for refusals made before anything is taken or installed (second install while the box is open, missing main window, box could not be shown). The backend still checks the key against its own downloaded update on every install, so a wrong hint can only lead to a refused install, never an unchecked one. `src/updateCommands.ts` turns those into `InstallRefused`; Software updates then shows "Update not installed: …", keeps its offer, and the next press asks again without downloading. Every other rejection keeps the old release-and-re-check path, and its message reaches the UI as before. `MessageBoxW` returning 0 (box not shown, for example its owner window was destroyed) used to read as No; it is now that kept-offer error. Evidence: 2 new Rust tests, `cargo test --bin pdf-workstation update_commands` 20 passed; new Vitest cases (3 refusals in `Updates`, 1 wrapper case in `updateCommands`), `Updates`/`updateCommands`/`capabilities` 27/27 from pwsh; `npm run build`. Not exercised in a running or installed app.

- Explicit 30 s timeout on the signed reading-tools pin test (SM5 flake sweep 2026-10-08). `scripts/signed-reading-tools-workflow.test.ts` "pins the inspected signed blobs…" runs 16 `git rev-parse` processes and hit Vitest's default 5 s once under native-build load (10.1 s). Test logic unchanged. Evidence: the file's Vitest 3/3 from pwsh.

- Removed the dead npm updater case from `scripts/dependency-notices.mjs` (SM0 review P3). It copied the Rust `tauri-plugin-updater` license files into the npm `@tauri-apps/plugin-updater` record; that npm package is gone, so the branch never ran. No inventory or notices change: `node scripts/dependency-notices.mjs --check` passes unchanged (366 packages, 0 review items) and `dependency-notices` Vitest 4/4 from pwsh.

- Install after No reuses the download (SM0 review P3; source build). After the user answered No in the "Install update?" box, pressing "Install update and restart" again downloaded the same update again, although the backend had kept it. Software updates now remembers which offer it downloaded and, for that same offer, skips the download and its `downloading` recovery marker, writes the `installing` marker and asks again. The backend guarantees are unchanged: `install_update` still installs only the downloaded update it holds for the exact `{version, signature, check}` key, single use. If the backend no longer holds it, the install fails, the offer is released and re-checked as before, and the next press downloads the refreshed offer. Evidence: new `Updates` Vitest case; `Updates` and `updateCommands` Vitest 21/21 from pwsh. Not exercised in a running or installed app.

- Update confirmation refuses without its owner window (SM0 review P3, both reviewers; source build). If the main window's handle could not be found, `install_update` fell back to a null owner, so the "Install update?" box appeared ownerless and not modal to the app. It now refuses before asking: "The update confirmation could not be attached to the PDF Workstation window. Nothing was installed." A zero handle counts as missing. The backend's downloaded offer is not taken, but at this commit Software updates still treated the refusal as a failed install, released the offer and re-checked, so the next press downloaded again; fixed in the "Install refusals keep the downloaded update" entry above. Evidence: 1 new Rust test, `cargo test --bin pdf-workstation update_commands` 18 passed. Not exercised in a running or installed app.

- One update confirmation at a time (SM0 review P3 on `66e9123..c0a4def`; source build). Two `install_update` calls could each open the Windows "Install update?" box. The backend now marks the question open while it is shown and refuses a second install with "An update installation is already waiting for your answer." without showing another box. The mark is set only after the downloaded-offer and newer-version checks pass, and clears however the attempt ends (No, Yes, installer error, or a panic in the dialog). Evidence: 2 new Rust tests, `cargo test --bin pdf-workstation update_commands` 17 passed. Not exercised in a running or installed app.

- OCR local-engine tests take the executable identity from a validated setup manifest, not a source constant (SM7 answer 2026-10-13; test code and docs only, no runtime or build behavior change). The ignored tests in `ocr.rs` and `ocr_process.rs` no longer carry `VERIFIED_ENGINE*` (an unsigned local 2026-09-24 hash) or the hard-coded `target/ocr-recipe-verified-20260924` path. A test-only module (`ocr_test_support.rs`) reads `PDF_WORKSTATION_OCR_SETUP_ROOT`, runs `ocr_build.rs::verify_setup` (the build script file, included into the test crate unchanged apart from `pub(crate)` on `verify_setup`, `absolute_from` and the receipt types) and builds the identity from the verified manifest; with the variable unset the tests print `SKIPPED ...` and return. The junction-guard test now creates its own junction in a temp folder and runs by default. The existing wrong-hash refusal tests are unchanged; a new runtime test (`wrong_declared_size_is_refused_for_engine_and_model_before_process_creation`) covers the wrong-size branch of the identity gate, which had no runtime test (only the build side tested size). A setup root that is set but missing now fails the test instead of skipping. `docs/ocr-feasibility.md` gains a dated note; the 2026-09-24 receipts are untouched. Evidence (source build, one machine): `cargo test ocr` from pwsh 7, 36 passed, 5 ignored (the `ocr_build` tests also run a second time because the bin test target includes `ocr_build.rs`); `-- --ignored` without the variable, 5 skip messages and pass; after a fresh `scripts/setup-ocr.ps1 -OutputRoot target/ocr-identity-check-20261013` and the variable set, the 5 engine tests in `ocr.rs`/`ocr_process.rs` pass against the real manifest. In that opt-in build 4 ignored `ocr_commands` tests fail because they look for resources under `src-tauri/target/debug` while the shared `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` puts them in `target/debug`; that path is unchanged by this commit. The local executable hash is per-build evidence, not a trusted or shipped identity.

- Open caps now cover copies and password retries (review follow-up on `c0a4def..ddbcd3a`: code P2/P3, SM7 LOW-2/INFO-1; source build). Every copy that opens as a new tab (fill form, combine/insert/replace, create PDF from one or several images, image and text replacement, raster redaction, searchable OCR) checks the produced bytes against the same limits before the file is written: 32 open documents, 4 GiB of open sources in total, and 1 GiB for the new document itself. The 1 GiB per-file cap applies to copies because a copy is held in memory exactly like an opened file (twice, about 2 GiB at the cap) and the app could not reopen a larger file it had written; in practice each copy path already bounds its output (256 MiB output caps; raster redaction by its 32-megapixel total), so this is a backstop. A refusal reads "... No file was written. Nothing was opened." and leaves no file and no tab. Combine/insert/replace and single-image Create PDF also check the count before doing any work. `Save` (save a copy without opening it) is unchanged because it adds no tab. In the password dialog, a size or open-count refusal now closes the dialog and shows the backend's message instead of "Unable to unlock…"; only messages ending "Nothing was opened." pass through, so native errors still never reach the screen. The non-regular-file refusal now ends "Nothing was opened." too, and its comment says devices and pipes from the webview are stopped earlier by the open-path gate. The limits remain three constants at the top of `src-tauri/src/open_limits.rs`. The worker-level cap test (open, password retry and combine refused at 32) uses its own PDFium worker, so like the other isolated-worker tests it is `#[ignore]` in the parallel suite and runs alone in `scripts/run-native-recovery-gates.ps1`, which the three CI workflows already call.

- Open has a file-size cap and an open-document cap (SM7 IPC-limits finding I-2; source build). New `src-tauri/src/open_limits.rs`; the worker's `Open`, `BeginOpen`/`Unlock` (so `open_document` and `reopen_document`) and `RestoreRecovery` read the source through it instead of `std::fs::read`. Before any byte is read it refuses a new document when 32 are already open, a file over 1 GiB (size from metadata), or one that would take the open sources past 4 GiB in total; the read itself stops one byte past 1 GiB, so a file that grows after the check is refused too, and a non-regular file (folder, device, pipe) is refused from the open handle's metadata. Each refusal ends "Nothing was opened."; nothing is partly loaded and no tab or password prompt is created. Why these numbers: real scanned and engineering PDFs run to hundreds of MB, so 1 GiB leaves headroom; the worker keeps each source twice (`EditSession.source` plus PDFium's copy, about 2 GiB at the cap), so the 4 GiB total (about 8 GiB resident) and the 32-tab count stop a `reopen_document` loop from exhausting memory. The three recovery re-reads that compare the disk file with the open source are bounded by the source's own length. Recent-files reopen now shows these refusals without the "moved or been deleted" wording. Derived copies (redacted, OCR, filled, combined) still open without the cap check; each is bounded by its own 256 MiB output limit and counts toward the cap for later opens.

- Dropped the unused npm `@tauri-apps/plugin-updater` (SM0 review P3). The frontend has called the app's own updater commands since the updater lockdown, and `scripts/capabilities.test.ts` already fails on any `@tauri-apps` import other than `api`. Removed with `npm uninstall`, which changed only `package.json` and that package's `package-lock.json` entry; the Rust `tauri-plugin-updater` crate stays. The notices inventory and `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.txt` were regenerated (366 packages, 0 review items; the npm record and its notice texts are gone, input hashes updated) and `node scripts/dependency-notices.mjs --check` passes. The regeneration also records the new `src-tauri/Cargo.toml` hash from the native-confirmation commit below, whose inventory was stale on its own. `docs/license-inventory.md` now lists seven frontend production packages. Evidence: `dependency-notices`, `capabilities`, `Updates` and `updateCommands` Vitest from pwsh; `npm run build`

- Updater release is tied to one offer (SM0 review P3 on `bd24501..7bdce27`; source build). `release_update` used to clear whatever update the backend held, so a check still running from a closed Software updates dialog could release the offer of a reopened one. Each check now gets a backend number, carried in the offer and in the key the webview sends back (`version`, `signature`, `check`; the number is required and unknown fields are still rejected). `release_update` takes that key and clears only a matching offer, and a check that finishes after a newer check started can no longer store its result over the newer one. Matching on version and signature alone would not have been enough: both dialogs check the same release. Evidence: 2 new Rust unit tests (release ignores a non-matching key; a stale check cannot replace or release the newer check's offer) plus the existing ones updated, and Vitest for the keyed release. Not exercised in a running app

- Native confirmation before installing an update (Joshua's ruling 2026-10-08, SM7's recommendation; source build). After the signed update has downloaded, `install_update` now shows a Windows Yes/No message box owned by the main window: "PDF Workstation <version> is ready to install", that the app will close and reopen, that unsaved changes will be lost, and "Install version <version> now?". The version shown is the one the backend itself checked and downloaded, never a webview value, and the box is not shown unless that downloaded update is held and newer than the installed version. No is the default button. No returns `cancelled`: nothing is installed, the downloaded offer is kept, and Software updates says calmly that the version was not installed (no error). Yes takes the update single use and installs it as before. The box runs on a blocking worker thread, like the Open dialog. It calls `MessageBoxW` directly (`windows` feature `Win32_UI_WindowsAndMessaging`) because `rfd` 0.15.4's Windows message box cannot make No the default; `rfd` is used only for the non-Windows fallback. "Check for updates" asks nothing. The in-app "Install update and restart" button stays as the action that starts the download; there is no second in-app confirmation, so the user is asked once. Evidence: 5 new Rust unit tests with the question answered by a function (No → nothing installed and the offer kept; Yes → installed once; no question for an unheld, forged or not-newer update; an offer released while the question is open is not installed; wording and `cancelled`/`started` serialization) and Vitest for the cancelled path. The native box itself was not seen: no running or installed app was exercised. `verify-in-app-update.ps1` is unaffected while its baseline is published 0.2.0, which has no box; once the baseline is a build with this change, the verifier must answer the box (UIA: find the top-level `#32770` window titled "Install update?" owned by the app process and invoke Yes) after clicking Install

- `yoke-derive` 0.8.3 → 0.8.4 (SM7 cargo-audit triage, lockfile only). 0.8.3 is yanked; `cargo update -p yoke-derive` changed only that package. It is a build-time proc-macro (inventory scope `build-or-auxiliary`), so no shipped runtime crate changes. The notices inventory and `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.txt` were regenerated (version line and `Cargo.lock` input hash) and `node scripts/dependency-notices.mjs --check` passes (367 packages, 0 review items); `dependency-notices` Vitest passes. `cargo test --bins` on the bumped lock: 250 passed, 19 ignored, 1 failed (`ocr_process::tests::abrupt_host_exit_kills_the_job_child`, untouched code, during a 12-minute run while other builds shared the machine), and that test passed when rerun alone. No installer was built.

- Minimal core permissions (SM7 I-4, source build). `capabilities/default.json` no longer grants `core:default` (menu and tray creation, event emit, image, path resolution and more). It now lists only what the frontend calls: `core:app:allow-version` (installed version in Software updates), `core:event:allow-listen` and `core:event:allow-unlisten` (window close request and drag and drop listeners), and `core:window:allow-destroy` (closing the window after the close checks). App commands and download progress channels are not governed by these permissions. A new `scripts/capabilities.test.ts` pins that exact list and fails if the frontend imports another `@tauri-apps` module or calls a `plugin:` command directly. Verified by `cargo build` (Tauri validates the capability file), that test, the `Updates`, `updateCommands`, `App.close`, `App.drop` and `App.window-discard` Vitest files and `npm run build`. The Vitest files mock the Tauri API, so they do not exercise the ACL; nothing here was exercised in a running or installed app.

- Updater lockdown (SM7 I-1, source build). The webview can no longer call the updater plugin: `updater:default` is removed from `capabilities/default.json`, so every `plugin:updater|*` command is refused by the Tauri ACL. Four app-owned commands in `src-tauri/src/update_commands.rs` replace it. `check_for_update` takes no arguments; it uses only the endpoint and public key in `tauri.conf.json`, a fixed 20-second timeout, no webview proxy, headers or target, and offers only a strictly newer version. `download_update` and `install_update` take only `{ version, signature }` (unknown fields such as `proxy`, `headers`, `target` or `allowDowngrades` are rejected) and act only on the update the backend itself fetched in the latest check; a mismatched version or signature is refused, install needs a finished download of that same update, consumes it (single use) and re-checks that it is newer than the installed version; download uses a fixed 120-second timeout; `release_update` drops it. The Software updates dialog keeps its flow and wording (open the dialog to check, see the result, click "Install update and restart"); `src/updateCommands.ts` wraps the commands and `Updates.tsx` no longer imports `@tauri-apps/plugin-updater` (the npm package stays in `package.json` for now). The backend still cannot prove a user gesture: a compromised webview can still start a check against the fixed endpoint and install a signed, newer update. Verified by 8 Rust unit tests (unknown-field rejection, no install without a check and download, mismatch, single use, retry after a failed download, release during download, newer-only versions), `Updates` and `updateCommands` Vitest files (18 tests) and `npm run build`; the dependency inventory was regenerated (input hashes only; `semver` is now a direct dependency, already in the lock at 1.0.28). The real update endpoint was not contacted, and no installed-app or real update was exercised.

- Open-path gate: network-share (UNC) paths by provenance (source build, Joshua's ruling 2026-10-08). The backend, not the webview, decides where an open came from (`OpenOrigin` in `open_path.rs`). A plain `\\server\share\...` or `\\?\UNC\server\share\...` file path is accepted only when it comes straight from the native Open dialog (`open_document`), or when the Rust `DragDrop` window event itself recorded that exact path as dropped (an in-memory list, at most 8 entries, each used by one open and expiring after 30 seconds). `reopen_document` consumes such a grant; any other UNC string it receives (recent files, anything the webview sends) is refused before the filesystem is touched, with a message saying to use the Open dialog or drop the file. Recent files are webview-held strings, so a share file opened earlier cannot be reopened from the list and must be picked or dropped again. Devices, pipes, `\\?\GLOBALROOT`, other `\\?\` forms, relative paths, reserved names, alternate data streams, `.`/`..` and wildcard characters stay refused for every origin, and a canonical form that is not a plain share or local path is refused. `restore_recovery` now passes its dialog-chosen path through the same gate with dialog provenance. Verified by Rust unit tests that use a mocked filesystem (dialog-origin UNC accepted, webview-origin UNC rejected with zero filesystem calls, devices refused with dialog origin, grant exact-match, single use, expiry and cap) plus the existing gate tests; `cargo test --bins` 243 passed, `recentFiles` Vitest and `npm run build` pass. Not exercised on a real network share, with a real drag-and-drop, or in an installed build.

- Open-path gate review fixes (source build). `open_path.rs` no longer slices strings by byte index: the `UNC\` prefix check, the `\\?\UNC\` check on the canonical path and the drive-letter tail now use `str::get`, so a multi-byte character at a prefix boundary (`C:\Ünterlagen\a.pdf`, `\\?\abc€`) cannot panic. Rust tests cover a real file under a non-ASCII top-level folder (accepted) and short or odd non-ASCII strings around each prefix length (rejected without panic). The password dialog now shows the backend's "Passwords cannot be longer than 1 KiB." message (other unlock errors stay generic so they cannot echo a secret) and limits the input to 1024 UTF-8 bytes. The reopen banner shows a gate refusal on its own and keeps "It may have moved or been deleted" for other failures. UNC policy is unchanged and `restore_recovery` is not gated; both await Joshua's ruling. Verified by `cargo test` (`open_path`, unlock, open), `PasswordDialog` and `recentFiles` Vitest files and `npm run build`; not exercised in an installed build.

- `unlock_document` password cap (SM7 I-5, source build). `PdfService::unlock` now rejects a password longer than 1 KiB (1024 bytes of UTF-8) with "Passwords cannot be longer than 1 KiB." before the request reaches the worker, so the PDF is not re-read and PDFium is not called. The existing null-character check is unchanged. A Rust unit test covers 1025 ASCII bytes, a multi-byte password over the byte limit, and a 1024-byte password reaching the worker; not exercised in an installed build.

- Open-path gate (SM7 F2 / I-2, source build). `PdfService::begin_open` and `PdfService::open` now pass every path through a new `src-tauri/src/open_path.rs` gate before the worker is contacted, which covers `reopen_document` (recent files, drag and drop), the Open dialog and every other caller. Accepted: an absolute `X:\...` path or verbatim `\\?\X:\...` path that canonicalises to an existing regular file, including mapped network drive letters (a drive letter whose canonical form is UNC is allowed because the user mapped it; a path written as UNC is not). Rejected with a user-facing message that the existing error banner shows: UNC (`\\server\share`, `//server/share`, `\\?\UNC\`), device and namespace forms (`\\.\`, pipes, other `\\?\`), relative paths, reserved device names (`CON`, `NUL`, `COM1`...) in any component, alternate data streams, null characters, missing files, folders and other non-regular files. A symlink under a local drive letter that points at a UNC target is not told apart from a mapped drive. Verified by Rust unit tests on the path classifier, on the filesystem gate (temp files and folders) and on `begin_open`/`open` against a real worker; not exercised in an installed build. There is no command-line or file-association open in `main.rs` today, so no such entry exists to gate; the size and count caps stay with SM5.

- Source-only: the `edit_pages` (rotate, delete, move, undo, redo) and `save_copy` (save, extract) IPC commands now take a `revision` and reject a stale one with "Document changed. Reload the page list and try again." before any work, as `crop_page` already does for crops. The check runs on the service thread when the request is dequeued, so it is atomic with the edit or save; `DurableEditAt` and `SaveAt` carry the revision and feed the existing `Edit` and `Save` handlers unchanged. The frontend passes the revision of the document it rendered (`editPages(id, revision, edit, page)`, `saveCopy(id, revision, pages)`) and shows the rejection as the existing error message. Rust test `page_edit_and_save_reject_stale_revisions_and_accept_the_current_one` (rotate, delete, move, undo, whole-document save and extract against `resources/welcome.pdf`) and a React test in `App.navigation.test.tsx` pass in a source build; behaviour in an installed build is unverified.

- `scripts/native-recovery-gates.test.ts`: the zero-test-receipt test, which spawns `run-native-recovery-gates.ps1` under PowerShell 7 with a fake `cargo.cmd`, now has an explicit 60 s test timeout (it had the 5 s default while its own spawn allowed 10 s), a 50 s spawn timeout, an assertion that the spawn did not time out, and removes its `target/native-recovery-gate-<uuid>` folder afterwards. In one of three full local suite runs with the CI flags (PowerShell 7.6.6, while a native build and another stream's native test binary were running) it failed at the 5 s default; the other two runs passed 565/565. The file passes 2/2 after the change. Source run only.

- `scripts/default-recovery-workflow.test.ts`: the dry run that stops at input validation now also runs with `HTTPS_PROXY`, `HTTP_PROXY` and `ALL_PROXY` set to the refused loopback proxy `http://127.0.0.1:9` (as the second dry run already did) and asserts the proxy address never appears in its output. If input validation ever broke, that run would fail at the proxy instead of sending the placeholder token to api.github.com. The file's 11 tests pass locally (PowerShell 7.6.6).

- `checkpoint_recovery` no longer rewrites the recovery journal when nothing changed since the last checkpoint. Before, every call wrote a new record with the next generation. Now, when the journal already holds this tab's active record at the same generation, revision and current page, and the record rebuilt from the current session matches it exactly, the call returns success without writing. The source PDF is still re-read and compared first, so a source changed on disk still fails with the same error. A new current page, a new revision or a missing or different record writes as before. A new unit test checkpoints the same state twice (generation stays 1), checks the changed-source refusal on an unchanged checkpoint, then checks that a new current page (generation 2) and a new revision (generation 3) still write. Only the checkpoint path changed; acknowledged-edit journaling, restore, keep, discard and open-original are untouched. Source build only.

- The PDF worker's request channel now admits at most 256 pending non-render requests. Before, only viewer renders were admission-limited (32 keys) and every other command went into an unbounded channel to the single `pdf-worker` thread. A request sent while 256 are already waiting is refused before it is queued, with "The PDF worker is busy with earlier requests. Try again."; it holds no slot and does not split render coalescing. A slot is freed when the worker takes the request off the channel. Cleanup requests (`Close`, `CancelPassword`, `EndPrint`, including the ones sent when an unclaimed reply or print snapshot is dropped) are counted but never refused, so a full queue cannot leak a document, password prompt or print snapshot. Viewer renders keep their own limit and do not count. The number comes from how the UI issues requests: most commands are awaited one at a time behind the busy flag, search awaits `page_text` page by page, annotations and form snapshots share one in-flight request per document and revision, and the largest fan-out is one `page_text_geometry` per page in the visible range plus a handful of per-document metadata reads. Two new unit tests use a standalone sender: one checks refusal at the bound, that renders and cleanup still pass, that every dequeued request frees its slot and that a send on a closed channel returns its slot; the other queues 4 documents' metadata, 64 text-geometry requests and 3 more commands (91) with nothing drained and checks all are admitted with at least half the queue free. Unit tests run the shared worker at 4,096 so parallel tests do not hit the bound. Source build only; not measured in the installed app or under a real rapid-scroll load.

- Source-only **Fill forms** can now clear a supported single-select dropdown or list-box selection. Each choice field has a labelled **Clear selection** button (a native button, disabled while the field is already blank or work is running) that returns focus to the field; the dialog then sends a changed-only `{ kind: 'choice', optionId: null }` patch. The `fill_form_copy` contract changed accordingly: `optionId` is nullable in the request and must still be present. For a cleared choice, native code removes the field's `/V` and `/I` entries and regenerates the normal appearance with the existing blank choice appearance; options, defaults, style, flags and every other object stay unchanged. Radio groups are intentionally not clearable (all supported groups carry NoToggleToOff): the dialog offers no radio clear, and native code rejects a null radio `optionId` with an explicit error, including for a group that began blank. Evidence (source build only): the touched Vitest files passed 13 tests on Vitest 4.1.11, the production frontend build passed, and 32 focused native forms tests passed with one ignored manual probe. A new native unit test checks the cleared dropdown and list write no `/V` or `/I`, keep `/Opt`, `/DV`, `/DA`, `/Ff`, `/Rect`, `/MK` and `/BS`, alter no other object, and carry exactly the regenerated blank appearance; a new service test saves a cleared copy of the ReportLab choice fixture, reads it back as blank, and finds its PDFium render pixel-identical to the source-blank ReportLab choice fixture, and checks a null radio patch writes no file. Installed-app, Windows save-dialog and other-viewer behaviour of a cleared choice remain unverified.

- `scripts/setup-pdfium.ps1` no longer deletes `.pdfium-backup-*` / `.pdfium-staging-*` folders with `Remove-Item -Recurse`. A new `Remove-TreeNoFollow` deletes a junction or symlink under those names (or nested inside one) as the link alone and recurses only into real directories, in the leftover sweep and in the end-of-run cleanup. A new behavioural test plants junctions with that naming (one top-level backup link, one staging link, one link inside a real backup folder), runs setup to a successful swap, and asserts the links are gone and each target's sentinel file survives; it runs under both `pwsh.exe` and Windows PowerShell 5.1 (`powershell.exe`, with the PowerShell 7 `PSModulePath` removed). On this machine the test also passes against the previous script under 5.1, so it guards the behaviour but does not show a failure before the change; the previous script's deletion of a link target was not reproduced. Callers: `npm run installer` runs `build-installer.ps1` under `powershell` (5.1), which invokes this script in-process, so 5.1 is a real caller; the three CI workflows use the runner's default shell. No `#Requires -Version 7` was added. Local source build, from PowerShell 7: `pdfium-provenance` 13/13. Hosted CI and the installer build have not run this change.

- `scripts/setup-pdfium.ps1` follow-ups from the review of the rename swap. When moving the staged folder into place fails on a run that started with no `pdfium/` folder, the error now says no folder existed before the run and none was restored, instead of claiming the previous folder was restored unchanged. A run killed between the two renames can leave a `.pdfium-backup-<guid>` (or `.pdfium-staging-<guid>`) folder behind; the script never restores or reuses such a folder. It downloads and verifies the pinned archive as usual, and only after a verified `pdfium/` is in place (a completed swap, or a `-SkipIfCurrent` skip on a matching marker) does it delete every leftover `.pdfium-backup-*` and `.pdfium-staging-*` folder, warning if one cannot be removed; a failed run leaves them where they are. Two new behavioural tests (both fail against the previous script): a first-run injected move failure with an orphaned backup present reports no restore and keeps the backup; an orphaned backup and staging folder never satisfy `-SkipIfCurrent` (the archive is downloaded) and are removed after the swap, and a later orphan is removed on a skip. Local source build, from PowerShell 7: `pdfium-provenance` plus the three workflow tests that reference the script, 22/22. End to end on a worktree with a planted orphan backup: a full run swapped in the verified folder (DLL SHA-256 `79D4676B…C118`, marker written) and removed the orphan; a second run with `-SkipIfCurrent` skipped the download. Hosted CI and the installer build have not run this change.

- `vite.config.ts` now excludes the repository's build, output and scratch folders from test collection explicitly: `target/`, `dist/`, `artifacts/`, `Smacrobat-private/`, `scripts/%SystemDrive%/`, `src-tauri/target/`, `src-tauri/gen/` and `src-tauri/resources/`. Vitest 4's default exclude list covers only `node_modules` and `.git`, so a stray test file in one of these folders in a local checkout would have been collected. With a probe test file placed in each of those folders, `npx vitest list --filesOnly` listed all seven probes in the folders outside `target/` before the change and none after; with or without probes it lists exactly the 94 tracked test files. Run from PowerShell 7 with the CI flags (`--pool=threads --maxWorkers=1 --no-file-parallelism`), 94 files / 559 tests pass, and `npm run build` passes. Hosted CI has not run this change.

- `default-recovery-verification.yml` now gives its one job, `verify-installed-recovery`, a job-scoped `contents: write` (with `actions: read`); the workflow-level permissions stay `contents: read` and `actions: read`. This is the same shape as the Step 1 draft, default expanded page tools and default comments-and-forms jobs. The job runs `verify-default-draft.ps1`, which reads the unpublished draft release's metadata and asset bytes with the job token, and GitHub lists draft releases only to callers with push access. Code inspection shows both verifier scripts issue only GET requests, and the recovery verifier clears the token before installing or launching the app. `scripts/default-recovery-workflow.test.ts` now pins this exact shape (one `contents: read` at the top, one `contents: write` on the job) instead of forbidding `contents: write`. Not dispatched; whether the recovery workflow can read a draft with this token remains unverified until a hosted run.

- `scripts/default-recovery-workflow.test.ts` now exercises `scripts/verify-default-recovery.ps1` instead of only matching its text. New tests: the script parses and assigns no PowerShell automatic or read-only variable (`$pid`, `$input`, `$args`, `$host` and others); every command the script reaches, followed transitively through its own `Import-RecoveryFunctions` calls and its dot-sourced libraries, resolves to a defined function, cmdlet or program; a dry run with placeholder arguments binds every parameter and stops at "Step 1 run id must be one positive base-10 integer."; a dry run with well-formed placeholders passes input validation and fails at its first GitHub request, which is routed to a refused loopback proxy (`127.0.0.1:9`) so nothing leaves the machine; `Open-RecoveryFixture`, run against stubs carrying the real helper signatures, passes the owned process id and start ticks as typed values to all six process-bound helpers; and `Stop-ExactRecoveryApplication`, run against fake process records, kills only the exact PID, start time and case-exact path, and rejects wrong start ticks, wrong path, path differing only in case, a missing PID and a kill that does not exit. Each of the four defects found in review of `4354ea7` (helpers imported into a function scope, nine transitive helpers never imported, an assignment to `$pid`, and the lost space after `$appPid` at six call sites) was reintroduced locally and made at least one new test fail; none of that breakage is committed. The string checks that only restated the kill logic and the separate parse check were removed; the workflow, source-path, oracle-text and receipt-claim string checks stay because they pin configuration and wording that no local run can reach. PowerShell-spawning tests in the file now carry explicit timeouts. Run from PowerShell 7: the file passes 11/11. The verifier itself is unchanged and has still never run on a hosted runner.

- Upgraded the test runner from Vitest 3.2.4 to 4.1.11 (exact pin; it brings `@vitest/mocker` 4.1.11 and no longer depends on `tinypool`), and applied plain `npm audit fix`, which moved `source-map-js` from 1.2.1 to 1.2.2 in the lockfile only. Before: `npm audit` reported 4 dev-only advisories (2 critical `tinypool` GHSA-5gmw-xhrv-c9v3 and GHSA-85c8-ppgw-ccpr, 1 high `source-map-js` GHSA-68fv-2mgg-jv7q, 1 moderate `@vitest/mocker` GHSA-82fw-gwwq-j7x9). After: 0, with and without `--omit=dev`. Vite stays at 6.4.3, which Vitest 4 accepts, so the shipped frontend build tooling is unchanged. Vitest 4 removed the `--minWorkers` option, so the frontend test step in `windows-validation.yml`, `release.yml` and `ocr-signed-artifact.yml`, and the three workflow tests that pin it, now run `npm test -- --pool=threads --maxWorkers=1 --no-file-parallelism`; with one maximum worker and no file parallelism the run is still single-threaded. Vitest 4 also types an untyped `vi.fn()` as a mock that is not assignable to a plain function type, which failed `tsc -b`, so eight dialog test files type their mock parameters as `Mock<(...args: any[]) => any>` instead of `ReturnType<typeof vi.fn>`. The dependency-notices inventory changed only in its `package.json` and `package-lock.json` input hashes (367 packages, `--check` passes). Local source build, run from PowerShell 7 with the CI flags: 94 files / 549 tests passed twice (3 min 14 s and 3 min 11 s), and `npm run build` passes. Hosted CI has not run this change.

- `scripts/setup-pdfium.ps1` now swaps the staged PDFium folder in by renames instead of deleting the live folder first. It renames the existing `pdfium/` to a sibling `.pdfium-backup-<guid>`, moves the staged folder into place, and only then deletes the backup; if the move fails it renames the backup back and fails with a message saying the previous folder was restored (or, if that restore also fails, names the backup path it left). If the existing folder cannot be renamed (for example a file inside it is held open), the script stops with that folder unchanged instead of deleting it file by file. Leftover staging or backup folders that cannot be removed are reported with `Write-Warning` rather than ignored. A switch named `-TestOnlyFailStagingMove` injects a move failure for tests only; it is off by default. `.gitignore` now covers `src-tauri/resources/.pdfium-staging-*/` and `.pdfium-backup-*/` (confirmed with `git check-ignore`). New tests in `scripts/pdfium-provenance.test.ts`: an injected move failure leaves the old folder's files and contents intact; a file in the old folder held open with `FileShare.None` by a child `pwsh` leaves every old file in place; and a check of the two ignore lines. The PDFium provenance and workflow tests pass locally (4 files, 20 tests, run from PowerShell 7). On a source checkout, one end-to-end run replaced the existing folder through the backup path and a second `-SkipIfCurrent` run skipped the download. Not yet exercised in hosted CI or in an installer build.

- `scripts/setup-pdfium.ps1` no longer extracts over an existing `src-tauri/resources/pdfium/` folder. After the archive's SHA-256 matches the pin, it extracts into a fresh `.pdfium-staging-<guid>` folder beside the destination, requires `bin/pdfium.dll` there, writes a `.verified-archive` marker (the pinned archive SHA-256 and the extracted `pdfium.dll` SHA-256), and only then removes the old `pdfium/` folder and moves the staged one into place; a failed extraction removes the staging folder and leaves any existing folder unchanged. A new `-SkipIfCurrent` switch skips the download only when the marker matches the pin and the current DLL. `scripts/build-installer.ps1` now always calls setup with `-SkipIfCurrent` instead of skipping it whenever a `pdfium.dll` exists, so an older unverified or altered DLL is re-extracted from the verified archive before a local installer build. The pinned hash and URL are unchanged. Two behavioural tests in `scripts/pdfium-provenance.test.ts` serve synthetic archives (with the pin substituted in a temporary copy of the script) from a local server: a stale unmarked folder is replaced and a changed DLL is re-extracted; a truncated archive and an archive without `bin/pdfium.dll` leave no partial folder and leave an existing folder untouched. Both fail against the previous script. One end-to-end run on a source checkout replaced an unmarked Chromium 7881 folder with the same DLL hash and a marker, and a second run skipped the download. Not yet exercised in hosted CI or in an installer build.

- Gave every PowerShell-spawning test in `scripts/installed-print-dialog-workflow.test.ts` (21 tests) and `scripts/installed-app-launch-workflow.test.ts` (20 tests) that still ran on Vitest's 5-second default an explicit 15-second per-test timeout, the value the other script tests in these files already use. Under machine load these tests had failed on the default timeout and passed when re-run alone. The global Vitest configuration is unchanged. Run from PowerShell, both files pass 51/51 in one single-file-at-a-time run (64 s). Run from Git Bash, two launch tests fail fast (not on a timeout) because the verifier rejects Git Bash's inherited `PATH` as its minimal child environment; that is a shell-environment effect, not a test change. Whether this removes the false reds under load was not measured.

- Added `scripts/workflow-action-pins.test.ts`, one test per file in `.github/workflows/` that fails if any `uses:` reference is not a full 40-character commit SHA (a local `./` action is allowed; a trailing `#` comment is ignored) or if a `uses:` appears anywhere other than its own key line. It replaces the narrower tag-and-branch-name check in `scripts/signed-ocr-artifact-workflow.test.ts`, which covered one workflow and four ref names. The new file passes 15/15 on the current 14 workflows; temporarily changing one line to `@v4`, to a branch-style `@release/v4`, or to a quoted short SHA made it fail each time, and the change was reverted. Source checks only.

- Unified the `dtolnay/rust-toolchain` pin. `release.yml` and `windows-validation.yml` move from `6bed0761…`, a commit no longer on any upstream branch, to `89b12181…`, the head of the upstream `stable` branch re-checked on 2026-10-07 with `gh api repos/dtolnay/rust-toolchain/branches/stable` and already used by `ocr-signed-artifact.yml`; all three lines carry a `# stable branch head` comment. The release and Windows validation workflow tests expect the new SHA, and the release test's `uses:` parser now accepts a trailing comment. Source checks only: the five workflow test files that read these workflows pass 18/18. No workflow was dispatched, so hosted behavior with this pin is unverified.

- Added `/target/` to `.gitignore`. Script tests write scratch folders such as `target/print-dialog-test-<uuid>` and `target/ocr-export-*` under the repository root `target/`, which was untracked but not ignored, so a broad `git add` could sweep them in. The pattern is anchored to the repository root; `src-tauri/target/` stays ignored by its existing line, and no tracked file lives under either folder. Checked with `git status` and `git check-ignore`; the tests' cleanup behavior is unchanged. Also tightened the spacing of the newest Recent changes entries.

- Two small follow-ups from the code review of the PDFium SHA-256 pin. `scripts/setup-pdfium.ps1` now compares the archive hash with case-insensitive `-ne` instead of `-cne`, so a hash differing only in hex letter case is not treated as a mismatch; the pin itself is unchanged. The mismatch test in `scripts/pdfium-provenance.test.ts` now closes open connections and awaits its local HTTP server's `close()` before deleting its temporary folder. Unchanged by design: setup still extracts over an existing `pdfium/` folder and does not remove an older `pdfium.dll` there (`build-installer.ps1` likewise skips setup when the DLL exists); that local-only behavior is left for a separate decision. The focused test file passes 4/4.

- Documented the WebView2 install requirement. The installer uses Tauri's WebView2 download bootstrapper (kept by project decision), so it carries no WebView2 Runtime and installing on a machine without WebView2 needs network access once. The Install section above and [Releases and signing](releases.md) now say so. Documentation only; installing on a machine without WebView2, offline install behavior, and the bootstrapper's version and terms remain unverified.

- Pinned the last 16 tag- or branch-referenced GitHub Actions steps to full commit SHAs, with the resolved tag kept as a trailing comment, in `ocr-signed-artifact.yml`, `in-app-update-verification.yml`, `persistence-verification.yml`, `print-dialog-verification.yml` and `reading-tools-verification.yml`. Each SHA was resolved on 2026-10-06 with `gh api repos/<owner>/<repo>/git/ref/tags/v4` (all five were lightweight tags pointing directly at commits): `actions/checkout` v4.4.0 `11d5960a…`, `actions/setup-node` v4.4.0 `49933ea5…`, `actions/setup-dotnet` v4.3.1 `67a3573c…`, `actions/upload-artifact` v4.6.2 `ea165f8d…`, `actions/download-artifact` v4.3.0 `d3f86a10…`, the same commits the other workflows already pin. `dtolnay/rust-toolchain@stable` is a branch, so it is pinned to that branch's head on 2026-10-06, `89b12181…`; this differs from the `6bed0761…` pin in `release.yml` and `windows-validation.yml` because the upstream branch has since moved. No `uses:` line in `.github/workflows/` now references a tag or branch. The signed OCR artifact workflow test now rejects mutable action references and matches the pinned upload step. Source checks only: all workflow files parse as YAML and the eight affected workflow test files pass 53/53. No workflow was dispatched, so hosted behavior with these pins is unverified.

- Removed the development server origins `http://localhost:1420` and `ws://localhost:1420` from the production Content Security Policy in `src-tauri/tauri.conf.json`; production `connect-src` is now `ipc: http://ipc.localhost`. The previous policy, including those origins, moves to `devCsp`, which Tauri 2's code generator (`tauri-codegen` 2.6.3, the locked version) uses only for development builds and otherwise falls back to `csp`. Source checks only: `npm run build` passes, the four configuration-reading test files pass 16/16, and a source-only `cargo build --features tauri/custom-protocol` compiles with the new configuration. `tauri dev`, an installer built from this source, and installed-app network behavior were not exercised.

- `scripts/setup-pdfium.ps1` now fails closed on the PDFium archive's SHA-256. It pins `73CC0DE638AC2095E7445BF56A38200A5B7C7CA0E9F4BA144598F2457377AC08` for the `chromium/7881` `pdfium-win-x64.tgz` asset, the same value `scripts/prepare-default-draft-pdfium.ps1` already pinned. On 2026-10-06 the GitHub release-asset digest (asset 441936973, 3,733,154 bytes) and a fresh local download's `Get-FileHash` both gave that value. After download and before extraction the script hashes the archive; on a mismatch or a failed download it deletes the file and throws without extracting. An optional `-ArchiveUri` parameter (default: the pinned upstream URL) exists for the test; the hash is not overridable. A new behavioural test serves a substitute tarball from a local HTTP server and checks the script exits non-zero, names both hashes, deletes the download and extracts nothing; the focused gate passes 15 tests across 4 files, and one local end-to-end run downloaded, verified and extracted PDFium 7881. `docs/license-inventory.md` records the pinned asset and hash. This is a source change only: no installer was rebuilt and no CI run has exercised it yet.

- Declared the project license as MIT in `package.json` (`"license": "MIT"`, mirrored into the root entry of `package-lock.json` by `npm install --package-lock-only`) and in `src-tauri/Cargo.toml` (`license = "MIT"`; `Cargo.lock` records no license and is unchanged). Both manifests are hashed inputs, so the generated dependency inventory was regenerated in the same change: only its three `inputHashes` entries moved, and the package set remains 367 records with zero review items. The regenerated 202,858-byte inventory has SHA-256 `9916BFC0E818BE3B17813E53EF7635132CCCA5F0741F9CB25709B45AD5CA0AEF`; `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.txt`, package identities, license evidence, and source archives are unchanged. The notice freshness check passes locally. Stale "project license undecided" wording in this README, `docs/gaps.md`, and `docs/license-inventory.md` now points to the MIT `LICENSE`. No installer was rebuilt, so no installed or released artifact carries these fields yet.

- Added the MIT `LICENSE` (copyright 2026 Joshua Beel). Until now the public repository carried no license file, which left it all-rights-reserved by default despite being intended as free open-source software. The `license` fields in `package.json` and `src-tauri/Cargo.toml` are deliberately not set yet: both files are hashed inputs of the third-party notice inventory, so that change must regenerate the inventory in the same commit and will land separately. The bundled third-party notices and MPL source archives are unchanged.

- Committed the unfinished work left when development moved from Codex to Claude. Unsigned local installer proofs now require and record the exact clean 40-character git HEAD (`Assert-InstallerSourceRevision`), as the artifact-only Azure OCR mode already did. Workflow tests now normalize CRLF before matching. A new default-recovery verification workflow, its PowerShell verifier and two contract test files were added. The native-evidence fixture test moves its scratch root from the OS temp directory to `target/` (the runner's 8.3 temp path failed the verifier's exact root comparison) and keeps its `finally` cleanup. With PowerShell 7.6.6 installed locally, the 8 affected test files pass 61 of 61 tests. Nothing here was run on hosted CI; this is local source evidence only.

- Fixed three defects in the new `verify-default-recovery.ps1` found by the independent code review and the SM7 security review of `100e0af..4354ea7`: the helper import used `Invoke-Expression` inside a function, so every imported helper vanished when the importer returned; nine transitive helpers (`Assert-ExactProperties`, `Assert-ReceiptValue`, `Assert-ReceiptFileObject`, `Get-UniqueResource`, `Convert-RegistryFilePath`, `New-GitHubGetRequest`, `Assert-GitHubResponseSuccess`, `Invoke-GitHubJson`, `ConvertTo-ExactUInt64`) were never imported; and `Open-RecoveryFixture` assigned the read-only automatic `$pid`. The importer now uses `Set-Item Function:script:` like the sibling verifiers, the import closure is complete, and the variable is `$appPid`. The contract test now removes its `target/` scratch directories, and the OCR upgrade runner-state test carries an explicit 30 s timeout instead of relying on the 5 s default that timed out on hosted CI. Both reviews also confirmed the `build-installer.ps1` guard is tighter, not looser: UnsignedLocal now requires the exact clean HEAD. The workflow has still not run on hosted CI.

- Extended the shared serialized native CI helper so aggregate recovery storage cannot rely only on the broad suite. After that suite passes, the helper now runs the exact ignored cross-process lock, cap, and generation proof through the same 64 KiB receipt bound and exact one-test footer check as the existing recovery probes, then runs an exact `recovery_store` integration boundary test without incorrectly marking it ignored. The helper contract gate passes 2 tests, including a fake-Cargo zero-test success that must fail closed, and independent review found no P1/P2. The underlying native gate separately passed the cross-process proof and the complete single-thread binary suite; no GitHub workflow was dispatched, so this is source-policy evidence rather than a hosted-run result.

- Added the exact PDFium `VERSION` and `args.gn` files to the source bundle beside the DLL and notices, and updated the pinned draft-resource reconstruction to extract, validate, and copy those already inventoried files. The 37-byte version file identifies Chromium **151.0.7881.0** and has SHA-256 `9EA21B933A52E57CD585F5A543C16155513F8195A158B0526F3A3C50FC3431CD`; the 216-byte configuration identifies the Windows x64 standalone noncomponent release build with V8, XFA, and partition allocation disabled and has SHA-256 `161BF08999E403B7DA9D924C45B7EE7BC8BEB3038B08F9E174BA6761DD06A976`. The focused provenance and affected packaging/configuration gate passes 30 tests across 7 files, and independent review found no P1/P2. A fresh source-only no-bundle debug build transformed 1,953 frontend modules in 2.87 seconds, completed the native build in 17.02 seconds, and produced a 34,564,096-byte executable with SHA-256 `0675E8D36CD5EB128EB3A6A8E911CC099DF6B55A3F08339EC781EF2E8C73E9B9`; both copied metadata files match their source hashes. Independent artifact readback found the exact current JavaScript and CSS Brotli payloads once each in that executable and decompressed them byte-for-byte to `index-BUNBSHHU.js` (`70112AB44278720BD2A56775CDED89B6765BFAFB90759253FF6FB6022E6999C6`) and `index-DLhpjJgN.css` (`73EB7C4F75E326A3834B81336DF63D16CA27955946A70BE437D835092C20147C`); embedded normalized HTML references both names. All 28 copied resource files match their sources, including the 367-record, zero-review dependency inventory. This does not change or retroactively describe an older installer. Final installer extraction, signing, installation, and WebView2 bootstrapper version/terms remain unverified.

- Bounded aggregate recovery storage without deleting or evicting records. A retained app-owned lock leaf uses Windows exclusive sharing to serialize writers across processes; while holding it, admission scans at most 4,096 directory entries, accepts at most 512 exact keyed `.smacrec` files totaling 128 MiB, opens every counted leaf with reparse-point checks, and subtracts only the exact destination when projecting a replacement. Active records and tombstones share the same budget; an over-budget store, concurrent writer, or new source at the boundary fails before a temporary file or publication can change the old record, while a shrinking exact replacement remains possible. The existing four-temp cap and 32 MiB record cap bound the worst app-owned write-time peak to 256 MiB: 128 MiB persistent plus at most four retained or candidate temporary records. Unrelated files are neither counted as keyed recovery nor modified, though more than 4,096 total directory entries fail closed. The storage gate passes 10 tests with 2 isolated process probes; exact checkpoint, fatal-lock/discard, fresh-process restart, and cross-process storage probes each pass separately, and the complete single-thread native binary gate passes 229 tests with 19 ignored resource, process, exporter, or manual probes in 511.76 seconds. Together these checks cover record-count and byte boundaries, exact replacement accounting, deterministic busy and retry behavior, same-key generation ordering, rejection without publication, and unrelated-file preservation. Independent review found no P1/P2.

- Fixed the recovery retry path after an Open-original acknowledgement is published but its required native session close initially fails. The retry-only dialog may now close only its own locked document through the matching recovery offer; unrelated offers, busy work, modal tools, and concurrent closes remain blocked. A successful retry removes the locked tab and stale offer while retaining the instruction to reopen the PDF and choose between recovered edits and the original. The focused recovery, tab-close, and window-discard gate passes 27 tests across 3 files, the production frontend build transforms 1,953 modules, and independent review found no remaining P1/P2 in this path. This is source integration evidence; installed-app interaction and crash or power-loss recovery remain unverified.

- Serialized the frontend and native test gates in all three Windows CI workflows to match the proven local execution policy without changing test deadlines or disabling coverage. Vitest now uses one thread, one worker, and no file parallelism so PowerShell-heavy workflow files cannot contend with each other.
