The locked native gate uses one libtest thread, then a shared fail-closed helper runs the exact ignored manual-checkpoint, post-publication lock/discard, and fresh-process recovery tests by full module name; it requires one bounded `1 passed; 0 failed` receipt for each, so a stale filter that selects zero tests cannot pass silently. The focused workflow/helper contract gate passes 11 tests across 4 files, including a fake-Cargo zero-test negative control, and independent review found no P1/P2. No GitHub workflow was dispatched; this is source-policy evidence rather than hosted-run evidence.

- Verified a current source-only Windows debug build after the recovery and dependency-notice changes. The production frontend transformed 1,953 modules in 2.99 seconds, the no-bundle native build finished in 41.57 seconds, and the resulting 34,553,344-byte executable has SHA-256 `64FE61D9BC12B8EC637704D3491D8AB91783FA3048C7BB0B4B943CE9CB904C53`. The build dependency receipt binds the exact generated HTML, JavaScript, and CSS; the cached Brotli JavaScript and CSS decompress byte-for-byte to the current dist assets, their compressed payloads occur exactly in the executable, and the embedded HTML references both exact hashed asset names. Copied PDFium, welcome PDF, dependency inventory, and notice resources match their source hashes; the inventory remains 367 records with zero marked for review. This is debug source-artifact evidence, not installation, signing, upgrade, installer, or release proof.

- Refreshed the generated dependency inventory after `sha2` moved from development-only use into the production recovery implementation. The package set remains 367 records with zero review items; `sha2` and its five existing transitive records now carry conservative runtime-candidate scope, while package identities, license evidence, source archives, and notice text remain unchanged. The current 202,858-byte inventory has SHA-256 `A4EF40C50F66EF1B1B384841F59187830FF27C0253451C2F8A4BE0423B5C0532`; the notice check and all five exact MPL source-archive checks pass. This inventory does not choose the project's license or complete release licensing review. The root project license has since been set to MIT (see `LICENSE`); final WebView2 bootstrapper terms/version plus bundled PDFium version/configuration provenance remain pending installer work.

- Completed the source frontend boundary for acknowledged recovery discard and post-publication verification failures. Explicit tab or window discard now requires an exact document/revision tombstone receipt before close or window destruction; partial multi-tab failures disclose the acknowledged count and remain retryable. Native fatal results use one strictly validated structured document/revision shape rather than error text. Page, crop, annotation, manual-checkpoint, and Open-original failures that may already have published recovery close the locked native session without discarding its journal and direct the user to reopen the PDF for the explicit Keep/Open-original choice; a failed cleanup close retains the tab and offer behind a retry-close-only dialog. The focused frontend gate passes 54 tests across 6 files, the production build transforms 1,953 modules, and independent review found no remaining P1/P2. This is source integration evidence; actual process restart, installed-app recovery, parent-directory synchronization, and power-loss durability remain unverified.

- Closed the acknowledged-recovery discard and post-publication failure gaps in the source build. Explicit discard now verifies the exact dirty document, revision, source identity, generation, and owned journal head before publishing a higher-generation inactive tombstone; retries are idempotent, stale or competing sessions remain rejected, and generic cleanup Close continues to retain active recovery. A write that publishes but cannot complete required published-path verification no longer rolls live state back behind the durable record: native locks that session, returns a strictly typed document/revision error, permits only the captured pre-revision to tombstone the exact committed state, and directs the interface to close and reopen for an explicit Keep/Open-original choice. Pre-publication failures still restore the exact session. Focused gates pass 5 command tests, 8 storage tests, the isolated checkpoint/restore regression, and the expanded discard/fatal-lock regression; the complete single-thread native binary gate passes 229 tests with 13 ignored retained-resource, isolated-process, exporter, or manual probes in 777.62 seconds. A separate controlled three-process proof passes: process A acknowledges an edit and exits without Close, process B reopens the source, keeps the exact recovered revision/page/render state and durably undoes it, and process C observes the higher-generation inactive tombstone without offering stale edits. Its harness uses a system-random create-new owned root, rejects reparse or escaped fixture paths, and clears the child environment except for the owned fixture identity and required Windows/temp variables. Independent review found no remaining P1/P2 in the lock/discard paths. Source bytes remain unchanged. This proves fresh source-process reconstruction, not an installed-app crash, parent-directory synchronization, or power-loss recovery.

- Stabilized two synthetic installed-print deadline probes without changing product, child-process, or Vitest deadlines. Each probe now has 250 milliseconds to reach its injected provider, whose 350-millisecond stall still crosses the deadline and must fail closed at the exact rooted-UIA or native-enumeration stage. In an exclusive single-worker lane, the four other implicated workflow files passed 49 tests with their original limits, the complete print workflow passed 26 tests in 62.17 seconds, and an independent review found no weakening of the overrun or cleanup checks. A subsequent frontend snapshot passed 519 tests across 89 files in 350.90 seconds; the final recovery-consumer edits landed during that run and are covered separately by their focused 54-test pass and production build, so the snapshot is not claimed as a final-HEAD gate. These are controlled workflow-test results, not installed-app or printer compatibility evidence.

- Updated three workflow regressions without changing any signed-artifact pins or security checks. The installed reading-tools test now parses the production script and compiles its native picker API in one bounded PowerShell child, and its exact Viewer marker includes the current redaction guard. The expanded page-tools test derives the first changed pinned feature path in verifier order and requires that exact fail-closed diagnostic. The signed reading-tools test still binds every expected blob to the inspected signed commit while positively detecting current feature and application-shell drift instead of incorrectly requiring current HEAD to equal the old signed feature blobs. The focused single-worker proof passed all four corrected assertions across three files in 9.96 seconds; independent review found no weakening. The broader frontend run has not been repeated, and its remaining PowerShell timing or diagnostic failures remain unresolved.

- Reconciled the gaps and development-roadmap documents with the current bounded source routes for ordered multi-image creation, strict full-page image replacement, strict one-run text replacement, opt-in searchable OCR, and destructive raster redaction. The documents continue to classify arbitrary editing, general sanitization and content-preserving or multi-page redaction, default/published searchable OCR, native installed interaction, and release behavior as unavailable or unverified. Recovery documentation now distinguishes the source journal/offer integration from unattended autosave and records that explicit-discard and post-publication error alignment are still under repair; actual restart, installed-app, and power-loss recovery remain unverified. A bounded single-worker full frontend run passed 495 of 507 tests across 80 of 88 files in 544.31 seconds, including the three searchable-fixture tests in 535 milliseconds, but failed 12 tests: nine PowerShell timing or diagnostic cases and three stale source/artifact assertions. This is not a full-gate pass.

- Replaced Vitest's deep structural comparison of the three deterministic searchable-OCR PNG buffers with Node's exact `Buffer.equals` byte comparison while retaining the existing byte-length and SHA-256 assertions. An isolated single-worker run reproduced the first test timing out at 6.702 seconds even though direct fixture generation took 64–74 milliseconds and a direct build, CRC, inflate, and file-read control took 136.7 milliseconds. With the unchanged five-second test budget, the same bounded command passed all three tests; the first test took 144 milliseconds, all test bodies took 492 milliseconds, and Vitest completed in 1.35 seconds. A separate owned hanging-child control confirmed termination left no live child. This corrects test-comparator overhead only; it does not establish OCR runtime, recognition accuracy, or installed-app behavior.

- Activated acknowledged edit recovery in the source frontend. Ordinary open now keeps the native session clean while it explicitly offers **Keep recovered edits** or **Open original**; Escape cannot choose silently, failed choices retain the prompt and clean session for retry, and malformed offers close the native session. Recovery paths, hashes, generations, and source bytes do not cross IPC. Keep opens the exact offered dirty revision at its recorded physical page without changing the source PDF; Open original acknowledges the native tombstone before dismissing the offer. The prompt blocks tab, open, close, window-close, drop, shortcut, and manual-recovery conflicts. Every supported page, crop, note, and highlight mutation now sends the exact pre-edit physical current page in the same acknowledged native request, rather than a later checkpoint. The affected frontend gate passes 56 tests across 7 files, the final recovery/focus rerun passes 15 tests, and the production build transforms 1,952 modules. This is source integration evidence: installed startup/crash interaction, parent-directory synchronization, and power-loss durability remain unproven, and manual recovery remains available.

- Added acknowledged edit recovery to the source build. Every supported unencrypted page, crop, note, and highlight mutation now carries the pre-edit physical current page, validates it against the current session, clamps it after page-count changes, and publishes a bounded journal record with decoded read-back before returning success; journal or source-file failure restores the exact plan, undo/redo stacks, saved baseline, revision, and note-ID state. Version 2 records add higher-generation inactive tombstones for undo-to-clean and explicit **Open original**, while version 1 active snapshots remain readable. Ordinary open can offer an exact matching active record without exposing a source path, journal path, hash, or generation to the frontend; **Keep recovered edits**, **Open original**, and manual restore all reject stale choices and competing same-source owners. Save a Copy preserves the original dirty session and its recovery record. A process-local accepted-record floor rejects older or different same-generation state during that run, but restart has no separate trusted floor and therefore does not claim cryptographic rollback protection against an attacker who can replace all local state. Parent-directory synchronization, installed crash interaction, and absolute power-loss durability remain unproven; records are tombstoned rather than deleted. The recovery codec gate passes 7 tests, storage passes 8, three isolated service recovery regressions and the manual checkpoint/restore regression pass, and independent review found no remaining P1/P2. A broad 239-test native run was stopped after prolonged silence after exposing save-copy expectation and synthetic render-admission test defects; all seven reported failures pass individually after correction, so that interrupted run is not reported as a full-suite pass.

- Tightened source-interface keyboard behavior after a focused modal accessibility audit. Closing any top-level or Organize Pages dialog now returns focus to its available opener, or to the owning Menu/Close tool control when the opener was removed, disabled, hidden, inert, threw during focus, or accepted focus without becoming active; text areas receive the same visible keyboard focus ring as other controls. Shared viewport bounds and scrolling are intended to keep dialog actions reachable at 200% zoom. The audit also confirmed explicit accessible dialog names and Escape handlers, including intentional Escape suppression while an operation owns a dialog. The focused gate passes 20 app, organizer, shortcut, and focus-helper tests, and the current production build transforms 1,952 modules. Independent review found no remaining issue. A real-DOM App/Organizer focus-return harness is checked in, but its attempted headless run produced no DOM receipt; no actual 200% zoom run was performed. This is finite source and unit evidence, not a full screen-reader, zoom-runtime, browser-runtime, or installed-app accessibility claim.

- Activated the explicit manual recovery interface in the source build. **Menu > Create manual recovery point** is available only for a dirty document and submits its exact document ID, revision, and zero-based physical current page; the UI accepts success only when the native receipt matches that captured state. **Restore manual recovery point** sends no path across IPC, opens the native source-PDF picker, treats cancellation as a no-op, and opens only a coherent dirty restored session at its recorded page while leaving the selected source PDF unchanged. The active picker blocks conflicting open, close, save, tab, and modal actions, and malformed or post-unmount returned sessions are closed instead of leaked. The interface states that recovery is manual rather than autosave and that full power-loss durability is unproven. The affected 28-test frontend gate passes across recovery, open, close, shortcut, and drop behavior; the production build transforms 1,950 modules, and an independent review found no DTO or lifecycle mismatch. This does not add autosave, startup restore, journal deletion or tombstones after save/close, installed interaction, or crash/power-loss proof; encrypted or restricted sources remain refused by the native service.

- Added explicit native recovery commands without enabling automatic recovery. `checkpoint_recovery` accepts only a document ID, exact revision, and zero-based physical current page, derives the app-owned recovery directory internally, and returns a typed camel-case receipt only after the service durably accepts the checkpoint. `restore_recovery` takes no frontend path, permits one native picker and restore at a time, treats picker cancellation as no result, and returns only the newly opened dirty document plus its physical current page after the service reopens, hashes, and validates the selected source. Source paths, journal paths, hashes, passwords, and journal identities never cross IPC. The 3 focused command tests and native no-run gate pass, and an independent review found no command or wiring issues. This remains a manual source-build boundary: no autosave schedule, startup prompt, clean-close or save tombstone, automatic record cleanup, installed interaction, or power-loss crash-recovery proof is implemented.

- Added an ignored, environment-gated native exporter for five exact synthetic saved-output cases: one PDF containing a sticky note plus area and selected-text highlights, a supported mixed form fill, a six-page raster-redaction copy, a three-page PDF created from committed synthetic images, and a strict one-run text replacement copy. It accepts only an existing empty non-reparse directory below `target`, writes every source/output and the manifest with create-new semantics, retains source bytes exactly, and checks each output through its existing native semantic and PDFium pixel oracles before writing the manifest last. The final native run passed its one exact test in 32.27 seconds. Independent Poppler 26.07.0 and `pdfinfo` verification then matched every bound source/output hash, selected page, independently rounded 72-DPI size, and feature-region RGB range across all five outputs. The retained 5,094-byte manifest has SHA-256 `B3D49AF18189DE5FDA3A54B5C04B7131792DF3C4DF95D84D6A1EC1C814348258`; its 4,124-byte no-clobber receipt has SHA-256 `43018D8FBCCB4A3BC013867C10A15F438BEA9B4856C8719FBDEDAB73B6FCDE17`. These exact controlled cases provide native-save and independent-renderer evidence only; they do not establish Adobe behavior, arbitrary-PDF support, broad cross-viewer compatibility, installed interaction, or release behavior.

- Connected the recovery codec and storage foundation to an explicit native service boundary without activating automatic product recovery. `checkpoint_recovery` accepts one current unsaved document/revision and physical current page, derives the source identity from the worker-owned original bytes, advances the stored generation, and acknowledges only after the bounded record is synchronously written and decoded from its published path. `restore_recovery` explicitly reopens and hashes a caller-selected source PDF, requires an exact matching unencrypted record, reconstructs the bounded page order, rotations, crops, app-owned annotations, revision, dirty/undo state, and current page, and validates the restored plan through the existing export and PDFium geometry paths before registering a session. The focused end-to-end native regression proves durable read-back and pixel-equivalent restoration across move, rotation, crop, and highlight state while preserving source bytes; the native no-run gate passes. This remains a source-only service API: no frontend or startup call site, autosave schedule, restore prompt, record deletion/tombstone, clean-close policy, or installed crash-recovery behavior is implemented.

- Added a source-only cross-viewer output harness and manifest template for future controlled saved-output acceptance runs. This is harness self-test evidence only: the current four-test gate copies one existing synthetic ReportLab PDF and exercises all five profile labels against that same unchanged file, so it does not verify any feature-generated output, Adobe behavior, arbitrary PDFs, or cross-viewer compatibility. The harness binds exact source and output SHA-256 values, independently preflights the selected page with `pdfinfo`, renders it with Poppler, checks bounded pixel samples, rechecks source and output immutability, rejects path escapes and unexpected renderer files, and publishes a new receipt without replacement. It limits manifests to 256 KiB and 32 cases, each PDF to 256 MiB, projected and rendered pages to 40 megapixels, samples to 64 regions and one megapixel per region, subprocess diagnostics to 64 KiB, and tool execution to fixed timeouts. The focused gate passes four tests covering the five-label self-test, pixel oracle, no-clobber receipt, empty manifest, path escape, hash mismatch, and oversized-page preflight. Separately, the current generated dependency inventory is 202,858 bytes with SHA-256 `A4EF40C50F66EF1B1B384841F59187830FF27C0253451C2F8A4BE0423B5C0532`; its check verifies 367 package records with zero review items, and all five exact MPL source archives verify. Project licensing remains Joshua's decision; final WebView2 bootstrapper terms/version and bundled PDFium version/configuration provenance remain pending installer work.

- Bounded native viewer-render admission to 32 pending keys before the PDF worker channel. Identical document/page/width requests within the same non-render barrier epoch coalesce without inserting another marker and return an explicit superseded result to the older caller; a distinct request beyond the bound returns an explicit queue-full retry error. Every non-render command advances the epoch before FIFO insertion, so renders never coalesce across edits or other command barriers. Admission ownership is removed when the worker dequeues a marker, closed receivers skip PDFium work, and the existing bounded Close/EndPrint cleanup overtakes remain unchanged. Focused gates pass the isolated >32 live/dropped-receiver backlog regression, the admission state regression, and all 3 existing scheduler ordering/window tests; the native no-run gate passes. The production bound is 32; the shared parallel-test singleton uses a larger compile-time-only limit while isolated product-limit fixtures remain fixed at 32. Installed interaction and sustained arbitrary-document memory behavior remain unverified.

- Added the filesystem storage foundation for future crash recovery. Each app-owned record is named only by the source PDF's lowercase SHA-256 and stores the bounded recovery codec rather than a source path, filename, password, or PDF body. Storage rejects reparse-point roots and records, oversized or corrupt input, stale generations or regressed revisions, and unsafe destinations; process-local writers serialize before publishing through a random same-directory create-new temporary file, file flush and synchronization, then an atomic Windows no-clobber initial move or namespace replacement. Failed temporary artifacts are retained and capped at four rather than deleted. The exact paired codec and storage gate passes 12 tests, including corrupt and stale reads, unsafe roots and destinations, bounded temporary artifacts, and concurrent writers. This is storage only: autosave, service integration, startup restore, and product crash recovery are not implemented. File synchronization and atomic namespace replacement are verified, but parent-directory synchronization and power-loss durability remain unproven.

- Activated strict one-run text replacement in the source interface. The current document, revision, and physical page are inspected for the native flat Helvetica/WinAnsi profile; the dialog accepts only the returned opaque `run-0`, printable ASCII with the exact original byte count, and finite normalized in-page bounds. Native code remains authoritative for exact AFM width. Picker cancellation and errors retain the target and entered replacement for retry, explicit close or unmount releases it, and success opens only the clean new copy while preserving the source, history, undo, and redo. The dialog blocks conflicting document, tab, keyboard, drop, tool, OCR, image, update, notice, and native-picker paths at the handler layer. The combined affected frontend gate passes 18 tests across 5 files, and the production build transforms 1,950 modules in 3.39 seconds. This remains a strict new-copy route: arbitrary fonts, Unicode, reflow, multiple runs, crops, rotations, installed interaction, and release behavior remain unverified or unsupported.

- Added a pure, versioned recovery-record codec for future crash recovery. It binds a source byte count and SHA-256, generation, revision, current page, and the current bounded page/crop/rotation/app-owned annotation state in a canonical length-delimited frame with an integrity digest. Decode rejects wrong sources, stale or nonmonotonic records, malformed or noncanonical payloads, duplicate page sources or annotation IDs, and annotation states outside the editor's current note and highlight rules. It records no source path, password, document body, credential, filesystem location, or log data. The exact focused native gate passes 5 tests, including hostile re-signed payloads. This is a codec only: no filesystem journal, autosave, startup restore, durability, or crash-recovery product behavior is implemented.

- Measured the existing ignored 98-page render-backlog probe under its controlled warm test. The fixed 48,356-byte fixture retained SHA-256 `EA2205E782074A8F4146A63D49F0A84BE6E06B4EF622EACD561F0C3E0A4C01BE`; eight unread live replies and eight preclosed replies retained zero PNG bytes after close, with all eight live requests returning closed errors and all eight preclosed requests skipped. Close/release latency was 1.369 ms for the live backlog and 1.141 ms for the preclosed backlog; sampled peak process private memory was 105,975,808 bytes and working set was 136,572,928 bytes. This short warm single-process measurement excludes the compiler and does not establish an aggregate application RSS cap, sustained throughput, frame rate, or arbitrary-document behavior.

- Added the native strict-text new-copy command lifecycle for the exact one-run profile. `inspect_text_replacement_target` returns one revision-bound opaque selection and the single printable-ASCII Helvetica/WinAnsi run, normalized displayed bounds, and byte limit. `replace_pdf_text_copy` accepts that exact selection/run/document/revision plus a replacement, rechecks the same encoded byte count and exact Helvetica AFM advance, opens the native save picker, and returns a clean copy or `null` on picker cancellation; cancellation and errors retain the target for retry, success settles it, and `cancel_text_replacement` releases it explicitly. The focused command/service lifecycle checks pass 1 and 1 tests, the native target compiles in the no-run gate, and its rendered-output oracle verifies the supported replacement ink. The source frontend is described immediately above; installed interaction, arbitrary fonts, Unicode, reflow, and general PDF text editing remain pending.

- Added a source-only pure native foundation for strict text replacement in a new PDF copy. It accepts only a canonical flat page whose entire content stream is exact `BT`, one direct unique Helvetica/WinAnsi font selection, identity text matrix, one printable-ASCII `Tj` run, and `ET`; the replacement must retain the exact encoded byte count and exact integer AFM advance. Canonical source and output serialization plus a semantic graph oracle prove that only the intended string and regenerable bookkeeping change. Limits are 256 MiB input/output, 20,000 objects, 4,096 pages, and 4,096 content-stream bytes. The focused parser/writer gate passes 4 tests and the module compiles in the no-run native gate. The subsequent command lifecycle adds the rendered-output proof and is described immediately above; general fonts, Unicode, and reflow support remain pending.

- Activated the source frontend for ordered multi-image PDF creation and strict full-page image replacement. Multi-image selection uses only opaque native selection/source IDs, preserves ordering and options, atomically replaces a reservation only after a new picker selection fully validates, retains the prior draft across replacement picker cancellation/errors, retains the current reservation across output cancellation/errors, and releases it on explicit close or unexpected unmount. Strict replacement captures the exact current document/revision/page, fails closed on malformed native target fields, discloses the exact-size flat-raster new-copy limits, retains its target across either picker cancellation and errors, and opens only a successful clean copy. Both dialogs block conflicting document, tab, keyboard, drop, tool, searchable-OCR, properties, update, and notice paths at handler and control layers. The combined affected frontend gate passes 28 tests across 7 files, and the production build transforms 1,948 modules in 2.40 seconds. Native installed interaction and release behavior remain unverified; installer and updater verification remains deferred.

- Added the native source path for strict full-page raster image replacement as a new copy. `inspect_image_replacement_target` captures one revision-bound opaque target, and `replace_pdf_image_copy` accepts a replacement only for an unprotected, unsigned, uncertified flat page with one unique full-page DeviceRGB 8-bit image, zero rotation, no non-equivalent crop, exact direct image drawing, and no masks, forms, aliases, extra channels, or layout ambiguity. The native PNG/JPEG picker applies the existing bounded decoder, EXIF orientation, white compositing, and RGB8 normalization, then requires the replacement to match the exact target pixel dimensions. Cancellation at either picker and processing errors retain the one active target for retry; `cancel_image_replacement` explicitly releases it. Revision and target structure are rechecked after the source picker and immediately before no-clobber publication. A semantic oracle proves every non-target object and draw parameter, while PDFium verifies the selected page's exact replacement RGB and exact unchanged pixels on every unselected page. Success opens a clean new session and preserves the source bytes, history, undo, and redo. The full native gate passes 220 tests; focused suites pass 8, 7, 4, 5, 13, 5, and 1 tests, with 7 verified-engine or manual tests ignored. This does not provide arbitrary image or layout editing, mutate the source session, or add source-session undo; the activated frontend is recorded immediately above, while installed-app interaction and release evidence remain pending.

- Added the native multi-image PNG/JPEG-to-PDF backend while keeping the existing one-image command and behavior unchanged. `choose_image_pdf_sources` returns only an opaque selection ID plus opaque source IDs and display names; paths never cross IPC. For reselection it accepts the current optional `replaceSelectionId`, opens the replacement picker first, and swaps reservations only after every replacement fully validates; picker cancellation or error retains the prior ordered draft and IDs. Native reservations retain verified Windows handles and stable volume/file identities, reject nonregular files, reparses, alias duplicates, and mutation, and use a retry-preserving RAII lifecycle through `create_pdf_from_images` or explicit idempotent `cancel_image_pdf_sources`. Creation accepts an ordered unique subset of 1–32 reserved images with the existing Letter/A4, Auto/Portrait/Landscape, and finite 0–72-point margin policy; applies EXIF orientation, white transparency compositing, and RGB8 normalization; and writes one unique image/content pair per ordered page. Limits are 64 MiB and 32 megapixels per source, 256 MiB aggregate source bytes, 128 MiB decoded bytes per source, 512 MiB aggregate decoded bytes, 128 megapixels aggregate, and 256 MiB output. Before no-clobber publication it requires all-page PDFium reopen, render, and dimension validation, while preserving every source and existing app session. The full native gate passes 217 tests with 7 verified-engine-dependent tests ignored, and the combined integration gate passes 42 tests. App, bridge, and UI activation, installed-app interaction, and release evidence remain pending.

- Activated the source-build searchable OCR frontend against the reviewed native command. The menu route appears only for the exact verified opt-in fixed profile, captures an immutable document ID and revision, generates a fresh UUID for every attempt, and opens only the returned clean copy. Picker cancellation and errors retain the source and dialog; page OCR and searchable OCR cannot overlap; stale revisions fail before native work; and the searchable modal blocks open, close, save, print, edit, tab, keyboard, and dropped-file actions, including direct handler calls. Missing or incoherent extended capability fields fail closed, so the default source build remains disabled. The focused contract, dialog, current-page OCR, and App integration gate passes 20 tests, and the production build transforms 1,943 modules in 2.39 seconds. This evidence does not establish OCR recognition accuracy, real-scan or general Unicode/layout support, installed-app interaction, or release behavior; installer and updater verification remains deferred.

- Added the native source-build command and capability contract for an all-page searchable OCR copy. It is available only with the verified opt-in local English engine and model; the default source build remains capability-unavailable because its gate ran without a verified OCR setup root. The command captures an exact document ID and revision, uses the native save picker, rasterizes every current page at fixed 150 DPI, and overlays invisible printable-ASCII text from bounded engine TSV while leaving the source session and history unchanged. It refuses stale, protected, signed, certified, malformed, parser-disagreed, unsupported, capped, timed-out, canceled, and clobbering requests without publishing a file. Limits are 32 pages, 33,554,432 aggregate pixels, 100,000 words, 8 MiB reconstructed text, 1 MiB TSV per page, 30 seconds per page, and 300 seconds per operation. Three deterministic fixtures pass exact PDFium pixel-render and text-extraction oracles. The full default native gate passes 208 tests with 7 verified-engine-dependent tests ignored, and the combined searchable-OCR integration gate passes 42 tests. The frontend activation is described above; verified-engine runtime execution in this environment, installed-app behavior, and release proof remain pending, and installer and updater verification stays deferred to the final phase.

- Prepared the searchable OCR dialog contract before product activation. The isolated dialog accepts only the exact advertised opt-in profile: verified local English OCR, fixed 150 DPI, at most 32 pages, 33,554,432 aggregate pixels, 100,000 words, 8 MiB of printable-ASCII reconstructed text, and an exact document ID and revision. Every submit attempt, including retries after picker cancellation or processing failure, requires a fresh validated UUID that cannot be reused during the dialog lifetime. It discloses that every page becomes a white-flattened raster with invisible text and that editable text, vectors, forms, annotations, metadata, bookmarks, and attachments are not retained as editable structures. Native picker cancellation keeps the dialog and source unchanged; errors remain visible; successful results use the existing clean-copy shape; one synchronous latch permits at most one cancellation call for the exact active attempt, including transport rejection; and late cancellation replies cannot alter a newer attempt. The focused frontend gate passes 9 tests and the production build transforms 1,941 modules in 2.37 seconds. The subsequent App route and bridge activation is recorded above; installed-app evidence and release proof remain pending.

- Added three deterministic 150-DPI RGB8 synthetic fixtures for future searchable-PDF geometry and extraction acceptance: a 640×180 single line, a 720×300 two-column layout, and a 660×300 mixed-size two-line layout. A dependency-free Node generator uses fixed 5×7 ASCII glyphs and manually encoded stored-DEFLATE PNG data, so committed pixels and bytes do not depend on system fonts or zlib compression choices. The schema-v1 oracle binds exact ordered text, strict page/block/paragraph/line/word TSV hierarchy, positive nonoverlapping pixel boxes, exact 72/150 point numerators, and PNG, raw-RGB, and TSV byte counts and SHA-256 receipts. Its focused three-test gate regenerates every byte, validates PNG signatures, CRCs, RGB8 IHDR, exact inflate length and filter bytes, rejects unsupported glyphs, and checks all geometry and reading order. Visual inspection confirms the intended layouts. These fixtures establish deterministic synthetic ASCII inputs and ground truth only; they do not prove Tesseract recognition, OCR accuracy, real scans, fonts, languages, skew, noise, handwriting, PDF writing, PDFium rendering, or extraction.

- Added source-only pure native foundations for searchable raster PDFs and strict flat-raster image replacement. The searchable path parses bounded printable-ASCII Tesseract TSV with exact hierarchy and geometry, then writes a canonical raster PDF with aligned invisible text. The writer is not connected to PDFium rendering, a verified OCR engine, the service, Tauri commands, a save picker, or the frontend, so it does not yet establish searchable output from a source document, OCR accuracy, or source preservation. The image path accepts only a flat, unencrypted, unsigned, uncertified PDF whose selected page has no CropBox, zero rotation, one direct content stream containing exact `q cm /Name Do Q`, and one unique unshared full-MediaBox DeviceRGB 8-bit image. It replaces that image only with exact-size canonical RGB8 bytes, preserves every non-target graph object and draw parameter, and refuses masks, alternate channels, inline images, Form XObjects, inherited/shared resources, arbitrary operators, crops, and rotations. Its pure limits are 256 MiB input, 256 MiB output, 20,000 objects, 4,096 pages, 4,096 content-stream bytes, and 32 megapixels aggregate. The combined pure-module gate passes 25 tests. Neither path has a product command, session history, picker, UI, installed-app evidence, arbitrary-document support, or product integration.

- Prepared the multi-image PNG/JPEG-to-PDF frontend contract before the native backend. The isolated dialog accepts 1–32 native-selected sources with required opaque stable identities, preserves their initial order, supports deterministic move and remove actions, and submits the existing shared Letter/A4, Auto/Portrait/Landscape, and finite 0–72-point margin policy. It discloses white 8-bit RGB normalization, transparency compositing, and loss of ICC profiles and image metadata. Native selection or save cancellation retains the current list, duplicate native identities and invalid bounds fail before creation, and same-basename controls remain position-qualified. The focused gate passes 12 tests across the new contract/dialog and the unchanged one-image dialog; the production build transforms 1,941 modules in 2.33 seconds. The reviewed native command boundary and limits are recorded above; no App route, bridge invocation, enabled tool, installed-app evidence, or release proof exposes this dialog yet.

- Added the source-build raster-redaction interface for the reviewed native copy flow. **All tools > Redact a PDF** captures the exact document revision and current physical page, accepts up to 256 bounded rectangles in displayed-page points, previews them as black areas, and creates a fixed-150-DPI raster copy through the native save picker. Canceling or an error retains the draft; success opens the clean output as a new session and leaves the source unchanged. While a draft is active, document switching, closing, opening, saving, printing, editing, and conflicting tools are blocked; Escape, Select, or Pan explicitly cancel it. The six focused frontend test files passed 37 tests, including geometry, caps, stale requests, picker cancellation, retained failures, and direct-handler exclusivity, and the production build transformed 1,941 modules in 2.37 seconds. The previously reviewed native command passed two focused service tests and nine writer/oracle tests. Installed-app interaction, arbitrary-document compatibility, and cross-viewer appearance remain unverified; installer and updater verification is deferred to the final release phase.

- Added the native source path for a bounded redacted raster copy. The `redact_document` command captures an exact document ID, revision, physical page, and 1–256 displayed-page rectangles; preflights every current edited page at fixed 150 DPI against a 32-megapixel document aggregate before rendering; refuses stale, encrypted, restricted, signed, certified, malformed, parser-disagreed, and out-of-bounds requests; then white-flattens every current page while blacking only the selected page’s conservative pixel union. Before no-clobber publication it requires the exact clean-raster oracle, PDFium reopen and render of every page, matching page count, and empty text extraction, then opens the result as a clean new session without changing the source. Two focused service tests and nine writer/oracle tests pass, covering selected and unselected pixels, all-page output, source preservation, stale and existing-path failures, protected inputs, and aggregate refusal. The command and native save picker are source-only; frontend integration, native desktop interaction, arbitrary-document compatibility, and final installed-app evidence remain open. The output intentionally loses searchable/editable text, vectors, forms, annotations, metadata, bookmarks, and attachments.

- Extended the native raster-redaction foundation with an exact clean-PDF writer and displayed-page pixel removal. The bounded contract accepts already rendered, white-flattened RGB pages after crop and cardinal rotation; validates every point dimension, pixel dimension, RGB length, rectangle, and the 32-megapixel document aggregate before output work; maps top-left point rectangles conservatively with floor/ceil edges; blackens the complete pixel union; and publishes bytes only through the exact clean-raster oracle. Nine focused native tests pass across the writer and oracle, including serialized RGB extraction, every covered and uncovered pixel, overlap counting, non-square axis orientation, empty and malformed refusals, no extracted PDF text, trailing-data rejection, and aggregate preflight. This remains a native-only primitive: it does not render source PDFs, enforce source permissions or signatures, publish files, expose UI, or prove removal outside the supplied displayed-page raster and rectangles.

- Added a bounded source-native acceptance verifier for sticky notes, area highlights, supported selected-text highlights, and the supported form-filling subset. Six exact native tests cover saved PDF semantics, PDFium-rendered appearance, crop and rotation behavior, save/reopen, immutable print snapshots, source preservation, form value and appearance round trips, and undo. Sticky notes already had explicit redo coverage; area and selected-text highlights now also prove redo by comparing the exact rendered state before and after undo/redo. The verifier binds the committed Cargo/native input tree, records the prepared PDFium byte/hash receipt and the existing notices-file override, caps each subprocess and output stream, and emits only fixed test IDs, booleans, counts, and hashes. Its combined source/workflow contract gate passes 11 tests; the two changed native render tests pass individually. The receipt explicitly leaves installed-app behavior, native desktop interaction, and arbitrary-PDF compatibility false. Cross-viewer form evidence and the final installed comments/forms run remain deferred with installer and update verification until the end of feature development.

- Added a native-only foundation for future raster redaction and sanitization output. The extraction oracle accepts only an exact trusted-writer PDF profile with one full-page 8-bit RGB raster per page, a one-level page tree, unique page streams, no metadata or other document channels, and a 32-megapixel aggregate preflight before image decompression. It rejects text and font resources, annotations, forms, actions, attachments, extra objects, trailing EOF data, noncanonical compressed bytes, malformed payloads, and alternate serialization. Five focused native tests pass, including adversarial trailing-data and pre-decode aggregate-limit regressions. This does not expose a product command or prove that sensitive pixels have been removed; true redaction still requires bounded pixel blanking plus serialized, rendered, and extraction-aware evidence.

- [Step 1 run 37090261339](https://github.com/Joshua-Beel/Smacrobat/actions/runs/37090261339) completed from workflow source `59863ed00ab89f96b510b8b36080c5e9a2eeb777` against immutable `v0.2.12` source `04a8110eb23a9a9bb1abee91d3286976bb64d4ba`. Its sole GitHub artifact `11261842926`, `default-draft-verification-04a8110eb23a9a9bb1abee91d3286976bb64d4ba`, is a 2,693-byte ZIP with SHA-256 `8E970267E238B7FEE12B159DEDD3ABBB82672BCD027AC1D22921861DEBEA1067`; a fresh raw download matched the API digest. It contains one 6,997-byte schema-v1 receipt with SHA-256 `B9B23364F5F19848B3537A22176A53D3AE7072260B8A998535191AB37FF83F8B`. The receipt binds all three draft assets, 26 base resources, 33 required and actual extracted files, the 18,243,880-byte packaged application, and valid timestamped installer, application, and PDFium signatures. It records the generated root uninstaller as observed but excluded, with no byte, hash, or source claim, and records OCR as absent. Source-build executable byte reconstruction, detached updater cryptography, installation, launch, installed behavior, and publication remain unverified. Draft `402057871` remains unpublished.

- The prepared Step 2 and comments/forms workflows now reconstruct the immutable target checkout's 26-resource PDFium map with the same pinned helper that passed Step 1, before either nested independent draft proof or any installer execution. Both retain final tracked/index cleanliness checks. The pre-dispatch source-only preparation contract passed 9 tests across 2 files; the first Step 2 result is recorded below.

- [Step 2 run 37090915126](https://github.com/Joshua-Beel/Smacrobat/actions/runs/37090915126) passed both exact checkouts, source identity, the successful Step 1 artifact download, pinned PDFium reconstruction, and hosted WebDriver preparation. It then failed before independent draft download or installation because the helper importer defined `ConvertTo-ExactUInt64` only in its local function scope; no artifact was retained and no installed behavior was exercised. Both installed verifiers now install only their exact allowlisted trusted AST function bodies into script scope. Executable regressions prove the imported helpers persist after each importer returns and successfully execute from both trusted source scripts; the combined focused gate passes 11 tests across 2 files. A corrected hosted run remains required.

- [Step 1 runs 37056669815](https://github.com/Joshua-Beel/Smacrobat/actions/runs/37056669815), [37058511128](https://github.com/Joshua-Beel/Smacrobat/actions/runs/37058511128), [37059218157](https://github.com/Joshua-Beel/Smacrobat/actions/runs/37059218157), and [37060561976](https://github.com/Joshua-Beel/Smacrobat/actions/runs/37060561976) passed pinned PDFium preparation and all three draft downloads, then failed before extraction on 7-Zip 26.03's blank-size report. The last run's sanitized diagnostic identified unknown entry 34; exact offline reproduction with the verified official 26.03 extractor proved it is the unique final root `uninstall.exe`, not `resources/welcome.pdf`. The verifier now accepts only that exact optional generated root entry as unique and final, excludes it with a constant nonrecursive extraction rule, and makes no byte, hash, or source claim for it. Nested, prefixed, duplicate, nonfinal, or other blank entries still fail. Local 26.03 no-install extraction omitted the uninstaller and produced the 32 bulk files; separately streaming the 6,965-byte tagged welcome resource produced the expected 33-file verified payload. Those runs produced no Step 1 receipt; the later successful run is recorded above.

- Corrected the native print-dialog test fixture so a snapshot that crosses its caller deadline retains the existing strict replacement-surface failure, while a genuine runtime-identity provider error still propagates. No production identity, ancestry, or actuation predicate changed. The three focused cases and all 26 print-dialog workflow tests pass.

- Signed [release run 37044328791](https://github.com/Joshua-Beel/Smacrobat/actions/runs/37044328791) completed in 28 minutes 29 seconds from immutable `v0.2.12` source `04a8110eb23a9a9bb1abee91d3286976bb64d4ba`. Version, signing inputs, locked dependency notices, 68 frontend/workflow files, 205 native tests with 7 ignored retained-resource checks, 7 OCR build tests, the 1,938-module production build, three publisher signatures, exact asset inventory, upload, and draft creation passed. Draft `402057871` remains unpublished and contains only `PDF.Workstation_0.2.12_x64-setup.exe` (7,411,200 bytes; SHA-256 `7F5430F8DC13C0BD36920CB767916EF70D2202833516482AD1406AB7B9B31848`), its 428-byte detached signature (SHA-256 `13CF8CD9BEBD613E1192233F82AFE7A5016A9ED4671CB2DF9A32795C097C6832`), and 7,492-byte `latest.json` (SHA-256 `2AD8168701BBC0D06E766F0C79DF5B4449B0908F737E437A23F7FA27A8219E6C`). Fresh downloads matched those receipts; the installer has a valid Joshua Beel Authenticode signature and Microsoft timestamp, and the manifest binds version 0.2.12, the canonical installer URL, and the exact detached-signature text. Detached updater cryptography, extracted application/PDFium signatures, installation, launch, tools, and update behavior remain unverified.

- Added a manual two-step verifier for that default signed draft. Step 1 independently downloads the exact three assets, binds them to the immutable tag and six-source version, verifies hashes, manifest URL and detached-signature text binding, publisher identity, extracted inventory, default no-OCR scope, and a bounded sanitized receipt without installing; it does not verify detached updater cryptography. Step 2 requires that exact receipt, downloads the installer independently again, installs it, and exercises controlled image creation/export plus Combine, Insert, Replace, Split, Crop, and Reset crop through the installed app before bounded cleanup. Their combined 18-test contract gate passed before dispatch. [Step 1 run 37047822231](https://github.com/Joshua-Beel/Smacrobat/actions/runs/37047822231) and the permission-scoped [run 37051435583](https://github.com/Joshua-Beel/Smacrobat/actions/runs/37051435583) both failed before asset download on the first unpublished-draft metadata GET. The verifier did not expose either HTTP status, so the earlier 404 diagnosis was unsupported; neither run produced a receipt or installed-app evidence. The second run proves the job received `contents: write`, and code inspection found that the shared .NET request constructor omitted GitHub's mandatory User-Agent header. The constructor now supplies the fixed nonsecret `Smacrobat-DraftVerifier` value for every metadata and asset GET, while failures expose only a fixed stage and numeric HTTP status. Runtime regressions require the exact GET method and User-Agent and cover 401, 403, 404, 429, 500, and 503 without response bodies, URLs, headers, or tokens. [Run 37052731354](https://github.com/Joshua-Beel/Smacrobat/actions/runs/37052731354) proved that metadata access then succeeded, but failed before download because GitHub gives unpublished draft assets one shared `untagged-` plus 20-lowercase-hex browser path segment instead of the tag. The metadata guard now accepts only the exact tag or that bounded generated form, requires the same segment for all three assets, and retains exact release, source, asset ID, name, size, digest, host, repository, escaped filename, and no-query checks. Downloads still use pinned API asset IDs, and `latest.json` still requires the canonical tagged updater URL. [Run 37053616542](https://github.com/Joshua-Beel/Smacrobat/actions/runs/37053616542) then downloaded all three assets and failed before extraction because the immutable source checkout omits generated PDFium resources required by its 26-file map. Step 1 now reconstructs only those resources from upstream asset ID `441936973`, whose published 3,733,154-byte SHA-256 receipt and a fresh download both match `73CC0DE638AC2095E7445BF56A38200A5B7C7CA0E9F4BA144598F2457377AC08`; bounded metadata, archive-type, inventory, size, traversal, and reparse checks precede selected extraction. A fresh immutable-source reconstruction produces exactly 26 map entries, but hosted installer extraction remains unverified until the next run. The tagged setup script itself is not claimed to verify this receipt. Job-scoped draft visibility, global read-only defaults, disabled checkout credential persistence, and token clearing before installer or app execution remain unchanged.

- Hardened the installed updater and added the remaining Item 1 verification paths. Update downloads and installer handoff now use separate signed-updater phases backed by an exact, bounded native recovery marker that is atomically written, flushed, replaced, and read back before either phase proceeds. Failed downloads release their native updater resource before a fresh retry; a reopened installer-handoff marker blocks a second concurrent installer and directs the user to wait or use the latest signed installer manually. A new read-only hosted workflow is ready to verify the real public 0.2.0 in-app path against a future newer stable release, including exact public release receipts, restart/relaunch, sentinels, cleanup, and controlled missing-executable repair. [Hosted run 36924870408](https://github.com/Joshua-Beel/Smacrobat/actions/runs/36924870408) failed closed at the Explorer Digital Signatures proof because the Windows accessibility tree exposed both the embedded signer row and its child text, two Details buttons, and an owned modal dialog omitted from the root automation tree. [Hosted rerun 36937479844](https://github.com/Joshua-Beel/Smacrobat/actions/runs/36937479844) reached the corrected proof but exhausted its original 25-second helper limit. [Hosted rerun 36938642907](https://github.com/Joshua-Beel/Smacrobat/actions/runs/36938642907) then reached the first installer publisher proof and failed safely after 65.877 seconds because the synchronous UI Automation `InvokePattern` call could remain blocked on the modal details dialog. In [hosted rerun 36945264215 (#25)](https://github.com/Joshua-Beel/Smacrobat/actions/runs/36945264215), every setup, exact-source, WebDriver, baseline, and signed-artifact check passed before the first installer publisher proof failed after 66.4 seconds. Its Node 20 annotation was a warning only; the child had replaced every internal error with one generic token, so that run could not identify the stalled stage. The helper now reacquires the exact enabled Details button beneath the exact revalidated Properties window handle and root, then acquires and invokes `InvokePattern` on the same dedicated MTA worker. It exposes only versioned, strictly allowlisted fixed stage/category tokens; preserves a primary failure through cleanup; and rejects any unrecognized child output. The existing deadlines, direct `GW_OWNER` requirement, and exact window, process, file, signer, and status identity checks are unchanged. The focused publisher regression passes all 11 tests, and three fresh-process local real-dialog proofs pass against the retained signed installer. [Controlled hosted run 36950684317](https://github.com/Joshua-Beel/Smacrobat/actions/runs/36950684317) then returned the fixed `wait-properties/unexpected` result, proving that the noninteractive GitHub-hosted runner never exposed the Explorer Properties window. The hosted verifier now requires explicit `HostedNonInteractive` mode and does not attempt or claim Explorer shell UI verification. It still verifies the exact byte receipts, expected publisher, and trusted timestamp twice for both the installer and installed application, and schema v6 records false shell-UI booleans plus the fixed noninteractive reason. The local interactive helper remains available. The Node 20 warning is addressed with verified official Node 24 action pins for checkout v7.0.1 and download-artifact v7.0.0. No additional workflow run will be dispatched without notifying Joshua; one hosted rerun remains pending. The complete source gate passes 397 frontend/workflow tests, 205 native tests with 7 ignored retained-resource checks, 7 OCR build tests, and the production frontend build. The in-app workflow still requires a newly built public stable target newer than 0.2.0 before shipped behavior can be claimed.

- Wired a guarded source-only **artifact-only Azure OCR installer** path for future manual validation: `npm run installer -- -AzureSigning -ArtifactOnly -OcrSetupRoot <verified unsigned setup> -OutputRoot <fresh target subdirectory> -ExpectedPublisher "<verified publisher>" -SourceRevision <exact lowercase git HEAD>`. This exact combination requires a clean tracked working tree and index, stages resources before the build, signs the staged OCR engine before its identity is captured, and lets Tauri skip only that verified staged engine while it signs other candidates. It clears updater-key variables, creates no updater artifacts or release manifest, keeps the regular Azure OCR route refused, and does not alter the tag-triggered release workflow. A regression fixed relative receipt paths when a fresh `-OutputRoot` ended in `\`; focused scripts passed 3 files and 9 tests in 7.00 seconds, and the full frontend/scripts gate passed 48 files and 222 tests in 8.37 seconds. Integration validation remains mock-only; no Azure request, credential use, signed package, extraction, installation, update, tag, workflow dispatch, draft, or release has occurred.

- Extended source-only **Export page as image** from PNG to a PNG/JPEG choice, defaulting to PNG. The UI sends lowercase `png` or `jpeg` and accepts a native receipt only when the captured document ID, revision, physical page, DPI, dimensions, and format all match. Both outputs are white-flattened 8-bit RGB images of the current crop, rotation, supported annotations, and supported form appearances, with no editable text, OCR, vectors, or extra pages. JPEG is lossy at fixed quality 90, writes exact 72/150/300-DPI JFIF density, suggests `.jpg`, accepts `.jpg` or `.jpeg`, and rejects format/extension mismatches; PNG retains rounded `pHYs` density. Both formats share the 16,384-pixel edge, 32,000,000-pixel, 128 MiB copied-BGRA-buffer, and 256 MiB encoded-output guards. Six focused native tests passed; the full native gate passed 202 tests with 0 failures and 7 ignored in 466.58 seconds, and `ocr_build` build-helper integration tests passed 7 with 0 failures in 0.07 seconds. The frontend gate passed 217 tests across 47 files in 13.72 seconds; production transformed 1,937 modules in 4.37 seconds. The final standalone debug/no-bundle build transformed 1,937 frontend modules in 3.78 seconds and native code in 26.96 seconds; its 30,668,800-byte executable SHA-256 is `D5538E1644249D40D18007473DD54B90F5217C3023AE497E88E8552A5F49EA46`. Retained immutable evidence passed local and independent readback of 47 hashes and embedded frozen assets; no build log file was retained. The updated mocked browser harness has not been rerun. This is source and artifact evidence, not Windows picker, real IPC, native desktop, or installed-app verification.

- Hardened source-only **embedded text** access and bookmark reads. Page text returns only visible cropped text and rejects output above 1,048,576 raw UTF-8 bytes without truncating; hidden out-of-crop text does not count. Bookmark titles share that returned-text bound across at most 1,000 items, and a closed bookmark reply is skipped. Text reading, Find, and on-page selection allow unencrypted PDFs, owner-password access, or the ordinary Copy permission; accessibility-only or unverifiable permissions are refused. A denied selection shows its extraction error without directing people to unavailable Page text. OCR still refuses encrypted PDFs. The default native gate passed 201 tests with 0 failures and 7 ignored in 429.62 seconds; separate `ocr_build` build-helper integration tests passed 7 with 0 failures in 0.07 seconds. The final opt-in standalone build transformed 1,937 frontend modules in 3.79 seconds and native code in 10.80 seconds; its 30,609,408-byte executable SHA-256 is `90B63B7F92614ADD6506B338406C46CD14AF36FFFB64A5EAA5E32769206BD104`. This is source and artifact evidence, not native desktop or installed-app interaction evidence.

- Hardened the source-only **current-page OCR** dialog against React development-mode effect replay and teardown failures. The full frontend gate passed 213 tests across 47 files in 5.35 seconds. The final retained opt-in standalone build transformed 1,937 modules in 2.19 seconds and native code in 7.22 seconds; its 30,602,240-byte executable SHA-256 is `7175555A89C781D538015B3307A625DED7C41DDB66B347409E4E8F7B1F63E889`. - Added source-only **current-page OCR** behind a verified opt-in build resource. The default source build reports OCR unavailable, and the initial source implementation changed neither installer nor release. When enabled, **Scan & OCR** recognizes one captured physical page in English in a copyable dialog; it keeps the source document, history, tabs, and recent files unchanged and never creates searchable PDF text or cloud content. A cancel request discards late text and keeps the workspace busy until the native work drains. - Added an internal native **OCR snapshot/coordinator foundation** only. It remains unreachable: there is no OCR command, bridge, interface, bundled resource, installer wiring, or release behavior. The coordinator captures one current physical page at fixed 150 DPI with supported edits, conservatively refuses encrypted, restricted, signed, or certified PDFs, and keeps shared admission until a cancelled worker drains. It cannot interrupt PDFium mid-call, and any future consumer must validate a later revision-tagged result. Retained checks captured page 0 of document 2, revision 5 as a 1,618×1,250 P6 snapshot (6,067,517 bytes, SHA-256 `8084BE11A715ADC2937EEF8E117DA35F69240E00D0D3158035E68F5DCF493292`) and produced the exact known text with 39,063,552 bytes peak child commitment. See [OCR feasibility](ocr-feasibility.md).

- Added an internal native **OCR runner foundation** only. It has no command, bridge, interface, bundled resource, installer wiring, or release behavior, so OCR remains disabled and unshipped. One global admission gate refuses a second job immediately; each child is created suspended, assigned to its kill-on-close job, cancellation-checked, then resumed. - Added an opt-in **OCR source-build recipe** for development research. `pwsh -NoProfile -File scripts/setup-ocr.ps1` builds a pinned local English fast engine only under `target`; it does not enable OCR in the app or add an app, installer, or release resource. A verified run completed in about 173.70 seconds, matched its owned P6 smoke text with empty standard error, and rejected a 16,777,217-byte input before process start. Twelve focused offline controls cover the recipe guards and bounded-process behavior. It is not a bit-reproducibility, general-accuracy, or shipping claim. See [OCR feasibility](ocr-feasibility.md).

- Recorded an **OCR feasibility study**; OCR remains disabled and no engine or language model is bundled or shipped. Microsoft documents `Windows.Media.Ocr` as requiring package identity, which the current NSIS distribution does not provide. An isolated static Tesseract 5.5.3/Leptonica 1.87.0 research build compared pinned English fast and best 4.1.0 models on bounded owned fixtures. Fast is only a later prototype candidate, not a product decision: this small fixture set does not establish real-scan accuracy, end-to-end latency, installed-app behavior, or readiness to ship. See [OCR feasibility](ocr-feasibility.md).

- Added source-only **Reset crop** in Organize Pages. It acts directly on the nonempty selected physical-page set and removes only crop edits made in the open session. A mixed selection changes cropped members in one undoable edit; an all-uncropped selection is a neutral unchanged-revision result that leaves history and redo intact. It makes content hidden only by an open-session crop visible again, but cannot reveal content outside original or inherited source crop/page boxes, including a crop stored in a reopened PDF. Protected, stale, invalid, or source-changed requests are rejected atomically. The focused UI checks and full frontend suite passed 199 tests across 43 files; the production frontend transformed 1,936 modules in 2.38 seconds. A local mocked-browser harness submitted `{id:42,revision:7,pages:[0,3]}`, retained the two selected physical pages through both a neutral result and a changed result, and reported no browser errors. The focused native three-test corpus gate included a separate inherited-box unit regression. The independent artifact review verified retained direct CropBox behavior across six pages: original page 1 boxes and rotation from 90° to 180°, the output note body, retained page 2 crop, and unchanged page 3–6 rasters. The full native gate passed 167 tests with 0 failures and 1 ignored test in 332.24 seconds. The standalone debug/no-bundle build exited 0 with 1,936 frontend modules in 2.28 seconds and native code in 13.84 seconds; its 30,197,760-byte executable SHA-256 is `FA558EA1C897EF0D7EF66765B7A87A25B0581F1611F8D98367EAA4DE05E79FAD`, with exact current `index-CE5_RcSi.js`, CSS, and transformed HTML Brotli bytes verified embedded. This browser check does not prove native desktop interaction or installed-app behavior.

- Added source-only **Fit page** reading mode. It uses the current target’s physical page dimensions, including the current crop and rotation, inside the existing 144-pixel width and 48-pixel height allowances. Ctrl+0, the reading controls, and the Zoom menu select it; Ctrl+1, Ctrl+2, and manual zoom retain their existing meanings. A manual scroll reports the reading page without continuously changing the fit reference; a page jump, tab restoration, or document revision supplies a new target. Older saved Boolean fit settings map to width or none. The 10% minimum scale remains, so a very small viewport can prevent a full-page fit. The frontend gate passed 196 tests across 43 files; the production build transformed 1,936 modules in 2.69 seconds. The final standalone debug/no-bundle gate exited 0 with 1,936 frontend modules in 2.25 seconds and native compilation in 18.38 seconds. Its 30,177,792-byte executable SHA-256 is `1C9C4C87A4239BF231CBDD2527D99CF06B6ADD4DDDF7FC4CD3F347349FF71F5A`; exact current `index-DLU2IhcL.js`, CSS, and transformed HTML Brotli bytes were verified embedded. A local mixed-size, 1,500-page browser harness captured page 750, explicitly moved to 751, retained that target through resize, and manually scrolled to 752 without changing it; it had no browser errors or native calls. This is not native rendering or installed-app evidence.
